# Progress

## Keep desktop navigation selection accessible and unambiguous (2026-11-14)

The app previously marked both “Active run” and “Saved runs” selected whenever the Runs screen was open, and the Saved runs link omitted `aria-current`. Navigation selection now follows the actual section anchor, so exactly one visible section is current; the selected link exposes `aria-current="page"`. Opening or creating a run selects Active run, while the Saved runs link selects its own target. Keyboard navigation now moves programmatic focus to the updated page heading after scrolling to the section, so assistive technology announces the destination. The model-free onboarding browser flow checks single selection, screen-reader state, keyboard activation, and heading focus across section changes. `npm run typecheck`, `npm run build:desktop`, and `npx playwright test tests/browser/onboarding.spec.ts --reporter=line` pass (1). The onboarding test uses a mocked API/diagnostic and does not run inference. Hosted Windows source run [43636628718](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43636628718) passed the complete source workflow, including the updated onboarding browser test; its installer job was skipped because packaging inputs were unchanged. A full-screen keyboard, assistive-technology, and contrast audit remains open.


## Verify scheduler behavior after clock corrections and restart (2026-11-14)

Added a deterministic schedule regression that advances past a daily occurrence, moves the supplied clock backward, reopens the SQLite store, and advances to the next occurrence. The original occurrence is never queued twice, the backward time does not consume the next occurrence, and the next day runs once. `uv run pytest tests/unit/test_schedules.py -q` passes all 16 tests; Ruff, format check, and `git diff --check` pass. Hosted Windows source run [43634885386](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43634885386) passed the full source workflow, including pytest and scheduler process-restart recovery; its installer job was skipped because no packaging inputs changed. This exercises the scheduler with clock values but does not change the host clock, so real operating-system clock steps and timezone-database updates remain unverified. No model inference or message delivery was used.

## Bound schedule catch-up after long downtime (2026-11-14)

Daily and weekly schedule recovery now calculates the latest occurrence directly from the saved start and missed-count, rather than iterating over every missed date. A 125-year fixture verifies both recurrence types without calling the incremental step function. The focused schedule suite passes (15); source run [43632412413](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43632412413) and installer run [43632435692](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43632435692) passed on `7e3a9440`. The tested unsigned installer is 78,156,658 bytes, SHA-256 `f235d97ed50c7685cc8502db5e155fb45d2f550a74e687f93321cf0c515e2638`, retained in `.acceptance/hosted-installer-43632435692/`. No model inference or live delivery was used. This bounds catch-up work; timezone database changes and real external clock transitions remain separate acceptance gaps.

## Allow cold start for frozen CLI diagnostics (2026-11-14)

The installer retry passed the task-worker scope probe after its timeout adjustment, then exposed a separate 15-second cold-start limit in the frozen CLI checks. Both frozen ACP/compression CLI probe limits are now 30 seconds. The current hosted source and installer runs passed, including the ACP version and trajectory-compression help checks; these are diagnostic-only bounds and do not change user execution deadlines ([source 43630380192](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43630380192), [installer 43630393305](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43630393305)).

## Allow cold start for frozen task workers (2026-11-14)

The first hosted installer run for the v1 npm parser commit stopped in the frozen filesystem-worker diagnostic: the one-file engine did not answer within its 15-second per-process limit. The same check passed locally against the just-built frozen engine in 11.3 seconds total. Raised only the diagnostic's bounded process allowance to 30 seconds, consistent with the neighboring frozen snapshot check. Source and installer CI passed on retry, including the task lease/read/write/expiry checks against the frozen worker ([source 43630380192](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43630380192), [installer 43630393305](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43630393305)); production execution deadlines are unchanged.

## Accept legacy npm lockfile v1 for imported procedure packages (2026-11-14)

The isolated importer now reads npm v1's nested dependency tree, derives root ranges from the exact linked `package.json`, and normalizes package paths for the same graph, registry URL, and integrity checks used by v2/v3. The trusted WSL installer independently flattens and checks the original v1 tree against the approved package proposal before invoking offline `npm ci`. The local frozen Windows engine was rebuilt from this source; npm parser, manager, plugin, and AppContainer suites pass (93 passed, 1 skipped across the focused suite and isolated Windows ACL retry), including both source and packaged worker checks. The full authenticated owner flow passed twice against live `is-odd@3.0.1` and transitive `is-number@6.0.0`; the final run verifies Windows AppContainer acquisition, explicit review, offline Ubuntu-24.04 install, exact procedure-bound execution, and digest-scoped cleanup ([final report](../.acceptance/npm-lock-v1-owner-flow-20261114-final/report.json)). Hosted verification passed: source run [43630380192](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43630380192) and full Windows installer run [43630393305](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43630393305) passed for the code revision; tested installer and reports are retained at `.acceptance/hosted-installer-43630393305/`. npm v1 records with missing registry integrity and linked/bundled entries remain rejected. This adds legacy lockfile coverage, not broad npm compatibility or model-mediated imported-procedure execution.

## Accept npm lockfile v2 for imported procedure packages (2026-11-14)

The isolated imported-package lock parser and the trusted WSL installer now accept npm `package-lock.json` v2 and v3 when the canonical `packages` map is present. This admits the standard format produced by npm 7/8 while continuing to reject v1 and unknown formats, linked/bundled entries, and non-registry tarballs. npm documents v2 as the npm 7/8 format and says the `packages` map is authoritative when present ([npm package-lock documentation](https://docs.npmjs.com/files/package-lock.json/)). The first full v2 owner-flow attempt exposed an additional v3-only check in the trusted installer; that check now accepts only the same explicit v2/v3 allowlist as the parser. The complete authenticated flow then passed with real `is-odd@3.0.1` and `is-number@6.0.0` tarballs: AppContainer acquisition, digest review, offline Ubuntu-24.04 install, bound procedure execution, and exact cleanup ([report](../.acceptance/npm-lock-v2-owner-flow-20261114-retry1/report.json)). Unit coverage passes (45 tests), Ruff/format and strict Pyright pass. Hosted source run [43623410861](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43623410861) passed; hosted installer run [43623472987](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43623472987) passed installed AppContainer readiness/command, native MCP/OAuth, recovery, upgrade, standard-user installation, startup, uninstall, and explicit data-removal acceptance. The exact tested unsigned 0.1.1 installer is 78,152,255 bytes, SHA-256 `45588dec9f3c1c498ad471ee9e1ca701479da1ccc8e42521b45056f3b8c82779`; its manifest records the desktop and bundled-engine hashes (`../../.acceptance/hosted-installer-43623472987/xaa-windows-installer-unsigned/build/installer/manifest.json`). Hosted reports and package are preserved under [installer evidence](../.acceptance/hosted-installer-43623472987/). The earlier installed-readiness failure in [43622079705](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43622079705) did not recur, but its transient root cause is unproven. Broader npm package-source, script, and platform compatibility remain incomplete; live model-mediated procedure trials remain deferred.

## Default-isolate new MCP stdio servers on Windows (2026-11-14)

New stdio integrations created from the desktop, authenticated API, or imported Agent Plugin now start with Windows AppContainer network isolation enabled. Owners can explicitly clear the desktop option for a reviewed server that needs network access. Existing database rows keep their stored setting, and HTTP integrations remain unchanged. Browser acceptance verifies the UI default is checked; API tests verify default-on, explicit stdio opt-out, and unchanged HTTP behavior. Windows MCP integration tests create, connect, invoke, and disconnect isolated API-created and package-imported fixture servers. The focused suite passes (43), Ruff/format/Pyright and desktop type/contract checks pass. A fresh React/Tauri development build in `../build/desktop-program-mcp-isolation-20261114/` passes native startup/lifecycle acceptance without inference ([report](../.acceptance/native-desktop-mcp-isolation-20261114/report.json)); desktop SHA-256 `80de12d9e68b89ae3f121e2efdeb6b98c24b04b582d726ff21a3b25bb21467f1`, engine SHA-256 `cb6d604aa9615c019d2d93fb429f26b05663123b43613d04cd97b762b35cf34a`. A local unsigned Windows setup program was built at `../build/installer/Xyst AI Agent_0.1.1_x64-setup.exe` (77,849,675 bytes, SHA-256 `f0954e7efae8949aadac224c6e63d2ae6bfac97cad9d57d0b8834f265be82684`); its manifest matches the package and bundled engine. Source CI [43617610844](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43617610844) passed. Installer CI [43617618493](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43617618493) exposed two acceptance-fixture issues: its Python server received the repository as a writable working directory, and the TypeScript notification fixture needed loopback for its test control endpoint. The smoke now uses a private empty Python working directory, explicitly opts the TypeScript fixture out of isolation for its loopback-only control server, and reports connect responses with sanitized server errors. The updated native release-stage smoke passes all MCP/OAuth flows and graceful shutdown without model inference ([report](../.acceptance/5a83878c-7a5a-4a22-8930-0433674fa060/report.json)); hosted installer retry is pending.

## Add NVIDIA GPU telemetry to the desktop monitor (2026-11-14)

The authenticated local resource panel now queries `nvidia-smi` from the Windows system directory with fixed arguments, a one-second timeout, bounded output, and no shell. It shows per-GPU utilization and VRAM totals/free when NVIDIA telemetry is available; other hardware remains explicitly unavailable instead of being treated as having no GPU. On this host the probe returned the NVIDIA GeForce RTX 5080 and live VRAM/utilization values ([hardware evidence](../.acceptance/gpu-telemetry-20261114/report.json)). Unit/API/queue tests pass (13), desktop type and generated-contract checks pass, and the focused monitor browser test passes. The fresh frozen engine and Tauri bundle passed native launch/lifecycle acceptance without runs or model inference ([native report](../.acceptance/native-desktop-gpu-monitor-20261114/report.json)). Hosted source run [43615193581](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43615193581) passed with 1,361 tests and 19 skips. Hosted installer run [43615440543](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43615440543) passed frozen-engine and full install/upgrade/boot/standard-user/uninstall acceptance. Its unsigned installer is 78,152,978 bytes, SHA-256 `952e80078fc1f4196895a5fc64b3e20233e0bc697027b3ba4567fc9858f788d2`; [download](../.acceptance/hosted-installer-43615440543/build/installer/Xyst%20AI%20Agent_0.1.1_x64-setup.exe), manifest and reports are under [hosted acceptance](../.acceptance/hosted-installer-43615440543/). The development executables are in `../build/desktop-program-gpu-monitor-20261114/`: desktop SHA-256 `ba858f872fa39fa76990fd3c95ee2e31370ec083f4d5dc3a63373292388b030a`; engine SHA-256 `e1dd80b336e07c29cfa365a2dff25ea73eb7b032fd661d50525bc87af34745ee`. Queue capacity remains bounded and serial; telemetry does not estimate a model's VRAM demand or justify increasing concurrency. Live model tests remain deferred to final acceptance.

## Fix the installed AppContainer shim temp permissions (2026-11-14)

The prior hosted installed coordinator failure reported `PermissionError` under the shim process's private `_MEI.../jsonschema_specifications` directory. The transport set that unique per-execution folder as the AppContainer shim's `TEMP` but had omitted it from the container's writable roots. Added the exact `shim_temporary` path to the AppContainer write grants; it is not a broader application-data or workspace grant. The targeted Windows transport integration suite passes (8 passed), Ruff/format/Pyright pass, and the rebuilt frozen installer passed three fresh local installed AppContainer readiness → coordinator command → cleanup/uninstall cycles. All three reports verify AppContainer identity, network denial, private runtime temp, nested snapshot access, owner review, unchanged source/outside files, and binary removal: [cycle A](../.acceptance/installed-appcontainer-shimtemp-20261114-a1/report.json), [cycle B](../.acceptance/installed-appcontainer-shimtemp-20261114-b/report.json), and [cycle C](../.acceptance/installed-appcontainer-shimtemp-20261114-c/report.json). Hosted source run [43608816608](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43608816608) passed its full Python suite and recovery checks on retry; the first attempt timed out on an external Postman Echo → Wikipedia redirect before those checks began. Hosted installer run [43610179227](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43610179227) passed installed AppContainer execution and full install/upgrade/recovery/uninstall acceptance. The installer is 78,142,809 bytes with SHA-256 `5992465a15292dd43e7af7f051356e9e08b7571a41dae7a63ecee20accfbe477`; manifest and reports are preserved under [hosted evidence](../.acceptance/hosted-installer-43610179227/). No live model inference was used. This fix resolves the observed missing write grant; broader AppContainer reliability and full release readiness remain open.

## Build and hosted-verify the current unsigned installer (2026-11-14)

Built the current 0.1.1 installer locally with `npm run build:installer`; its manifest matches the setup executable at 77,837,919 bytes and SHA-256 `1939454d26d34986a98a6492501b024041ad37638731b0946e58dcd4ec7dd8e3`. The first hosted attempt built successfully and passed staged/installed readiness, but the installed coordinator command's fresh AppContainer probe hit an intermittent `PermissionError` under a PyInstaller temp directory; downstream installer checks were skipped. Rerunning the failed job on a fresh hosted runner passed installed coordinator execution (all reported isolation checks, owner review, clean workspace, graceful shutdown and credential cleanup), native and specialist recovery, frozen workers, 0.1.0 upgrade, startup/uninstall, standard-user install, and explicit data removal. The successful package and report are preserved under [retry evidence](../.acceptance/hosted-installer-43605529851-retry/); the first attempt remains under [failure diagnostics](../.acceptance/hosted-installer-43605529851/). The retry proves the package can pass these checks but does not resolve the intermittent permission failure. Manual dispatch skipped the source job; source run [43603741402](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43603741402) passed. The successful hosted package is 78,143,138 bytes, SHA-256 `2c318088f39f299e4ca5165733a06b2d4e93f4904f8798738dbd7215c6edc0c1`; it remains unsigned and unpublished.

## Verify the imported npm owner flow through the API (2026-11-14)

Added `scripts/npm_live_registry_api_acceptance.py` and completed the full authenticated lifecycle against real `is-odd@3.0.1` and transitive `is-number@6.0.0` registry tarballs. The acceptance imported linked procedure resources, inspected and saved the exact lock, confirmed approval is refused before acquisition and installation is refused before approval, acquired both archives through the API's Windows AppContainer worker, explicitly approved the lock, installed it offline through the API into Ubuntu-24.04 WSL, ran the linked Node resource with the exact procedure/environment binding, and removed the digest through the API. It also verified unauthenticated import rejection, sandbox cleanup, no workspace `node_modules`, and no network during install or execution. Evidence: [report](../.acceptance/npm-live-registry-api-20261114/success/report.json). The existing rendered npm panel Playwright flow passed locally (1 test) with mocked API responses. Ruff, strict Pyright, TypeScript, generated API contracts, relevant unit/integration tests and diff checks pass locally. Hosted source run [43599066483](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43599066483) passed the full suite and recovery steps; installer packaging was skipped because the change did not touch packaging inputs. The browser fixture and live API acceptance are separate checks, not a live browser-to-API acceptance. No model inference was used; model-mediated procedure execution remains deferred.

## Acquire and run a real npm package through a selected procedure (2026-11-14)

Added a repeatable acceptance for the supported public-registry path using pinned `is-number@7.0.0`. X.A.A.'s Windows AppContainer acquisition worker fetched the exact integrity-bound tarball through its broker; the verified archive was persisted, installed offline in Ubuntu-24.04 WSL, imported from a procedure-bound command, and removed by exact digest. The command verified expected module results, the selected read-only procedure mount, and absence of workspace `node_modules`. The 3,730-byte tarball and runtime report are preserved at [report](../.acceptance/npm-live-registry-20261114c/report.json). Installation and execution used no network; only the acquisition worker contacted npm. Ruff, strict Pyright, formatting and diff checks pass locally. This is one small package on npm 9.2.0 and does not prove broad package compatibility or desktop approval-flow acceptance. Model-mediated procedure testing remains deferred.

Extended the live registry acceptance to a real transitive graph: `is-odd@3.0.1` depends on `is-number@6.0.0`. Both tarballs were acquired and independently integrity-checked by the Windows AppContainer worker, stored, installed offline, imported through the selected procedure sandbox, and removed by exact digest. The command checked odd/even results, procedure-only module scope, and no project-root `node_modules`: [two-package report](../.acceptance/npm-live-registry-transitive-20261114/report.json). This is one graph on npm 9.2.0 and Ubuntu 24.04, not broad ecosystem compatibility.

## Verify CPU-filtered npm optional dependencies in WSL (2026-11-14)

Extended the offline Ubuntu-24.04 dependency graph with a CPU-specific optional package whose declared `arm64` architecture does not match the WSL Node runtime (`x64`). npm 9.2 skipped it, as it did the existing `win32` optional package, while compatible scoped, transitive, peer, and root-optional packages remained usable by the procedure-bound command. The fixture chooses the opposite architecture from `node process.arch`, records the runtime architecture, and rechecks recovery, rollback, leases, cross-procedure isolation, exact cleanup, and unchanged system Node/npm: [report](../.acceptance/npm-platform-cpu-optional-20261114/report.json). Ruff, formatting, strict Pyright, and diff checks pass. Hosted Windows source run [43593080701](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43593080701) passed the full Python suite, static checks, desktop checks, and recovery scenarios; it does not run WSL. This covers Linux x64 with npm 9.2 on Ubuntu 24.04; broad npm/platform compatibility remains open. No public registry request or inference was used.

## Verify platform-filtered npm optional dependencies in WSL (2026-11-14)

Added a Windows-only optional package to the exact offline npm graph and ran it on Ubuntu 24.04 WSL. The run also rechecked scoped/transitive loading, required and optional peers, root optional loading, installer recovery, rollback, cleanup, isolation, and unchanged system Node/npm: [report](../.acceptance/npm-platform-optional-20261114/report.json). Ruff, formatting, strict Pyright, and diff checks pass. Hosted Windows source run [43591735049](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43591735049) passed the full Python suite, static checks, desktop checks, and recovery scenarios; it does not run WSL. This verifies one platform-filter case on one WSL/npm version and does not establish broad npm or platform compatibility. No public registry request or inference was used.

## Verify optional and peer npm dependencies in offline procedure runs (2026-11-14)

Expanded the live Ubuntu-24.04 WSL install fixture with a required peer dependency, a root optional dependency, and an absent peer marked optional. The exact tarballs install offline; a procedure-bound command loads the required peer and root optional package, while confirming that the absent optional peer does not prevent execution. The report also rechecks recovery, lifecycle suppression, cross-procedure isolation, rollback, active-use leases, exact cleanup, workspace isolation, and unchanged system Node/npm: [report](../.acceptance/npm-optional-peer-graph-20261114b/report.json). ## Acquire and review exact npm package tarballs (2026-11-14)

The Windows-only npm acquisition path runs the lock-bound tarball worker in the network-denied AppContainer. The parent broker accepts only the exact ordered URLs from the lock on `registry.npmjs.org`; redirects are refused. It streams with a 64 MiB per-archive cap, 256 MiB aggregate cap, bounded IPC, and a 120-second worker limit. The worker checks SHA-512 SRI, package name/version, safe tar paths, and lifecycle metadata; the parent independently verifies the completed archive set. A content-addressed store persists the immutable lock and exact tarballs, and the desktop now shows the full package list, byte sizes, integrity, and lifecycle script names. Authenticated save/acquire/review API actions reject lock/source/asset mismatches and refuse approval until every package archive is present and reverified.

Combined focused npm/parser/API/AppContainer tests pass (39). Ruff, strict Pyright, desktop TypeScript and generated API contracts pass. No public registry request or model inference was used. ## Install owner-approved wheels into immutable WSL environments (2026-11-13)

Added an authenticated install endpoint and desktop action for exact approved Python dependency locks. The endpoint refuses pending or rejected locks and re-verifies the content-addressed wheelhouse before installation. WSL receives a mapped path to the application-owned wheelhouse rather than a large JSON/base64 payload; its root manager copies and re-hashes the exact files and checks the lock-bound manifest. Package installation runs as UID 65534 in a Bubblewrap network namespace, in a systemd unit capped at 120 seconds, 768 MiB memory, 50% CPU, and 32 tasks, with a 32 MiB tmpfs. Wheel archives are rejected for unsafe members, links/special files, excess member count, or aggregate expansion over 256 MiB. The installed tree is limited to 640 MiB and 20,000 files, transferred to root ownership, made read-only, and committed with an atomic manifest containing the tree hash. Status re-verifies the tree; the WSL manager can remove only the requested digest. SQLite records the installation receipt and the desktop displays it.

The real Ubuntu-24.04 WSL acceptance passed: a local top-level wheel and transitive wheel installed without network access; a second install returned the same tree hash; status rehashed the tree; exact-digest removal succeeded and status then reported absent. The acceptance does not import procedure code or exercise command-time mounting. The API approval-gating test, dependency storage suite, Ruff, strict Pyright, contracts, and desktop TypeScript passed. One unrelated Windows directory-rename fixture failed with `WinError 5` under the default pytest temp root; rerunning with a repository-local temp base left that one case isolated. Next: mount the chosen immutable environment read-only for authorized procedure commands, add use leases and user-facing removal, and verify imports cannot escape their exact procedure scope. No public PyPI request or live model inference was used.

## Add an owner-initiated exact-lock PyPI acquisition flow (2026-11-13)

Added an authenticated `POST .../python-dependency-locks/{environment_digest}/acquire` endpoint and a desktop action to fetch only the files in a saved pending lock. It runs the bounded PyPI-only fetcher, persists the byte-verified set to the content-addressed wheelhouse, and returns the lock with wheelhouse presence set; failed downloads remain pending and cannot be approved. Approval still rechecks every stored byte. The browser flow verifies that approval stays disabled until acquisition completes. The API test uses a mock fetcher for failure and success, then mutates stored bytes to verify approval fails closed. The API test and both Skills browser tests pass, as do Ruff, strict Pyright, OpenAPI/type generation, and TypeScript. Acquisition still runs in the engine process and does not solve requirements or isolate network/resource use; no installer consumes approved environments. No public package service or live model was used.

## Persist verified wheelhouses and gate dependency approval (2026-11-13)

Added `ProcedureWheelhouseStore`, which atomically writes exact lock-matched wheels and a canonical manifest to application data under the environment digest. Reads reject unexpected files, malformed or mismatched manifests, links/path escape, changed sizes or hashes, and wheel metadata that no longer matches the lock. `SkillStore` accepts bytes only for the exact saved pending lock, and approval reopens and verifies the wheelhouse before recording the owner decision; rejection remains available without artifacts. The authenticated lock response reports whether the exact wheelhouse is present, and the desktop disables approval while it is absent. Focused API/storage and wheelhouse tests pass (5 selected); both Skills browser tests pass; Ruff, strict engine Pyright, OpenAPI/TypeScript contract checks, and desktop TypeScript pass. There is not yet an authenticated endpoint or desktop flow to acquire and attach wheelhouses, and the fetch core still runs in the engine process. Thus users cannot yet prepare a verified lock in the app, and no installation path consumes approved locks. No public package service or live model was used.

## Add a bounded exact-lock PyPI wheel acquisition core (2026-11-13)

Added `PyPIWheelAcquirer` for proposed Python dependency locks. It accepts only PEP 691 JSON from `pypi.org`, selects the single exact wheel record, requires matching PyPI SHA-256 and optional size, rejects yanked artifacts and incompatible `Requires-Python`, and fetches only HTTPS wheel URLs on `files.pythonhosted.org`. Redirects and ambient proxy settings are disabled; connection count, response sizes, and total elapsed time are bounded. Downloaded bytes must match the lock and pass the existing archive/metadata/tag verifier. Mock-transport coverage passes (10 tests); Ruff and strict Pyright pass. This is deliberately recorded as an acquisition core, not an isolated resolver: it runs inside the engine process and keeps all wheels in memory. It is not wired to durable wheelhouse storage, review state, or installation, so current API locks still have no byte evidence and dependencies remain unsupported for execution. No live package service or model was used.

## Capture the selected WSL interpreter's wheel target (2026-11-13)

The authenticated Python dependency readiness result now includes the selected WSL interpreter's complete marker environment and an ordered compatible wheel-tag set. Tags come from `sys_tags()` in a temporary venv bootstrapped with that distribution's bundled `ensurepip`; the probe prioritizes the first 240 platform tags and retains universal `*-none-any` fallback tags within a 256-tag bound. The desktop exposes the target data for the upcoming wheel-resolution flow. The command remains network-denied and cleanup-confirmed; its bound is 15 seconds while memory, CPU, task, output, and temporary storage limits remain unchanged. Local Ubuntu-24.04 WSL returned Python 3.12.3 and 254 tags including `py3-none-any`. The readiness/API focused suite passes (55 passed, 1 skipped), and both procedure browser flows pass (2 passed), including the target-data display. A neighboring procedure CRUD browser fixture was updated to return the empty dependency-lock response. Ruff, Pyright, TypeScript, and API type generation pass. This does not resolve, download, verify, store, or install wheels. No live model inference was used.

## Verify locked wheel bytes before dependency installation (2026-11-13)

Added `verify_locked_wheel_bytes` as the artifact-verification core for imported Python dependencies. It requires the wheelhouse filenames to equal the reviewed lock exactly, then checks each wheel's size, SHA-256, safe unique ZIP paths, non-symlink entries, bounded member count and metadata size, matching `.dist-info` identity, `METADATA` package name/version and normalized `Requires-Dist`, and `WHEEL` format/tags. Focused schema and artifact verification coverage passes (27 tests); the adjacent plugin API/storage suite passes with 68 passed and 1 skipped. Ruff, formatting, strict Pyright, and diff checks pass. This verifier is not yet connected to a resolver, persisted wheelhouse, owner approval, or installer, so saved locks can still be prepared without byte evidence and dependencies remain unsupported for execution. Next: acquire wheels through the isolated PyPI-only resolver and route only verified bytes into the owner review flow. No live model inference was used.

Rerun it with `uv run python scripts/wsl_procedure_dependency_acceptance.py --distribution Ubuntu-24.04`. A locally generated root wheel and transitive wheel installed with `--isolated --no-index --only-binary=:all:` into a temporary venv inside the network-denied, size-limited command workspace. ## Validate procedure dependency lock graphs (2026-11-13)

Extended the immutable Python dependency lock so every wheel can carry normalized `Requires-Dist` metadata. The schema now rejects extras and direct dependency URLs, resolves markers against the selected interpreter, checks all active direct and transitive requirements against locked wheel versions, rejects unreachable extra wheels, and caps the total lock graph. ## Verify Windows AppContainer active-process limit (2026-11-13)

Added a real process-tree acceptance that launches small `findstr.exe` readers with open stdin pipes so they stay active. Windows rejects the next launch with quota error 1816 near the configured 32-process ceiling; the test allows 28–31 descendants for existing nested-job membership, records every PID, and verifies cleanup. Hosted run [43486803324](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43486803324) passed all 1,220 Python tests and configured Windows checks; the focused eight-test AppContainer module and static checks pass locally. This directly verifies active-process rejection and descendant cleanup in the Windows command path.

## Verify Windows AppContainer CPU hard cap (2026-11-13)

Added a real CPU saturation acceptance for the Windows command Job Object. It runs PBKDF2 work across threads, measures the command's CPU time against wall time and logical processor count, and checks that its system CPU fraction remains within the configured 50% hard-cap range with scheduling tolerance. Hosted run [43484053186](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43484053186) passed all 1,219 Python tests and configured Windows checks; the focused seven-test module and Ruff, format, strict engine Pyright and diff checks pass locally. Active-process rejection is verified separately above.

## Verify Windows AppContainer command job-object limits (2026-11-13)

The Windows command supervisor now reads its actual Job Object settings back before resuming an isolated command. It fails closed unless the active-process ceiling is 32, process-tree memory is 128 MiB, and the CPU hard cap is 50%. The eight-test Windows local transport integration module passes, as do Ruff, formatting, strict engine Pyright and diff checks. Hosted runs [43481558676](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43481558676), [43484053186](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43484053186), and [43486803324](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43486803324) passed configured Windows checks. The frozen-build fingerprint includes the verification code. CPU saturation and active-process rejection are verified above; broader backend parity remains partial.

## Verify Windows AppContainer process-tree memory limit (2026-11-13)

The Windows command job config applies a 128 MiB process-tree commit limit. The focused local acceptance passes, as do Ruff, formatting, strict engine Pyright and diff checks. Hosted run [43477718830](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43477718830) passed all 1,218 Python tests and configured Windows checks. This verifies process-tree commit-memory enforcement for one Windows command case; active-process rejection and general backend parity remain open.

## Enforce the Windows AppContainer command-output limit (2026-11-13)

Ruff, formatting, Pyright and diff checks pass locally. Artifact review and broader execution resource limits remain open; this does not establish general backend parity.

## Run the Phase 3 coordinator slice in the Windows-local AppContainer (2026-11-13)

The Windows-local command backend could read files at the workspace root but could not access files nested in the already materialized private snapshot: adding an inheritable ACE to the root did not update existing descendants. `AppContainerAccess.grant_existing_tree` now validates and bounds the complete tree, rejects reparse points, journals the full set of explicit grants before changing ACLs, and applies the required read/write/execute rights to existing files and directories. The Windows command transport uses this grant on its private snapshot. Its integration fixture now reads and writes a nested file while still confirming the host sentinel and loopback listener are denied.

The deterministic coordinator vertical slice passes on `windows-appcontainer-v1`: one fixture defect is edited, its restricted command reports `1 passed`, and a separate final validation passes against the current edited tree. An earlier full integration attempt had one opaque failure; a focused rerun and the subsequent full group both pass, so it is recorded as a transient test failure rather than hidden. Hosted verification is pending. This proves one coordinator slice on the Windows-local backend; it does not close Phase 3, installed-app/clean-machine acceptance, or Hermes backend parity. ## Bind isolated AppContainer descendants to an owned Windows job (2026-11-13)

The Windows AppContainer shim now assigns each isolated child to a nested Job Object with kill-on-close before resuming it. If setup fails before assignment, the suspended child is terminated directly; if the shim exits while descendants remain, closing the job terminates the process tree. A Windows integration acceptance starts a heartbeat child from an isolated MCP server and confirms the heartbeat stops when the server exits; the existing loopback-denial acceptance still passes. Ruff, strict Pyright, and both focused Windows integration cases pass. This hardens the MCP launch path and provides a containment primitive for the planned Windows-local executor; it does not implement or verify workspace command execution.

## Define a shared command transport contract (2026-11-13)

`CommandController` now depends on a typed transport protocol for immutable command intents, private workspace snapshots, streamed output, and stop/cleanup/status controls instead of importing the WSL implementation directly. The controller requires the output callback contract from every backend; existing controller fixtures were updated to match. Ruff, formatting, strict Pyright, `git diff --check`, and the command-controller unit suite pass (12 tests). WSL remains the only configured and implemented command backend; this refactor does not claim a Windows-local sandbox. It establishes the controller boundary needed to add and verify one without changing saved WSL behavior.

## Re-run the Phase 3 workspace slice through WSL without model inference (2026-11-13)

Added `scripts/workspace_vertical_slice_diagnostic.py` as a reproducible acceptance for the actual coordinator workspace tools: a deterministic provider identifies and fixes a fixture defect, the coordinator runs the project check inside the Ubuntu-24.04 network-denied WSL sandbox, and the separate final-project validator confirms fresh evidence matches the edited tree. The acceptance passed with one changed file (`answer.py`), a successful restricted test command, current-tree validation evidence, and no unresolved calls. Report: [`.acceptance/workspace-vertical-slice-6e937af2ce684937af0acc3f8defe023/report.json`](../.acceptance/workspace-vertical-slice-6e937af2ce684937af0acc3f8defe023/report.json). Model inference was not requested. This adds repeatable coordinator-level evidence for the bounded Phase 3 slice; broad project compatibility and other execution backends remain open.

## Switch into a restored profile and cold-start the installed app (2026-11-13)

The installed smoke exposed that Tauri's `app.restart()` launched its replacement inside the old desktop's kill-on-close process job, so Windows terminated the new instance when the old one exited. Profile activation now spawns the replacement first, then removes the old job's kill-on-close flag only after spawn succeeds; the replacement survives and establishes its own process job. If launch fails, the current app keeps running with its cleanup rule intact. The installed UI acceptance now invokes the real profile activation, observes the process restart, confirms the selected profile after cold start, reads the restored run through the new engine, re-verifies the database/artifact, and verifies graceful shutdown without inference. Report: [`.acceptance/native-installed-backup-d77961b6d8bc4f46a03f49048f6325db/report.json`](../.acceptance/native-installed-backup-d77961b6d8bc4f46a03f49048f6325db/report.json). The unsigned local installer is 77,584,639 bytes, SHA-256 `214f55169982a6d74dd3c0219eadce95c7918d4f37e56710bcb1488ebe9f2119`; desktop SHA-256 `339c2cc671565bc6d4c7e8b94540c0954c9683fc1d976139b7c82eb72ea486c4`, engine SHA-256 `1c52b98d1e6756447a60bb8e6f2a894c337cc22215b78d8049df53ee07949059`. Rust tests pass (11), the production desktop and installer build pass, and installed UI/CLI backup restore, activation, cold-start validation, uninstall and data preservation pass. Hosted source run [43383169650](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43383169650) and installer run [43383183151](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43383183151) passed on commit `0238f5e6093d488d4918b7085683d4976069edc2`. The hosted installed-app report confirms restore UI, desktop restart, active-profile cold start, restored-run access, database/artifact verification, and no model inference. The hosted 0.1.1 installer is 77,889,064 bytes, SHA-256 `059f6f8988695f5dc5cb3d3e59a01b3358fb8238fe95cd69d3575e67a98f3802`; desktop SHA-256 `59d3b097dfae43f94f5517e6996c1f5e5c1543aea13165d893c7220f356e616a`, engine SHA-256 `65c3dd221d27ae9c7dfeaabb4ae45acee1caa4f83fed692846b4d96040b32edd`. The installer and reports are preserved in [`.acceptance/hosted-installer-43383183151/`](../.acceptance/hosted-installer-43383183151/).

## Exercise desktop backup restore in the installed Windows app (2026-11-13)

Extended the isolated installed-app smoke to drive the actual Tauri WebView restore flow, send the generated `.xaa-backup` through the authenticated `/backups/restore` endpoint, verify the copied run/events/artifact bytes and SQLite integrity, and prove the active profile remains intact. The restore-profile restart button stays disabled until its explicit checkbox is selected. A small native dialog-selection interface is enabled only when `XAA_TEST_ACCEPTANCE=1`, allowing the packaged UI path to run unattended in isolated acceptance; ordinary launches still use the native file picker. The local unsigned installer passed isolated install, UI restore, CLI restore, profile preservation, uninstall and data preservation; report: [`.acceptance/native-installed-backup-fb37fdc304964840b885ccef8fcefa7c/report.json`](../.acceptance/native-installed-backup-fb37fdc304964840b885ccef8fcefa7c/report.json). Hosted installer run [43379437695](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43379437695) also passed and its installed restore UI report is [here](../.acceptance/hosted-installer-43379437695/xaa-windows-installer-unsigned/.acceptance/installed-backup-aa3402b56c3c48f0b014bda8dff9b76a/report.json). The hosted unsigned installer is 77,895,852 bytes, SHA-256 `3ce38b10feb7f4581c93d58fd9fe77353fff31934cc2c1d16b03f55631a1092e`; desktop SHA-256 `746a8df74985db0c7ed9f85c9aa1945347c94d51196bc8290cb4d4c482cbf04e`, engine SHA-256 `348e8e29bccfaa3b775a5c006edeebc35513b66eddff6e3416039dd1d80dee30`. The UI restore browser acceptance passes (1), the native Tauri suite passes (11), desktop typecheck/production build and generated API contract check pass, and Rust/PowerShell/Node smoke syntax checks pass. No inference was requested. The initial installed UI acceptance verified confirmation gating; its follow-up found and closed the activation/restart gap documented above.

## Restore portable backups from desktop Settings (2026-11-13)

Settings now supports both backup export and restore. Restore asks for a `.xaa-backup` and a destination parent; the authenticated engine validates the archive, database schema/integrity, and artifact hashes, then writes into a uniquely named new profile. The existing active profile is not overwritten. The owner can explicitly switch to the restored profile and restart X.A.A.; the selected profile root is saved in desktop settings, backup export follows the active profile, and Settings can switch back to the default profile. Focused API restore tests pass (2), API-client tests pass (17), the browser export/restore/restart-confirmation flow passes (1), storage backup regressions pass (6), the Rust desktop suite passes (11), and the frontend production build/typecheck and generated contract check pass. The bundled release preview also passed native boot, tray/window lifecycle, API authentication, engine readiness and graceful shutdown without inference; report: [`.acceptance/2536f146-3c48-4efc-9856-cb2182049126/report.json`](../.acceptance/2536f146-3c48-4efc-9856-cb2182049126/report.json). `npm run build:installer` produced an unsigned 0.1.1 installer at `../build/installer/Xyst AI Agent_0.1.1_x64-setup.exe`, 77,580,841 bytes, SHA-256 `6e6e62c6fb990fd2c01cc57922e9c5ea6edd18959f150bb8d8ec0140f359c580`, matching the installer manifest. Installer run [43371792633](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43371792633) exposed a stale skill-readiness fixture assertion; that was corrected. Source run [43374691059](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43374691059) found the new endpoint missing from the exported OpenAPI contract; both `contracts/openapi.json` and generated desktop types are now updated. Installer run [43374702047](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43374702047) found the native boot smoke still expected a blocked run to open on startup, contrary to the intended recovery notice. The smoke now verifies the run stays on the workspace home until **Review saved run** is selected; local packaged recovery acceptance passes in [`.acceptance/fb27d299-ff7e-47f0-931e-a25a967e5b72/report.json`](../.acceptance/fb27d299-ff7e-47f0-931e-a25a967e5b72/report.json). The follow-up source run [43375890528](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43375890528) and Windows installer run [43375898520](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43375898520) both passed on `f71a58a`. The hosted installer is 77,883,298 bytes with manifest-matched SHA-256 `5190c6fcc8e1030afb650dd1a71e25dd091f4a535ca61e303b5d39268f7254e1`; hosted acceptance verifies installed boot, user-data preservation, CLI backup/restore, and owner-reviewed startup recovery without automatic resume. The local installed desktop restore flow, activation/restart and cold-start into restored data now pass; hosted CI for the restart fix, real-user migration, and disaster-recovery behavior remain open. No live model inference or real-user data restore was run.

Python package installation for imported procedures remains open. Added [the dependency lifecycle design](architecture/procedure-python-dependencies.md), a frozen Pydantic lock schema bound to the canonical skill UUID/version/source digest and WSL target interpreter, and SQLite persistence keyed by exact environment digest. The authenticated API saves, lists and records a final approve/reject decision for each lock. Saving verifies the imported source digest and exact normalized Python declarations before marking the lock `pending_review`; incomplete declarations and direct references fail closed. The desktop shows each pending lock's exact requirements, wheel names, versions, hashes, sizes, target tags and environment digest before an explicit approve/reject action. The lock, API, plugin import and migration suite passes (81 passed, 1 skipped); the browser review flow passes (1), contracts and desktop type checks pass, as do Ruff and strict Pyright. No package manager or procedure code has yet been run to install dependencies. Lock preparation/resolution, wheel acquisition, offline installation, sealed mounts, installed-environment lifecycle and local-index acceptance remain open gates.

## Keep blocked-run recovery from taking over the home screen (2026-11-13)

Startup still loads the latest recoverable run without resuming it, but a blocked run no longer forces the desktop away from the workspace landing page. Home now shows a short recovery notice and a deliberate “Review saved run” action; the saved objective and any pending recovery controls remain available in the run view. This keeps old test prompts from dominating the app on launch while preserving owner recovery. Startup-recovery browser acceptance passes (2 tests), including checks that the run is not opened or resumed until the owner selects it; the production desktop TypeScript/Vite build passes. `npm run build:installer` succeeds and produces the unsigned 0.1.1 installer at `../build/installer/Xyst AI Agent_0.1.1_x64-setup.exe`, 77,564,927 bytes with SHA-256 `f6291a0e482c17c65d94911b3914184f5e2699bfc096964aef681625f977a375`; the manifest matches the release desktop and engine staged at `../build/native-release-preview-87700-1794546472116565700/`. The normal development stage could not be refreshed while an existing desktop process held its executable open. No model inference or persisted user data changes were made. The rebuilt installer has not received installed-package acceptance or been published.

## Verify skill dependency readiness in the native desktop (2026-11-13)

Added `scripts/native_skill_readiness_smoke.mjs` and `npm run test:native:skill-readiness` to exercise the owner-facing dependency check in a freshly built Tauri WebView. The smoke imports an inactive fixture procedure into an isolated engine database, discovers and self-tests Ubuntu-24.04, invokes the exact-version Python readiness action from the procedure panel, and checks the rendered available, incompatible, missing and unverified results. It verifies there are no saved runs, the model was not called, no package was installed or imported, and the engine shut down gracefully. The native frontend/type/Rust/frozen-engine build passed; the smoke passed with desktop SHA-256 `62fab97140b45ac6ded8bf8bb1282d1add9726349ba60481672461309e3340e9` and engine SHA-256 `fb37df6d5fe198dfef054d3f9721db9650058e6de0deefd2fa38343a6d0917b4`. Report and UI capture: [report](../.acceptance/native-skill-readiness-ded36a43-3a62-4fa2-9e01-645242d5ad2c/report.json), [screenshot](../.acceptance/native-skill-readiness-ded36a43-3a62-4fa2-9e01-645242d5ad2c/native-skill-readiness.png). Hosted source workflow [43367241259](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43367241259) passed; its installer job was skipped because only the diagnostic and docs changed. The later current-source installer run [43383183151](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43383183151) also exercised installed-package WSL dependency readiness: the frozen engine classified available, missing, incompatible and unsupported-manager declarations, performed no install/import/inference, and shut down cleanly ([install report](../.acceptance/hosted-installer-43383183151/xaa-windows-installer-unsigned/build/installer/install-smoke.json), nested report `installed-skill-readiness-3c733c89d0584bb18a6ea39250628332/report.json`). Dependency installation and broader skill parity remain open.

## Check skill Python dependencies in the restricted WSL runtime (2026-11-13)

Added an authenticated readiness check for the exact imported procedure version. The API derives its Python requirement specs from immutable import provenance; the desktop can invoke the check only after the owner has selected a WSL distribution that passed the sandbox self-test. The probe runs fixed `/usr/bin/python3 -I` code inside the network-denied, private WSL sandbox and reads only `importlib.metadata`; it does not import declared packages, install dependencies or inspect the project workspace. PEP 508 markers are evaluated against the target WSL interpreter's reported environment, and results distinguish available, missing, incompatible, not-applicable and unchecked declarations. Other package managers stay counted as unverified. The focused Python/API suites pass (50 passed, 1 skipped), the browser flow passes (2), generated API contracts and desktop type checking pass, and strict Pyright/Ruff/format checks pass. Real Ubuntu-24.04 source acceptance found `python-apt` and `requests` available and a deliberately absent package missing; report: [`../.acceptance/skill-dependency-readiness/34a847308ac2476c9897203de751fdf7/report.json`](../.acceptance/skill-dependency-readiness/34a847308ac2476c9897203de751fdf7/report.json). A newly frozen Windows engine then imported a fixture procedure through the installed API and queried this exact endpoint against Ubuntu-24.04: the packaged route classified `requests` as available, a deliberately absent dependency as missing, a version conflict as incompatible, and two unsupported declarations as unverified; report: [`../.acceptance/installed-skill-readiness-49474ac3ac1c4ccfa8113f7509d3ce11/report.json`](../.acceptance/installed-skill-readiness-49474ac3ac1c4ccfa8113f7509d3ce11/report.json). The probe ran network-denied without importing package code, installing dependencies, using the project workspace or requesting model inference. Hosted source run [43362066077](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43362066077) passed all configured checks, including 1,193 Python tests, API/browser checks and strict source validation. Hosted installer run [43363167713](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43363167713) passed package creation, installed-app recovery, upgrade from 0.1.0, boot, standard-user install, explicit data removal and uninstall; its manifest-verified unsigned 0.1.1 installer SHA-256 is `88e10d3126e81d91555580787e996dd1963daa268f35d2948af3af362dbbfb65` and the artifact/reports are preserved in [`.acceptance/hosted-installer-43363167713/`](../.acceptance/hosted-installer-43363167713/). The hosted generic readiness smoke does not invoke the WSL endpoint; installed endpoint coverage is from the local frozen-engine acceptance. This work does not install dependencies or close the broader skills gate.

## Package the current desktop build as a Windows program (2026-11-13)

Built the current desktop source into a per-user Windows NSIS installer with `npm run build:installer`. The build includes the Tauri desktop executable, frozen Python engine, MIT license, dependency inventory, third-party notices and 0.1.1 release notes; TypeScript checking, Vite production build, PyInstaller engine bundling, Rust release build and NSIS packaging all completed successfully. The unsigned installer is at `../build/installer/Xyst AI Agent_0.1.1_x64-setup.exe` (77,556,085 bytes; SHA-256 `d55df94f9038280338d336cb76219c4c2bde78cf62a7598d78cceec0178c1845`). Its tested unsigned installer is preserved at [`.acceptance/hosted-installer-43358674361/build/installer/Xyst AI Agent_0.1.1_x64-setup.exe`](../.acceptance/hosted-installer-43358674361/build/installer/Xyst%20AI%20Agent_0.1.1_x64-setup.exe) (77,864,683 bytes; manifest SHA-256 `9e9c3afaa47cf6d024d5f32adabba05a505f64cb5664c6dce3ebba3712f3e612`). The local installer build remains in `../build/installer/`; the hosted tested artifact has stronger clean-runner install evidence.

## Execute selected skill scripts in the restricted command snapshot (2026-11-13)

The owned snapshot worker now validates the selected immutable procedure digest and stages linked `.py` and `.sh` resources under a reserved `__xaa_selected_skill__/<skill-id>/v<version>/...` path for both parent and specialist command snapshots. The saved WSL command grant remains independently required; specialists also need explicit `workspace.command` task permission. Skill metadata grants neither permission. Command changes are discarded, and the source project is never modified. Parent and specialist command descriptions name available selected scripts only when their respective grants are present. Specialist command evidence rebuilds the same augmented snapshot before checking its hash. Focused snapshot, command, controller, procedure and specialist suites pass (68 tests); Ruff and formatting pass on the changed files, and strict Pyright reports no issues in the affected engine modules. A local Ubuntu-24.04 WSL diagnostic ran a selected Python script in a parent command snapshot, read its fixture input and confirmed the original workspace remained unchanged ([report](../.acceptance/selected-skill-script/32f24fc1-cfff-4d4e-bbc6-c640cd50d9ff/report.json)). A specialist WSL diagnostic executed selected Python and shell scripts through a private task, verified both command records against the augmented snapshot, confirmed the original project stayed unchanged and left no unresolved commands ([report](../.acceptance/selected-skill-specialist/4d5d7822-2c2e-43b3-aa98-d180cdea047d/report.json)); neither acceptance used model inference. A rebuilt frozen Windows engine passed snapshot IPC acceptance with the script bytes included in the returned hash-verified manifest ([report](../.acceptance/d6fc2ef6-7240-4600-bfa5-61b4f3bd24e7/report.json)). The desktop objective form and user guide now state that Python/shell execution needs the separate restricted-command grant and that other script types stay inactive; the selected-procedure browser flow passes (1), and the production desktop build/typecheck passes. No model inference was used. Hosted source run [43356184122](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43356184122) passed all checks, including 1,191 Python tests, for the preceding commit; hosted source run [43357517381](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43357517381) then passed the user-facing copy follow-up, the selected-procedure browser regression and all 1,191 Python tests; its installer job was skipped because packaging inputs were unchanged. Manual Windows installer run [43352302657](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43352302657) passed packaged browser, private task command scope, install, boot, upgrade and uninstall acceptance. Its unsigned 0.1.1 installer is 77,858,797 bytes with SHA-256 `8c4244971d27973d14177c8de5a4aeedc80dd882b0d25c5cb3c2651014ff2565`; the binary and reports are preserved in [`.acceptance/hosted-installer-43352302657/`](../.acceptance/hosted-installer-43352302657/). Dependency installation and non-Python/non-shell scripts remain unsupported, so this is a narrow script-execution slice rather than full Agent Skills parity.

## Show which imported skill dependencies remain unverified (2026-11-13)

Agent Plugin discovery and the desktop now show bounded manager/package names for dependencies outside the supported Python metadata check. The display omits version constraints, direct URLs and unparsed declarations; it installs or executes nothing. The names are guidance for owner review only, and unsupported managers remain unresolved. Focused plugin tests pass (37, 1 platform skip), the desktop production build passes, and Ruff, strict Pyright and `git diff --check` pass. A browser regression verifies an npm package appears in both requirement summaries while its version spec and URL stay hidden (1 passed). The frozen installed-readiness diagnostic now verifies that `npm:left-pad` survives API serialization while its version constraint and an unparsed private URL do not; a local PyInstaller engine acceptance passes without inference ([report](../.acceptance/installed-skill-readiness-56edeb20034b481b97e9e214c9ebaac9/report.json)). Hosted Windows source run [43344562617](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43344562617) passed the full Python suite, static checks, pinned inventories and recovery checks. Hosted installer run [43344581108](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43344581108) passed upgrade/install/boot/uninstall and installed-app recovery checks; its frozen readiness report confirms installed Python package versions are classified as available, missing, incompatible and unresolved without model inference ([report](../.acceptance/hosted-installer-43344581108/xaa-windows-installer-unsigned/.acceptance/installed-skill-readiness-0534715457344a44b57d3b3ac8035a05/report.json)). These checks do not verify non-Python dependency installation, credential readiness or skill execution. This improves the Phase 5 readiness report but does not close dependency installation or skill execution parity.

Extended the native MCP smoke to request and display prompt and resource-template argument completions in the actual Tauri WebView, mark suggestions untrusted, require an owner selection, and use that value in the following prompt preview or resource read. This does not yet prove previous-to-new installed recovery or compatibility with every historical Linux receipt schema; manager incompatibility remains fail-closed and unresolved recovery remains reserved for reconciliation. ## Journal MCP isolation profile intent before Windows creates it (2026-11-12)

The engine now durably writes an owner-bound, empty-grants record before `CreateAppContainerProfile` can create a persistent Windows profile. If the engine exits after profile creation but before its first ACL grant, the next isolated connection can derive and remove the orphan profile from that intent record. Temporary journal filenames also bind the writer PID and process-creation FILETIME; startup removes an orphaned partial temp file after verifying its owner exited or its PID was reused, while preserving a temp file owned by a live engine. Subprocess acceptances cover the profile-created/pre-ACL boundary, an interrupted initial temp write, and active-owner temp preservation. All six crash-recovery variants and the temp-file case pass; the full MCP stdio integration file passes (30 tests). Ruff, formatting, strict Pyright, and `git diff --check` pass. Hosted source run [43221314774](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43221314774) passed all checks, including 1,164 Python tests and the journal crash-recovery cases. Manual installer run [43222767057](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43222767057) passed all 44 steps, including package build, MCP isolation, upgrade/boot/backup/restore/uninstall, standard-user install and explicit data removal. Its unsigned 0.1.1 installer is retained at [`.acceptance/hosted-installer-43222767057/`](../.acceptance/hosted-installer-43222767057/); SHA-256 `e1e8b873e88cb74aefe1ad7d81c815926122114b6ad05e3f6ab6d582ac0770ba` matches the manifest. Physical power-loss durability remains open. No model inference was used.

## Recover MCP isolation grants safely after PID reuse and crash-boundary interruption (2026-11-12)

Version-2 MCP isolation journal entries now bind their owner PID to the Windows process-creation FILETIME. Recovery removes a grant when the numeric PID belongs to a different process, while refusing cleanup when process identity cannot be queried. Liveness now uses the process handle's wait state, avoiding the ambiguous `STILL_ACTIVE` value that can also be an exit code. Existing version-1 journals remain readable and use the conservative PID-only rule. Extended the Windows subprocess acceptance to cover an engine crash after the journal is durably replaced but before the ACL is changed, simulated PID reuse, legacy journal recovery, the existing post-ACL crash, and an exited owner with code 259; all five pass. The complete local Windows MCP stdio integration file passes (28 tests). Ruff, formatting, strict Pyright, and `git diff --check` pass. Hosted source run [43215777839](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43215777839) passed all checks, including 1,162 Python tests and the five Windows crash-recovery variants. Manual installer run [43215853689](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43215853689) passed all 44 steps, including upgrade/boot/backup/restore/uninstall and installed MCP network-isolation acceptance. ## Verify the pinned MCP SDK in isolated native desktop acceptance (2026-11-12)

Replaced the installed native AppContainer list probe's hand-written JSON-RPC fixture with a real `MCPServer` stdio server from the pinned `mcp==2.3.0` SDK. The broader third-party interoperability and installed-release fixture coverage remain open. No model inference was requested.

## Flush the MCP isolation journal rename before ACL changes (2026-11-12)

The isolation journal now writes its complete record, fsyncs the temporary file, then replaces the destination with same-directory `MoveFileExW(MOVEFILE_WRITE_THROUGH)` before applying any AppContainer ACL. This tightens crash ordering; actual power-loss behavior is still unverified. The full Windows MCP stdio suite passes locally (24 tests); Ruff, formatting and strict Pyright pass for the changed module. Rebuilt the frozen engine and ran the full native desktop smoke against it: report [`.acceptance/84e05f22-12e2-4275-aeb0-436cf286d4e5/report.json`](../.acceptance/84e05f22-12e2-4275-aeb0-436cf286d4e5/report.json), desktop SHA-256 `6830605a065607efe9e05f858a4b1d0042e0e5d42894a6036716c6f54452a426`, engine SHA-256 `edd735f0742ed95003c1fdad1321712c8dfb863bf266d0c5e7c6a9991316cc7f`. It proves the saved isolation option, parent loopback reachability, blocked loopback from the frozen MCP process, clean disconnect and shutdown; model inference was not requested. Hosted source run [43202366803](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43202366803) passed all checks, including 1,158 Python tests. Current-source installer run [43203298039](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43203298039) passed the full Windows install/boot/upgrade/backup/restore/uninstall acceptance; its installed native MCP test confirms isolation persisted, the desktop can reach loopback, the AppContainer server is blocked, and disconnect cleans up. The unsigned installer is retained with its report at [`.acceptance/hosted-installer-43203298039/`](../.acceptance/hosted-installer-43203298039/); SHA-256 `2d8a2ef1a543ee4cfd38bf5242c3000dae24a4b04ac1a46a3e30ec938c004795`, 77,841,530 bytes. No model inference was used.


## Recover temporary MCP isolation ACL grants after engine crashes (2026-11-12)

Windows AppContainer filesystem grants are now written to an atomic, bounded journal before the matching ACL change; journal file contents are fsynced and the same-directory rename uses [MoveFileExW with `MOVEFILE_WRITE_THROUGH`](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winbase/nf-winbase-movefileexw?view=win32-7.0) before ACL mutation. This strengthens ordering and persistence but does not substitute for a real power-loss acceptance. Each record binds the temporary profile to its engine PID and exact path/mask/flags. A new isolated connection scans the engine data directory and removes grants/profile records only when the owning PID is no longer running; active engines are skipped so a second desktop process cannot revoke their access. Failed or invalid recovery records stop isolation startup and remain available for retry. Orderly shutdown removes the journal only after ACL and profile cleanup succeed. The Windows integration suite previously passed 24 tests, including a child engine process that applies a real ACL grant, exits via `os._exit`, and has its grant removed by the fresh parent process. After adding the write-through journal rename, the full Windows MCP stdio suite passes locally (24 tests); Ruff, formatting and strict Pyright pass for the changed module. A freshly frozen engine passes abrupt-crash recovery and loopback-denial shim acceptances. A native desktop/engine stage also passes an end-to-end MCP flow that verifies saved isolation settings and proves a local listener reachable from the desktop is blocked from the isolated server; report `.acceptance/bdf7e63d-c5bf-4ec3-b895-a45a9d3d4a24/report.json`, engine SHA-256 `422a1030252fd72903b05013198072609bf884e397107ed96c73f5035b948582`. That acceptance exposed that a `Scripts/python.exe` venv launcher also needs read/execute access to the base runtime in `pyvenv.cfg`; the engine now validates and grants that bounded runtime root, and the direct Windows isolation tests pass with the actual venv launcher. Hosted source run [43192298498](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43192298498) passed on `b70bd81`, including the complete Python suite and process-recovery acceptances. The follow-up native desktop smoke passes from the frozen stage with the actual AppContainer loopback denial; report `.acceptance/bee33cf3-e274-46dc-b587-f55e95c8d653/report.json`, desktop SHA-256 `6830605a065607efe9e05f858a4b1d0042e0e5d42894a6036716c6f54452a426`, engine SHA-256 `422a1030252fd72903b05013198072609bf884e397107ed96c73f5035b948582`. Commit `2cdc9b6` adds that same isolation proof to the installed-package smoke flow and passed hosted source run [43195981370](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43195981370). Commit `ff37d89` fixes the native smoke log path by explicitly routing logs into its acceptance directory; the complete frozen native smoke passed locally and hosted source run [43197593828](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43197593828) passed all checks, including 1,158 Python tests. Manual installer run [43203298039](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43203298039) passed against the journal-durability code revision, with the installed network-isolation evidence and package hash recorded above. No model inference was used. Power-loss recovery remains pending.

## Add opt-in Windows MCP process network isolation (2026-11-12)

Added a per-server `network_isolation` setting for stdio MCP connections, stored in schema 33 with existing rows defaulting to `false`, exposed through the API and desktop, and rejected for Streamable HTTP. On Windows the SDK-owned process is now a short-lived shim inside the existing resource-control Job Object; it launches the MCP executable as an AppContainer with no network capabilities. The host grants temporary filesystem ACLs only to the executable/runtime, explicit script/package roots, configured working directory, and package-data root. The child inherits only its three protocol handles. Selected roots reject reparse points; descendant reparse targets are skipped rather than traversed, and selected trees are bounded to 50,000 entries. A Windows subprocess acceptance verifies that an ordinary process reaches a local listener while the isolated MCP tool is blocked, completes MCP handshake/tool discovery, and restores ACL entry counts for the working directory and existing descendants after cleanup. The focused MCP stdio/HTTP and schema migration suites pass (54 tests); the targeted isolation acceptance also passes through the freshly frozen engine shim. The rebuilt desktop frontend, frozen engine and Tauri executable stage at `build/native-appcontainer-preview/` have SHA-256 values `6830605a065607efe9e05f858a4b1d0042e0e5d42894a6036716c6f54452a426` and `0833b17b5bddb37327652184bc28516d772047d2654b18b4047bb87291e3e5dd`; TypeScript, Ruff, strict Pyright, generated API contract, and the MCP desktop browser flow pass. No model inference was used. ACL grants/profile are removed on orderly shutdown; abrupt engine termination can leave stale ACL ACEs for a stopped identity, so crash-time ACL cleanup remains a release gap. Hosted source and installed-package confirmation remain pending; isolation is Windows-only and opt-in, `.cmd`/`.bat` commands are unsupported, and third-party filesystem/runtime compatibility is not yet established.

constraints in the desktop. Other managers remain explicitly unverified; the check neither runs

engine builds and the existing installed workflows still pass; see the follow-up above for the
new dedicated installed-app readiness acceptance and its pending hosted run. No live model
inference was used.

## Bound specialist plan dependency depth (2026-11-11)

Task graphs already rejected dependency cycles and over-budget reservations, but allowed a flat
plan to chain all 64 tasks serially. Added a 16-level maximum prerequisite chain while retaining
the 64-task graph limit, with a regression covering the allowed boundary and the first rejected
level. This bounds dependency/review depth; it does not add recursive specialist delegation.
Focused graph, planning, and plan-API suites pass (14 tests), as do Ruff formatting/lint, Pyright
for the changed runtime module, and `git diff --check`. Broader live-model acceptance was not run
for this deterministic graph change. Hosted Windows source workflow
[43111503649](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43111503649) passed the
full Python suite, static/client checks, Hermes inventories, browser checks, and process recovery
acceptances; installer validation was skipped because packaging inputs did not change.

## Rename shared desktop and CLI conversations (2026-11-11)

Saved shared conversations now have an editable 80-character display title independent of the stable session ID. The authenticated API validates titles, limits updates to local CLI-backed sessions, and preserves the model/workspace/mode binding and saved message history; renaming does not alter the last-used ordering. The desktop lists and edits those titles, while CLI commands continue using the unchanged session ID. The focused storage/API suite passes (11 tests), the shared-session desktop browser acceptance passes, the generated OpenAPI/TypeScript contract is current, and TypeScript, Ruff, Pyright and `git diff --check` pass. The browser fixture now follows the redesigned app navigation back to the session panel after a run. No live model inference was used.

## Preserve XLSX worksheet structure, visibility, and hyperlinks (2026-11-11)

XLSX extraction now labels worksheets declared `hidden` or `veryHidden`, hidden rows, and cells in hidden column ranges while retaining the underlying values for review. Column definitions are bounded to 1,024 ranges per sheet and the Excel worksheet limit of 16,384 columns; invalid or conflicting range metadata and malformed visibility flags fail closed. Explicit merged-cell ranges are attached to their anchor cells, with bounds and overlap validation. Cell hyperlinks preserve their cell range, display string, tooltip and internal location; external relationship targets are never opened, and web targets expose only the host while omitting credentials, path, query and fragment. Hyperlink data is capped at 1,024 per workbook and each text field is bounded. The focused document extraction suite passes (98 tests); Ruff, formatting, strict Pyright for the parser, and `git diff --check` pass. Filtering, non-anchor merge geometry and other workbook display behavior remain open. Hidden-range handling follows Microsoft's [Open XML guidance](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/office/open-xml/spreadsheet/how-to-retrieve-a-list-of-the-hidden-rows-or-columns-in-a-spreadsheet); merged ranges follow the [`mergeCell` reference](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/documentformat.openxml.spreadsheet.mergecell?view=openxml-3.0.1); hyperlink references follow Microsoft's [`Hyperlink` documentation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/documentformat.openxml.spreadsheet.hyperlink?view=openxml-3.0.1); the 16,384-column ceiling follows [Excel's worksheet limits](https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/excel/excel-specifications-and-limits). No live model inference was used.

## Add run-bound legacy MCP sampling

MCP `sampling/createMessage` now routes through the owning run coordinator for servers explicitly configured with the legacy handshake. Automatic protocol negotiation remains the default because SDK 2.3.0 rejects the deprecated sampling callback on protocol 2026-07-28; the desktop exposes the per-run grant only for a legacy-configured server. The callback is active only during that server’s approved connected tool call, persists bounded request/completion/failure events tied to the parent tool call, uses the selected run model and shared iteration/token budget, supplies no objective, workspace or conversation history, disables tools, and accepts only bounded text with measured usage. A failed sample records its usage when known, marks the MCP result uncertain, and blocks before another model turn. Schema 29 adds the protocol-mode setting and migrates existing MCP servers to automatic negotiation. The focused MCP/API/coordinator/migration set passes (95 tests), the MCP browser workflow passes, strict Pyright and TypeScript/contracts pass, and the production desktop bundle builds. No live model inference was used. A full local `pytest -q` run reported 1,048 passed and 50 setup errors because Windows denied access to pytest’s shared `%TEMP%\pytest-of-hicks` directory. The six affected test files were rerun under a repository-local temp root (96 passed, 1 skipped; one unrelated directory rename was denied) and that case passed when rerun under a fresh `build/` temp root. A single clean full-suite invocation and hosted CI are still pending; installed desktop acceptance is also pending.
