## Render common XLSX date values for reading (2026-11-11)

XLSX extraction now adds a readable date beside the original numeric serial when a cell uses a simple year/day date format. It honors the workbook's 1900 or 1904 date system, including Excel's compatibility serial for the fictitious 1900-02-29, while retaining the raw value and number-format code. The limited formatter does not evaluate formulas or reproduce Excel locale, time-only formats, conditional sections, column-width clipping, or general workbook rendering. The focused document suite passes (62 tests); Ruff, formatting, Pyright for the changed parser and `git diff --check` pass. No live model inference was used. Sources: [Microsoft's Open XML number-format behavior](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/documentformat.openxml.spreadsheet.numberingformat) and [the 1904 date-system definition](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/openspecs/office_file_formats/ms-xls/4a5e900a-0eb0-4355-8fc1-81aab8f46e8b). Hosted Windows workflow [43064992112](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43064992112) passed, including the full Python suite and process-recovery gates; the installer job was skipped because packaging inputs were unchanged.

## Render common XLSX numeric display formats (2026-11-11)

The bounded XLSX formatter now also renders common fixed-point and grouped values, simple currency prefixes, and percentages beside the original cached numeric value. It applies explicit decimal precision and half-up rounding. Advanced Excel format sections, locale-specific currency behavior, scientific notation, fractions and column-width display remain unsupported; formulas remain uncalculated. The focused document suite passes (62 tests); Ruff, formatting, Pyright for the changed parser and `git diff --check` pass. No live model inference was used. Hosted Windows workflow [43066750065](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43066750065) passed, including the full Python suite and desktop/recovery checks; the installer job was skipped because packaging inputs were unchanged.

## Resolve inherited DOCX section headers and footers (2026-11-11)

DOCX section properties now resolve header and footer relationships independently by type (`default`, `first`, `even`). Explicit references replace the inherited reference for following sections; omitted types continue the previous section's relationship. A referenced part is extracted once and labeled with every section that references it, including inherited uses. References inside revision history are ignored; duplicate section/type references and references outside section properties fail closed. Two regressions cover shared header/footer inheritance and a replacement inherited by the next section. The focused suite passes (61 tests), and the full local Python suite passes (1,083 passed, 1 skipped) using a fresh workspace temp directory. Ruff, formatting, parser Pyright and `git diff --check` pass. Hosted Windows run [43062092107](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43062092107) passed the full Python suite, Edge browser checks, Hermes inventories, static checks, desktop browser checks and recovery gates; installer validation was skipped because packaging inputs were unchanged. The section numbers describe effective reference inheritance, not page-by-page visibility or full Word rendering. Sources: [header reference inheritance](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/documentformat.openxml.wordprocessing.headerreference) and [footer reference inheritance](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/documentformat.openxml.wordprocessing.footerreference?view=openxml-3.0.1).

## Validate DOCX table merge alignment (2026-11-11)

DOCX extraction now requires a legacy horizontal merge continuation to occupy the grid position immediately following its active restart/continuation group. Explicit vertical merge continuations must match the same grid-column span as an active preceding-row merge; overlapping but mismatched spans fail closed. Orphan vertical continuation markers remain preserved because Word attempts to interpret them even without a preceding restart. This checks group alignment without reconstructing merged table geometry. Two regressions cover orphan horizontal continuation and mismatched vertical spans; the focused suite passes (59 tests), and the full local Python suite passes (1,081 passed, 1 skipped) using a fresh workspace temp directory. Ruff, formatting, parser Pyright and `git diff --check` pass. Hosted Windows run [43059738788](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43059738788) passed the full Python suite, Edge browser checks, Hermes inventories, static checks, desktop browser checks and recovery gates; installer validation was skipped because packaging inputs were unchanged. Sources: [Open XML horizontal merge](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/documentformat.openxml.wordprocessing.horizontalmerge?view=openxml-3.0.1), [vertical merge](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/documentformat.openxml.wordprocessing.verticalmerge?view=openxml-3.0.1), and [Word's orphan-continue behavior](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/openspecs/office_standards/ms-oi29500/70e3977f-3686-456a-896f-b98b9a3be08e).

## Apply DOCX row-level table-look exceptions (2026-11-11)

DOCX table-style numbering now resolves a row's `tblPrEx/tblLook` independently, allowing row exceptions to enable or disable first/last row and column formatting and banding without changing other rows. A `tblPrEx` containing unrelated properties such as borders leaves the table-level conditional look in force. Duplicate row exceptions and duplicate look settings fail closed; the existing bounded flag and bitmask validation applies to row looks. The focused document suite passes (57 tests), and the full local Python suite passes (1,079 passed, 1 skipped) using a fresh workspace temp directory. Ruff, formatting, parser Pyright and `git diff --check` pass. Hosted Windows run [43057516822](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43057516822) passed the full Python suite, Edge browser checks, Hermes inventories, static checks, desktop browser checks and recovery gates; installer validation was skipped because packaging inputs were unchanged. This continues structured extraction, not rendered Word layout. Source: [Microsoft Open XML `tblPrEx` and `tblLook` documentation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/documentformat.openxml.wordprocessing.tablepropertyexceptions?view=openxml-3.0.1).

## Resolve DOCX table row and cell conditional flags (2026-11-11)

DOCX conditional table-style numbering now honors row- and cell-level `cnfStyle` masks and explicit flags. Explicit attributes take precedence over `w:val` as Word specifies; malformed masks, duplicate markers, and invalid on/off values fail closed. Table-level row property exceptions (`tblPrEx`) remain unsupported and fail closed when they could affect conditional numbering. Two focused regressions cover row overrides and explicit flag precedence. The focused document suite passes (54 tests); the full local Python suite passes (1,076 passed, 1 skipped) using a fresh workspace temp directory. Ruff, formatting, parser Pyright and `git diff --check` pass. Hosted Windows run [43053793536](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43053793536) passed on retry 2, including the full Python suite, Edge checks, Hermes inventories, static checks, desktop browser checks and recovery gates; installer validation was skipped because packaging inputs were unchanged. This remains structured text extraction rather than full Word rendering. Source: [Microsoft's `cnfStyle` implementation note](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/openspecs/office_standards/ms-oi29500/ae9b02e2-eea5-4af1-825a-aaae9e79496c).

## Apply conditional DOCX table-style numbering (2026-11-11)

DOCX extraction now activates table-style paragraph numbering for whole tables, first/last rows and columns, odd/even row and column bands, and corner cells. It reads explicit `tblLook` flags and the legacy bitmask, uses Word's documented default look when the element is absent, accounts for grid spans and row grid offsets, and honors configured row/column band sizes. Table style `basedOn` chains remain bounded and support property overrides. Numbering that depends on row/cell conditional-style masks or table-level exceptions fails closed because those placement exceptions are not yet resolved. The focused document suite passes (52 tests); Ruff, formatting, parser Pyright and `git diff --check` pass. Hosted Windows source run [43051644493](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43051644493) passed the full source workflow, including the Python suite, browser checks, inventories and recovery diagnostics; installer validation was skipped because packaging inputs were unchanged. No live model inference was used. Conditional precedence is applied in the order documented by [Microsoft Open Specifications](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/openspecs/office_standards/ms-oi29500/2ac331d4-cf1e-4fa0-8bca-6da74411e284), with table-look settings from [Open XML table-look documentation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/documentformat.openxml.wordprocessing.tablelook?view=openxml-3.0.1).

## Inherit DOCX numbering from whole-table styles (2026-11-11)

The initial DOCX table-style slice implemented only `wholeTable` numbering. It passed 48 focused tests and hosted source run [43048707444](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43048707444). ## Follow DOCX numbering-style links (2026-11-11)

DOCX list extraction now resolves `w:numStyleLink` from an abstract numbering definition through numbering-style `w:pPr/w:numPr` to the referenced concrete list. Resolution tracks both abstract numbering and concrete numbering instances, detects cycles, and applies per-instance level overrides after resolving the linked definition. A bounded cycle and valid linked-style fixture are covered. The focused document suite passes (45 tests); Ruff, formatting, parser Pyright, and `git diff --check` pass. Hosted Windows source run [43045333987](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43045333987) passed with 1,068 Python tests and the full desktop/recovery workflow; installer validation was skipped because packaging inputs were unchanged. Numbering-style `w:styleLink` metadata is validated, while table-style numbering and full visual Word layout remain open. No live model inference was used.

## Rework the desktop workspace layout (2026-11-11)

The desktop now opens to a focused Home workspace with a native-style sidebar for Home, Runs, Integrations, and Settings. The objective form leads with workspace and goal; advanced permissions, attachments, and resource limits are collapsed until needed. Model/setup and tool panels remain available under Integrations, saved runs and live execution under Runs, and application behavior under Settings. This is a presentation change; existing APIs and execution behavior are unchanged. The onboarding, task review/conflict, voice input, and blocked-run startup recovery browser acceptances pass (8 tests); `npm run build:desktop` passed TypeScript and the Vite production bundle; `cargo build --manifest-path apps/desktop/src-tauri/Cargo.toml --features custom-protocol` produced the native Windows executable. The rebuilt executable is at `apps/desktop/src-tauri/target/debug/xaa-desktop.exe`. Hosted installer coverage still predates this UI redesign. No model inference was used.

## Inherit DOCX numbering from paragraph styles (2026-11-11)

DOCX extraction now reads bounded `word/styles.xml`, resolves paragraph styles through `basedOn` chains up to 256 levels, and inherits their numbering instance. Paragraph-level `numPr` fields override the style, and direct `numId=0` disables inherited numbering. Style-level `ilvl` is validated but ignored as required by [the Open XML `numPr` rule](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/openspecs/office_standards/ms-oi29500/6a8f14ff-df90-4154-9bc9-e0e88ae5e641); a direct paragraph `ilvl` selects the level. Paragraph styles inherit through same-type [`basedOn` references](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/documentformat.openxml.wordprocessing.basedon?view=openxml-3.0.1), while a reference to a different style type is ignored. Duplicate definitions, malformed metadata, missing bases, cycles, external entity declarations and over-limit XML are rejected. The focused document suite passes (43 tests); Ruff, formatting, parser Pyright and `git diff --check` pass. Hosted Windows run [43037966574](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43037966574) passed on `06f09d6`, including 1,066 Python tests, the full browser/desktop/static checks, Hermes inventories and recovery diagnostics. Numbering-style links, numbering inherited from table styles, complete style resolution, and visual layout remain out of scope. No live inference was used.

## Calculate DOCX numbered list labels (2026-11-11)

DOCX extraction now computes rendered list labels for `decimal`, `lowerLetter`, `upperLetter`, `lowerRoman`, and `upperRoman` formats. It honors level start values, per-instance `startOverride`, and `lvlRestart` behavior as defined by [Open XML start overrides](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/documentformat.openxml.wordprocessing.startoverridenumberingvalue?view=openxml-3.0.1) and [level restarts](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/documentformat.openxml.wordprocessing.levelrestart?view=openxml-3.0.1), while keeping independent counters for each numbering instance; nested label templates can include already-established parent counters. The original format/template metadata remains in output, and unsupported formats or unresolved parent counters retain the template without inventing a label. Focused document extraction passes (38 tests); Ruff, formatting, parser Pyright, and `git diff --check` pass. Hosted Windows run [43035231115](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43035231115) passed on `d3bb237`, including 1,061 Python tests, the full Edge/desktop/static checks, Hermes inventories, and process recovery. This does not reproduce every Word numbering format, paragraph-style numbering inheritance, or full layout. No live inference was used.

## Honor partial DOCX numbering overrides (2026-11-11)

DOCX numbering now applies per-instance level overrides field by field: a `numFmt` or `lvlText` override keeps the other value inherited from the abstract level. ## Preserve DOCX numbering marker formats and templates (2026-11-11)

DOCX list extraction now follows `numId` references into bounded `word/numbering.xml`, reads the effective `numFmt` and `lvlText` for each used nesting level, and honors explicit per-instance level overrides. The numbering part is included in the 4 MiB per-part and 8 MiB total XML limits. Duplicate, malformed, or dangling definitions fail closed. Extracted text records the marker format and template (for example, `decimal` with `%1.` or an explicit bullet glyph), while actual sequence values and restart behavior are not calculated. Focused document tests pass (30), Ruff, formatting and strict parser Pyright pass. No live model inference was used.

## Preserve DOCX headings and list levels (2026-11-11)

DOCX paragraph extraction now retains common `Heading…` style identifiers as explicit heading markers and preserves list nesting levels from `w:numPr`. Malformed or duplicate paragraph/list metadata is rejected. This gives the model document structure that plain paragraph text discarded; list numbering and bullet glyphs are not reconstructed because numbering definitions are not yet resolved. No live model inference was used.

## Preserve DOCX table merge metadata (2026-11-11)

DOCX table extraction now records `w:gridSpan` column spans and legacy `w:hMerge` / `w:vMerge` restart/continue states next to each cell. An omitted merge value on a present marker is represented as `continue`, matching the Open XML definitions for [grid spans](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/documentformat.openxml.wordprocessing.gridspan?view=openxml-3.0.1), [vertical merges](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/documentformat.openxml.wordprocessing.verticalmerge.val?view=openxml-3.0.1), and [horizontal merges](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/documentformat.openxml.wordprocessing.horizontalmerge.val?view=openxml-2.10.0). Invalid/duplicate property markers and malformed spans or merge values fail closed. The focused document suite passes (22 tests), Ruff and formatting pass, and Pyright reports zero issues. Hosted Windows source run [43024839713](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43024839713) passed commit `c40db49` with 1,045 Python tests and browser/desktop plus specialist, Telegram, scheduled-outbox and due-schedule recovery diagnostics; installer validation was skipped because packaging inputs were unchanged. Merge group geometry and visual rendering are not reconstructed; no live model inference was used.

## Extract linked DOCX headers and footers (2026-11-11)

DOCX extraction now follows only header/footer parts referenced by the main document, preserves their first/even/default labels and reference order, and includes their body text alongside document text. Internal relationship targets are resolved relative to the source part, consistent with [Open Packaging Conventions relationship semantics](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/system.io.packaging.packagerelationship.targetmode?view=net-11.0-pp), and must remain within the DOCX package; external targets, malformed relationships, encrypted or invalid parts, duplicate/missing parts, more than 50 distinct referenced parts, per-part XML over 4 MiB, and total parsed XML over 8 MiB are rejected. Header/footer content is extracted once per part and still passes through the common 48,000-character cap. The focused document suite passes (19 tests), Ruff and formatting pass, and Pyright reports zero issues. Regression cases cover default/first/even labels and reject literal/encoded traversal and external targets. Hosted Windows source run [43022991183](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43022991183) passed commit `01474ba` with 1,042 Python tests and specialist, Telegram, scheduled-outbox and due-schedule recovery diagnostics; the installer job was skipped because packaging inputs were unchanged. These checks establish bounded package parsing, not fidelity of Word layout or section inheritance; merged cells, visual formatting, and headers/footers inherited without an explicit reference remain unverified. No live model inference was used.

The existing XML and character bounds still apply. The focused document-input suite passes (14 tests), Ruff and formatting pass, and strict Pyright reports no issues for the parser. When a saved Telegram identity is unlinked, the Automations editor now labels it as unavailable and disables Save until the owner selects a currently linked recipient or clears the stale one. ## Show the actual recipient on scheduled Telegram executions (2026-11-11)

Each saved schedule now displays its result and separate failure-notification account. The API response and generated OpenAPI types expose that destination. ## Correct the Automations browser fixture and verify failure routing controls (2026-11-11)

The first Automations browser runs failed because their mock omitted `GET /runs/latest-unfinished`; its catch-all returned `{status:"ok"}`, which the desktop treated as a saved run and passed to `PlanPanel`. The real endpoint returns `null` when no unfinished run exists. The fixture now models that response, and the Playwright Automations workflow passes (1), including selection and submission of both result and separate failure destinations. This resolves the browser-test fixture defect; no product `PlanPanel` defect was found.

## Verify local full-suite temp isolation and retry hosted browser acceptance (2026-11-11)

Running the unit suite with the default Windows temp root yielded 963 passed and 50 fixture-setup errors because access to `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Temp\pytest-of-hicks` was denied. Rerunning all unit tests with a unique workspace-local pytest basetemp passed (1,013 passed, 1 skipped); no code or test was skipped to obtain that result. The focused hosted specialist-review UI check failed once at its final callback assertion, but all three `task-review.spec.ts` cases passed locally. Hosted run [42984450097, attempt 2](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42984450097) passed the full source workflow, including 1,033 Python tests and specialist, Telegram, scheduled-outbox, and schedule-restart diagnostics. This was an environmental temp-path issue and a transient browser acceptance failure; both reruns passed.

## Route scheduled failure notices to a separate Telegram account (2026-11-11)

Schedules can now select a result recipient and a separate failure-notification recipient. The API validates both against currently linked users on the configured bot; schema 28 preserves the new schedule destination and per-occurrence routing data while migrating legacy deliveries to their original result recipient. Successful runs use the result destination; terminal failures use the explicit failure destination, falling back to the result destination when none is selected. A failure-only schedule records `not_required` after success and sends no result message. Desktop controls, OpenAPI and generated types are updated. Deterministic schedule, Telegram and migration suites pass (61 tests); Ruff, strict Pyright, desktop TypeScript and generated-contract checks pass. Hosted Windows run [42981771398, attempt 2](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42981771398) passed 1,032 Python tests and recovery diagnostics. Attempt 1 stopped on a 45-second managed-Edge subresource timeout; the rerun passed. The follow-up result-history commit passed hosted run [42984450097, attempt 2](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42984450097), including 1,033 Python tests and all recovery diagnostics. No live Telegram send or model inference was used. Real bot delivery and other channels remain open; this closes only recipient selection/routing for the existing Telegram adapter.

## Verify installed MCP package activation path (2026-11-11)

Added deterministic end-to-end lifecycle coverage at both engine and desktop levels. The 16 MCP stdio integration tests install a bounded Agent Plugin ZIP, discover/import its MCP entry as disconnected, confirm uninstall is blocked while referenced, explicitly connect the real package-backed stdio server, verify default-deny, grant read access, call its tool, disconnect and remove the package. The rebuilt native Windows desktop acceptance additionally installs a real ZIP through the authenticated engine, lists it in the native UI, imports its MCP entry without connecting it, shows an actionable 409 when removal is attempted while referenced, then removes the integration and package. Evidence: [`.acceptance/3eb20734-82d0-4330-8b6b-95cc575967dc/report.json`](../.acceptance/3eb20734-82d0-4330-8b6b-95cc575967dc/report.json). The native build, TypeScript checks, and local acceptance pass; no model inference was used. The actual Windows file-picker interaction and broad third-party compatibility remain open.

## Audit Hermes trajectory compression source (2026-11-11)

The pinned Hermes README's trajectory-compression claim is implemented by the repository-root `trajectory_compressor.py` and `scripts/sample_and_compress.py`, beyond the basic `agent/trajectory.py` writer. At commit `1e208b280deb18f01d015b0acdf3ef3552d08039`, the compressor counts tokens, protects configured head/tail turns, summarizes a bounded middle region, avoids splitting tool-call/response boundaries, and records compression metrics; the helper samples datasets and feeds the compressor. The compressor defaults to a Transformers tokenizer with `trust_remote_code=True` and a separately configured summarizer provider, so reproducing it in X.A.A. needs explicit provider/data consent and bounded inference accounting. X.A.A.'s review-gated text-only export is a different capability and does not close this parity item. Updated the Hermes matrix accordingly. This source audit does not run upstream code or model inference.

## Bind batch trajectories to their requested run (2026-11-11)

Batch execution now validates each downloaded trajectory envelope before persistence or scoring and rejects it unless its embedded run ID matches the durable run ID saved for that case. This prevents scoring or retaining a different run’s data as the requested case. Batch resume also adopts the exported state so a concurrent owner review between status polling and trajectory download is reflected in the checkpoint. The new mismatched-run regression and the complete batch suite pass (11 tests); Ruff, formatting, strict Pyright and `git diff --check` pass. No live model inference was used. This closes one provenance check only; broader trajectory redaction review and replay remain open.

## Keep manual acceptance runs from being cancelled (2026-11-11)

Hosted source acceptance takes about 7–9 minutes and installer acceptance about 9 minutes. The prior workflow used one ref-only concurrency group with `cancel-in-progress: true`, allowing a newer push to cancel a user-dispatched installer acceptance. CI concurrency is now event-aware: normal push/pull-request runs still replace older runs of the same event/ref, while manually dispatched validation has a separate group and is allowed to finish. Workflow YAML and assertions for the concurrency expressions pass, as does `git diff --check`. A hosted follow-up will verify the configuration on GitHub Actions. No application behavior or live model testing changed.

## Re-run hosted Windows installer acceptance on current branch (2026-11-11)

Manual Windows workflow [42962317968](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42962317968) on `7461bc3` passed the unsigned installer build, release-notice package coverage, installed MCP OAuth/credential clearing, native desktop and specialist restart recovery, frozen API/task recovery, browser cancellation, task scope checks, upgrade/install/boot/uninstall, standard-user install, and explicit data removal. The source job was skipped by the manual-dispatch workflow condition; this is packaged Windows acceptance only. The 77 MB installer artifact and reports are retained locally in `.acceptance/hosted-installer-42962317968/`; artifact expires 2027-02-09. ## Keep MCP sampling inside coordinator controls (2026-11-10)

Reviewed the installed MCP SDK 2.3.0 callback surface and X.A.A.'s connection/coordinator boundary. Directly connecting `sampling_callback` to the provider would allow an MCP server to initiate inference without a durable run/task event, shared token reservation, or the saved provider/model route. Sampling therefore remains unavailable until it can be authorized for a specific run, journaled with bounded request content and recovery behavior, admitted through the existing inference budget, and constrained to text-only responses without tool grants. This is a dependency finding for Phase 9, not an implementation claim; no model inference or test run was performed.

## Extend the hosted installer upgrade baseline (2026-11-11)

The public repository permits up to 90 days of GitHub Actions artifact retention. The unsigned Windows installer artifact now uses that maximum instead of 14 days, reducing the chance that the current upgrade fixture expires before follow-up release checks. Hosted installer run [42960402173](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42960402173) passed the full package build, frozen recovery and workspace create/edit/delete checks, schema-18 upgrade, install/boot/uninstall, standard-user install and explicit data removal. GitHub reports the artifact expires 2027-02-09; the installer and reports are preserved in `.acceptance/hosted-installer-42960402173/`. This extends the temporary CI baseline only; a durable published predecessor is still needed. No live inference was used.

## Complete read-before-edit frozen acceptance fixtures (2026-11-11)

The crash-recovery edit diagnostic now saves an exact-path read before simulating an interrupted write, and gives fresh-process owner recovery 45 seconds to finish on hosted Windows. The private command snapshot diagnostic now reads each task-private file before its branch write. Local frozen checks pass for both. Hosted installer run [42957769429](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42957769429) on `060f57d` passed the unsigned Windows package build, native desktop and frozen API/specialist recovery, owner-reviewed frozen create/edit/delete recovery, ACP/snapshot/browser/task-scope acceptance, schema-18 upgrade from 0.1.0, install/boot/uninstall, standard-user installation, and explicit data removal. Reports and the installer/manifest are preserved under `.acceptance/hosted-installer-42957769429/`; the frozen edit report is `workspace-edit-owner-42957769429/report.json`. Its matching source workflow rerun [42957764163](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42957764163) at the same source commit also passed: 1,025 Python tests, browser and desktop acceptance, Ruff, formatting, Pyright, API/TypeScript contracts, Hermes inventory, and process-recovery diagnostics. Source reports are preserved in `.acceptance/hosted-source-42957764163/`. The packaged build is unsigned and no live LM Studio inference was performed. Signing, broader clean-machine coverage, durable published upgrade baselines, third-party legal review, and final live model acceptance remain open.

## Require same-run reads before file edits (2026-11-10)

Root workspace edits now require a durable, completed, untruncated `workspace.read_file` for the same path and SHA-256 earlier in the same run, before the write intent. Specialist private branch writes and deletions enforce the matching task-scoped prerequisite. Replacement and patch operations already record their own reads. Regressions cover direct-write rejection without mutation, specialist branch isolation, stale versions, correction after pre-write failure, and interrupted-write recovery. All 37 cases across the focused write, specialist workspace, tool-failure, and write-recovery suites pass, as does the 19-test command suite; Ruff, formatting, strict Pyright, and `git diff --check` pass. Initial hosted run [42951078240](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42951078240) exposed nine command fixtures that edited without reading first (1,016 other tests passed). The fixtures now follow the actual tool contract. Hosted rerun [42952077241](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42952077241) passed the full Windows source workflow, including full Python tests, desktop checks, Hermes inventory, and process-recovery acceptance; installer validation was skipped for unchanged packaging inputs. This proves the application’s recorded read-before-edit flow, not immunity to outside changes between read and mutation; compare-and-swap remains in place. No live inference was used.

## Serialize managed plugin removal with MCP registration (2026-11-10)

Package and retained-data removal now hold SQLite's cross-process `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` write lock from reference inspection through filesystem deletion. MCP server registration takes the same lock and rechecks the package source digest before recording its reference. A controlled concurrent API regression pauses removal while a registration races; removal completes, registration then rejects the missing package, and no dangling MCP server is stored. The focused plugin/MCP suites pass (49 passed, 1 skipped); Ruff, formatting, Pyright and `git diff --check` pass. Hosted run [42947450254](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42947450254) found one legacy migration test creating an empty package with a fabricated source digest, which the new registration guard correctly rejected. The fixture now contains a source file and uses its actual digest. Hosted rerun [42948524334](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42948524334) passed the full Windows source workflow with 1,024 Python tests and recovery diagnostics; installer validation was skipped because packaging inputs were unchanged. This serializes these managed API operations across processes sharing the database; manual out-of-band filesystem changes still rely on the existing per-call source-digest checks. No model inference was used.

## Make competing procedure edits race deterministically (2026-11-10)

Strengthened the immutable-procedure concurrent-edit regression with a two-writer barrier so both revision requests begin from expected version 1 at the same time. Exactly one transaction creates version 2; the losing stale writer receives a conflict, version 1 remains immutable, and no duplicate revision is created. The focused backup/edit/delete test passes; Ruff, formatting and `git diff --check` pass. Hosted Windows run [42945715044](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42945715044) passed the full source workflow with 1,023 Python tests and process-recovery diagnostics; installer validation was skipped because the change did not affect packaging inputs. This verifies the optimistic version guard under concurrent edits for this storage path; it does not settle broader package mutation locks, background-review policy or read-before-write protections. No model inference was used.

## Wire optional signing into hosted installer builds (2026-11-10)

Added `scripts/build_installer_ci.py` to accept an optional base64 PFX from repository secrets, validate and materialize it only in a runner temporary directory, and pass its path/password to the existing builder via child environment. With no certificate it clears stale signing variables and creates the same unsigned build. The Windows installer workflow now passes optional signing secrets and configuration, and labels uploaded artifacts according to the builder's successful signing mode; the historical unsigned artifact download remains unchanged. Local wrapper/builder tests pass (12), Ruff, formatting, strict Pyright and diff checks pass. Hosted run [42943261906](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42943261906) passed the full source workflow, including full Python tests and recovery diagnostics; the installer job was skipped because packaging inputs were unchanged on this branch. No repository signing secrets are configured, so hosted secret-backed installer execution and trusted-chain signing remain unverified and current artifacts stay unsigned. Broader release acceptance gates remain open. Live model testing remains deferred to final acceptance.

Manual hosted installer run [42944294840](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42944294840) then exercised the wrapper's no-secret path: the Windows installer built unsigned, all native/frozen recovery checks passed, and install/boot/uninstall, standard-user install, and explicit data-removal acceptance passed. GitHub uploaded `xaa-windows-installer-unsigned`, confirming mode labeling. No production signing certificate was configured; the signed path and trusted-chain verification remain open. This run validates the unsigned CI integration only, not an unrelated clean-user-machine survey.

## Require explicit package attributions in the release coverage gate (2026-11-10)

The installer’s release-notice verifier no longer counts any `name@version` occurrence anywhere in the document, which could let license text or SPDX metadata stand in for attribution. It now reads only explicit package groupings and verified source-file records; the stricter first run exposed 17 locked Python packages whose existing source attribution was outside the old parser’s accepted structure. The PDFium wheel is now explicitly named in its bundled-license section. The full lock coverage command passes for Python 86, npm 5, and Windows Cargo 247 packages. Two parser regressions cover prose false positives, alternate-license notes, scoped npm names, and verified source records; Ruff, format, Pyright, and `git diff --check` pass. This validates explicit package references, not license text correctness, copyright completeness, compatibility, or legal approval. No live inference was used.

## Protect pinned procedures from deletion (2026-11-10)

Added owner-controlled pin/unpin state for reusable procedures. The authenticated `PUT /skills/{id}/pin` API reports the pin in `SkillSummary`; `DELETE /skills/{id}` checks it inside `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, so a racing pin/delete cannot remove a protected procedure. The desktop offers pin/unpin controls and disables deletion until the owner unpins. Schema 27 adds a cascade-bound pin table, with schema-26 migration coverage for preserving existing entries as unpinned. Storage, API, skill and schema migration suites pass (47 tests); the focused Playwright procedure editor flow passes (1), desktop TypeScript, generated API contracts, Ruff, formatting and strict Pyright pass. Hosted Windows run [42935559004](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42935559004) passed the complete source workflow, including the full Python suite, desktop/browser acceptances and recovery diagnostics; installer validation was skipped because packaging inputs did not change. No live model inference was used. This closes the pinned-delete behavior only; mutation locks and background-review protections remain open.

## Add Deno project validation suggestions (2026-11-10)

The bounded validation manifest scan now recognizes `deno.json` and `deno.jsonc` only when they explicitly declare a non-empty `tasks.test` string or command. JSONC comments and trailing commas are supported; malformed files, empty or missing test tasks, and directories containing both config formats are skipped. The suggestion uses a fixed `deno task --config <file> test` argv through the existing restricted project-validation path, leaving the declared task body to Deno. The Deno task and config behavior follows the [official Deno documentation](https://docs.deno.com/runtime/reference/cli/task/). The focused suite passes (14 tests), Ruff, formatting and Pyright pass. The monorepo regression now covers 12 distinct supported manifests. Hosted Windows run [42931858885](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42931858885) passed the full source workflow with 1,010 Python tests, static checks, desktop browser checks and process-recovery diagnostics; installer validation was skipped because packaging inputs did not change.
## Phase 2 objective gate demonstrated on tested restart boundaries (2026-11-10)

The Phase 2 acceptance criterion is demonstrated across recorded process-death scenarios. A frozen API and worker were killed after a workspace write was fsynced but before its journal completion; after restart, authenticated reconciliation recorded one completed execution and continued to `verifying` without replaying the write (`.acceptance/frozen-api-restart-7d40bcc0-b8f4-41b7-a056-09f769aa0348/report.json`). A separate Tauri plus bundled-engine process-death acceptance restarted from the same profile/database, restored one candidate and verified zero tool effects (`.acceptance/native-restart-275d3233-73fe-46ef-ae15-8fd7cdef7c4c/report.json`; hosted Windows run [42399357940](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42399357940)). These demonstrate the stated restart gate on tested process boundaries. Whole-machine reboot/power-loss, broader external-effect reconciliation and complete recovery remain open; no reboot claim is made.
## Phase 4 objective gate demonstrated (2026-11-10)

The stated Phase 4 gate is demonstrated on the recorded fixture scenarios: two real-model specialists completed independent private edits and restricted Ubuntu validation; the native Tauri review refused a conflicting integration without an explicit choice, then integrated only the selected proposal after hash-backed preview and explicit authorization. Both tasks completed, the source stayed unchanged until review, and no calls remained unresolved (`.acceptance/294a45bc-157b-45c1-b76f-b8644715e5f8/`, `.acceptance/live-task-workspaces-wsl-exact-1024-20261107/report.json`). This demonstrates the objective's Phase 4 gate on tested scenarios; it does not establish broad model reliability, autonomous reviewer judgment, exact accounting for every provider, or full Hermes parity. No live LLM testing was run for this continuation.
## Merge explicitly linked channel identity groups (2026-11-10)

Added an owner-confirmed API and desktop flow to merge two existing identity groups into the selected target group's session. All members must still be authorized and the merged groups must have distinct channels. The source link is revoked, its identities continue in the target session, and its prior session/messages remain in local history without being folded into the target transcript. The transaction emits audit events for both link records; a schema migration expands the event constraint while preserving older records. Updated the OpenAPI contract, desktop types, identity-linking guide and Hermes matrix. Focused identity/API tests pass (50 total), the channels Playwright workflow passes, TypeScript and generated-contract checks pass, and the production desktop bundle builds. The first hosted attempt stopped at repo-wide lint because the release-notice coverage helper had import/typing/format issues; these were corrected and the full Ruff, formatting, Pyright, contract, desktop and recovery checks passed on the rerun. Hosted Windows run [42929632129](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42929632129) passed all source checks and 1,009 Python tests; installer validation was skipped because packaging inputs did not change. The notice coverage script verifies 86 Python, 5 npm and 247 Windows Cargo package references. No live model inference was used; this remains a partial channel feature and makes no live third-party interoperability claim. X.A.A.'s MIT source license is already recorded in `LICENSE` and package/release metadata per the owner's choice.

## Report specialist wall-clock exhaustion as a blocked budget outcome (2026-11-10)

`SpecialistRunner.run_claim` now distinguishes its own task/run deadline expiration from a provider-raised timeout. Expired time budgets persist as blocked outcomes with separate `task_time_budget_exhausted` and `run_time_budget_exhausted` reasons; the plan view explains each case. Provider timeouts continue to be classified as inference failures. A follow-up fixes the coordinator's specialist-only continuation: expiration of its parent run deadline now transitions the run to blocked with `time_budget_exhausted`, while a provider-raised `TimeoutError` remains a failure. Regression tests cover cancellation, task and run budget outcomes, coordinator deadline expiry, and provider timeout classification. Focused specialist/API suites pass (35 tests); Ruff, formatting and Pyright pass. Hosted Windows run [42909339459](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42909339459) passed all checks, including 1,003 Python tests, desktop/browser acceptances, and specialist, Telegram and schedule recovery diagnostics. Installer validation was skipped because packaging inputs did not change. The earlier run [42908522716](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42908522716) was cancelled when the follow-up push superseded it. No live model inference was used.
## Remove the stateless inference path outside the coordinator (2026-11-10)

Removed authenticated `POST /chat/stream`, which called `provider.stream` directly without a durable run, saved usage, run budgets, or coordinator lifecycle. No desktop caller used the route. Interactive chat remains available through the persisted CLI/desktop sessions and coordinator; callers of the legacy raw API path now receive 404 and must use durable runs. Regenerated `contracts/openapi.json` and desktop API types. The API suite passes (37 tests), API and TypeScript contract checks, desktop typecheck, Ruff, formatting, targeted Pyright, and `git diff --check` pass. No live model inference was used.
## Bind specialist token holds to their task inference (2026-11-10)

`RunStore.reserve_tokens` now matches a specialist reservation to the exact durable `task.inference.started` event and task ID. It rejects attempts to reserve a specialist inference as a parent call, to attach it under another task's live lease, or to attach a parent inference to a child task. The regression proves these mismatches fail and that the owning task can still reserve its allowance; the concurrent parent-cap regression now exercises two claimed specialists through `TaskStore.reserve_tokens`. The claim and diagnostic-usage suites pass (25 tests); the expanded claim, diagnostic-usage, specialist, and coordinator suites pass (56 tests). Ruff, format, targeted Pyright, and `git diff --check` pass. Hosted Windows run [42903686498](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42903686498), attempt 2, passed the full source workflow and recovery diagnostics; installer validation was skipped because packaging inputs were unchanged. Attempt 1 failed before the Python suite when an external redirect smoke timed out waiting for final-page content; the retry passed that check. No live model inference was used.

## Prove concurrent specialist holds stay within the shared token ceiling (2026-11-10)

Added a two-thread storage regression that races two independent 60-token reservations against a 100-token run cap. The immediate SQLite transaction admits exactly one hold; the other raises `TokenBudgetExceeded`, and the durable usage report retains one 60-token reservation. `uv run pytest tests/unit/test_diagnostic_usage.py -q` passes (10 tests); Ruff lint, format check, and `git diff --check` pass. Hosted Windows source run [42901433641](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42901433641) passed the full source workflow and recovery diagnostics; installer validation was skipped because packaging inputs were unchanged. This verifies the atomic parent-run cap for this race; it does not establish tokenizer accuracy, external-client accounting, or full Phase 4 acceptance. No live model inference was used.
## Verify PyInstaller license notice source text (2026-11-10)

Removed generated `Additional notice: UNKNOWN` placeholders from the PyInstaller and community-hooks notice blocks. The remaining fenced texts match the pinned installed package files (`pyinstaller==6.22.3` `COPYING.txt`; `pyinstaller-hooks-contrib==2026.9` `LICENSE`) after normalizing line endings, markdown fence boundaries, and whitespace-only line differences. The inventory now records PyInstaller's metadata-declared GPLv2-or-later exception and keeps package-specific copyright and complete installer-notice review open. `git diff --check` passes. No live model inference was used.

## Correct the X.A.A. package license in release inventory (2026-11-10)

The development-environment `pip-licenses` scan reports the project's own `xaa-engine==0.1.1` distribution as `UNKNOWN`, although `pyproject.toml` declares MIT and the root `LICENSE` contains the selected source license. The release inventory now classifies this row from authoritative project metadata and identifies the project repository. This removes a false unknown for X.A.A. itself; third-party license/copyright review and the provisional notice bundle remain open. Documentation consistency and `git diff --check` pass.

## Preserve all source reads in reviewer evidence discovery (2026-11-10)

The normal candidate summary intentionally shows at most 16 source reads, but reviewer discovery had reused that summary and silently omitted older evidence. Reviewer gating and its paged manifest now enumerate every completed, hash-verified source read while omitting excerpts; the desktop and ordinary candidate summary remain capped at 16. The submission gate now requires the reviewer to read the same path and SHA-256 recorded by the dependency; reading a changed version of that path does not satisfy the evidence requirement. The deterministic 17-file regression confirms early evidence remains in the manifest, all matching reviewer reads pass, and a mismatched hash stays missing. The task workspace and specialist suites pass (41 tests); Ruff, formatting, strict Pyright and `git diff --check` pass. Hosted Windows run [42897738572](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42897738572) passed the full source workflow, including the Python suite, Hermes inventories and recovery diagnostics; installer validation was skipped because packaging inputs were unchanged. No live model inference was used. Broader Phase 4 reliability acceptance remains open.

## Avoid rebuilding private diffs for reviewer evidence paging (2026-11-10)

Reviewer dependency-manifest paging and evidence-gate checks now request change metadata without generating full unified diffs. This avoids repeated diff allocation for every page while preserving full diffs for the owner review UI and normal candidate evidence. The task workspace and specialist suites pass (40 tests); Ruff, formatting, strict Pyright and `git diff --check` pass. Hosted Windows run [42891572306](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42891572306) passed the full source workflow, including the Python suite and recovery diagnostics; installer validation was skipped because packaging inputs were unchanged. This bounds one repeated allocation in reviewer inventory; broader Phase 4 resource/reliability coverage remains open. No live model inference was used.

## Keep reviewer evidence discoverable beyond the prompt budget (2026-11-10)

Reviewer prompts always carry saved dependency IDs, even when dependency summaries exceed the 8 KiB initial-context budget. A reviewer-only `review_dependency_manifest` lists source-file evidence, private changes and validation-output IDs in pages of up to eight items, dynamically kept under 64 KiB using worst-case JSON escaping. Candidate submission requires complete manifest paging for each dependency with evidence, plus direct reading of source files, changed artifacts and validation outputs through their existing tools. A deterministic 30-item pagination test covers worst-case Unicode expansion; the fake-reviewer integration verifies discovery, full reads and the gate. The specialist, workspace and task-command suites passed (59); Ruff, formatting and strict Pyright pass. Hosted Windows run [42888770822](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42888770822) passed the complete source workflow, including the full Python suite and recovery diagnostics. Installer checks were skipped because packaging inputs did not change. Broader orchestration reliability and the Phase 4 gate remain open. No live LLM inference was used.

## Deliver assigned image artifacts to vision-capable specialists (2026-11-10)

Specialist plans can now assign up to eight same-run unowned artifacts consisting of UTF-8 text and PNG/JPEG images. Images are integrity-checked from managed storage and attached only to the assigned specialist's initial model context, with 3 MiB per-image and 6 MiB total limits. The selected route must advertise vision capability; unsupported routes fail the task before inference with a stable `vision_capability_missing` code. Image assignments are rejected when exact text-token admission is enabled because image-token cost cannot be counted by that path. Text artifacts retain the lease-checked 16 KiB reader and EOF submission gate. Tests cover vision-capable delivery, unsupported-model refusal, exact-count refusal and text EOF enforcement; the focused planning, task graph, artifact and specialist suites pass (34). Ruff, formatting and strict Pyright pass. No live LLM inference was used. Hosted Windows run [42883940346](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42883940346) passed the complete source workflow, including full Python tests and specialist recovery; installer checks were skipped because packaging inputs were unchanged. Broader multimodal inputs (including direct PDF attachments beyond supported rendered PNG page artifacts) and the Phase 4 orchestration gate remain open.

## Declare and deliver specialist task inputs and outputs (2026-11-10)

Task plans now declare up to eight existing same-run artifact IDs as inputs and require at least one expected-output description per proposed task. Text inputs are exposed through a specialist-only paged reader that checks the live task lease, assignment, path containment and stored SHA-256; candidate submission requires all assigned text to have been read through EOF. The image follow-up above adds assigned PNG/JPEG delivery with model capability and byte-limit checks. The saved declarations survive schema-26 migration, appear in the plan API and desktop review, and are included in specialist instructions. The focused task graph, model planning, migration, artifact reader, inference-budget and specialist suites pass (56 tests); the full deterministic Python suite passes (992 passed, 1 skipped) with an isolated workspace temp directory. Ruff, formatting, strict Pyright, desktop TypeScript, generated API contract and diff checks pass. After updating browser mocks to include the new task fields, the task-review browser scenarios pass locally (3); hosted Windows run [42880361091](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42880361091) passed the Edge, desktop, Python and recovery checks. The installer job was skipped because packaging inputs did not change. No live LLM inference was used. Broader multimodal inputs and orchestration acceptance remain open. Text extracted from supported PDF/DOCX/XLSX attachments can be assigned as a text artifact; direct scanned-page assignment is supported only through existing PNG page artifacts. The Phase 4 gate remains open.

## Contract model-authored procedure changes (2026-11-10)

Procedure draft, content revision, linked-resource revision and removal calls now have shared versioned contracts for input/output schemas, explicit run grants, 10-second SQLite lock bounds, candidate-only effects, retries and audit fields. Coordinator schemas come from those contracts and bind revisions to the selected procedure/version; linked-resource removal fields appear only with the separate removal grant. Each transaction checks its durable receipt against a 32 KiB serialized cap before committing the candidate and tool result together. Focused procedure draft/revision/trial/contract tests pass (50); Ruff, formatting, strict Pyright and diff checks pass. No model inference was used.

## Keep specialist web-tool schemas aligned (2026-11-10)

Delegated specialists exposed search, fetch, and browser inputs from static tool
constants, while the coordinator used the bounded contract metadata. Specialist
calls now source each input schema from the same metadata as coordinator calls.

## Validate MCP structured outputs (2026-11-10)

MCP tool discovery already pinned server output schemas with each tool identity,
but calls did not check structured results against those schemas and output
schemas had no discovery size check. MCP now caps output schemas at 128 KiB and
validates any structured result when a schema is declared. Invalid read output
is reported as invalid; invalid write output remains an unknown outcome and
requires reconciliation. The existing 256 KiB result bound remains enforced.
The full MCP stdio integration file passes (13 passed); Ruff, formatting, strict Pyright and diff checks pass. Hosted Windows source run [42859332060](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42859332060) passed; installer checks were skipped for this source-only change. No LLM inference was used.

## Bound selected procedure reads (2026-11-10)

Selected procedure text and linked-resource reads now use shared input/output contracts. The resource schema is constrained to the selected package paths. Both paths check the final serialized result against 512 KiB before journaling/delivery, returning a bounded untrusted `output_limit` result if needed. Procedure-trial and contract tests pass (17 passed); Ruff, formatting, strict Pyright and diff checks pass. The full Python suite passes locally (984 passed, 1 skipped) using an isolated pytest temp directory; the microphone capture browser acceptance passes three repeated local runs. The first attempt stopped before code checks when the live Edge subresource worker exceeded its 45-second timeout. No LLM inference was used.

## Bound procedure catalog tool results (2026-11-10)

`procedure_catalog` now uses a strict shared input schema and a versioned read contract. The runtime checks the final ASCII-escaped JSON result against 512 KiB before it is journaled or returned; overflow becomes a small untrusted `output_limit` result so the caller can request a smaller page. Contract, coordinator-schema and Unicode-overflow tests pass (3 passed); Ruff, formatting, strict Pyright and diff checks pass. Its source change is included in descendant commit `fca1640`, validated by hosted Windows run [42866134969](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42866134969), which passed on retry; the installer job was skipped for source-only changes. ## Bound managed-browser tool results (2026-11-10)

The browser worker's 1 MiB response ceiling did not by itself bound the final
serialized tool result after URL, title, content and form data were assembled.
The browser now checks the serialized result before journaling or delivery and
returns a small read failure if it exceeds the same ceiling. The output schema
declares field and nested form-option limits; the coordinator exposes the input
schema from browser metadata. Screenshots remain separate managed artifacts.
Two focused browser-contract tests pass; Ruff, formatting, strict Pyright and diff checks pass. Hosted Windows source run [42857284995](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42857284995) passed; installer checks were skipped for this source-only change. No LLM inference was used.

## Bound web-search results and align schemas (2026-11-10)

Audited the configured Brave search tool's declared 250,000-byte output cap against actual result serialization. Each of ten results may include a 500-character title, 2,048-character URL, and 1,500-character description; the actual worst-case escaped JSON can exceed the old declaration. Search now declares a 512 KiB serialized-result limit, verifies it before journaling/delivery, and replaces overflow with a bounded read failure. The output schema describes every result field and separately models success versus failure/cancellation; the coordinator exposes input schema from search metadata. Metadata and Unicode-overflow tests pass. `tests/unit/test_web_search.py`: 9 passed; Ruff, formatting, strict Pyright and diff checks pass. Hosted Windows source run [42855951331](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42855951331) passed; installer checks were skipped for this source-only change. No LLM inference was used.

## Bound serialized web-fetch results (2026-11-10)

Corrected `web.fetch`'s output contract, which had called a 20,000-character content cap a 20,000-byte total output limit despite returning title and URL fields too. The fetch path now validates every redirect URL against the 2,048-character bound, models URL/title/content/error character limits in the output schema, and caps the serialized tool result at 300 KiB before journaling or returning it. Metadata distinguishes the 512 KiB response-body cap from the result cap and declares URL, title, content, redirect, and host limits. The Unicode-heavy overflow regression verifies an oversized result becomes a small `read_failed` result; schema and metadata bounds are checked. `tests/unit/test_web_fetch.py`: 10 passed; Ruff, formatting, strict Pyright and diff checks pass. Hosted Windows source run [42855951331](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42855951331) passed; installer checks were skipped for this source-only change. No LLM inference was used.

The authenticated project-validation POST now maps a bounded execution timeout to HTTP 409 with owner guidance to refresh evidence and reconcile unresolved saved execution before retrying, matching the existing reconciliation endpoint. ## Versioned workspace tool contracts (2026-11-10)

Added a versioned registry for the coordinator's workspace list/read/search/create/patch/replace/write tools and the internal delete recovery operation. Contracts define schemas, permissions, effect class, worker timeout and cancellation, retry rules, resource limits, audit fields, and model exposure. Coordinator schema generation, permission checks, operation selection, and the worker timeout now use the registry. ## Import Agent Plugin Streamable HTTP MCP servers (2026-11-10)

The package import route now supports both stdio and Streamable HTTP entries. HTTP header `${ENV_NAME}` references are mapped to OS Vault credentials, and literal package header values are also vaulted under generated per-server keys; SQLite retains only header names and secret references. The MCP HTTP client resolves mapped values at connect time. MCP summary discovery now returns environment names for HTTP entries without exposing values. Schema 24 persists package header mappings, and the ordinary MCP create API rejects caller-supplied package paths/mappings. Local loopback HTTP integration verifies resolved Authorization and custom headers reach the server; API tests assert header secrets are absent from responses and SQLite. Focused suite: 63 passed, 1 skipped; the MCP browser workflow, Ruff, Pyright, formatting and TS/API contract checks pass. Package activation/installation, redaction of credentials if a referenced package changes after import, and broad third-party compatibility remain open. No LLM inference was used.

## Import Agent Plugin stdio MCP servers as disconnected integrations (2026-11-10)

MCP server records and schema 23 now support package/data paths; stdio launch creates the persistent data folder, supplies reserved `PLUGIN_ROOT`/`PLUGIN_DATA`, expands allowed placeholders, and resolves package/data working directories and `./` commands with containment checks. The Agent Plugins panel can add a selected stdio server as a disconnected MCP integration. The authenticated API re-reads it from a fresh validated package scan, stores non-secret configuration in SQLite and literal environment values in the Windows Vault, and omits package paths and secret values from API responses. Added migration, parameter resolution, and import/API regressions. Focused package, migration, MCP prompt, and stdio tests pass (see current session verification); Ruff, formatting, Pyright, desktop TypeScript typechecking, and diff checks pass. Package Streamable HTTP import, UI/browser end-to-end verification, installation/activation, and wider third-party compatibility remain open. No inference was used.

The plugin scanner now skips an invalid `SKILL.md` with a per-skill diagnostic while retaining valid sibling skills and independently discovered MCP servers. ## Upgrade MCP client to SDK v2 and add subscriptions/listen (2026-11-10)

Migrated the client and deterministic stdio/HTTP fixtures from MCP SDK 1.x to pinned `mcp==2.3.0`. The v2 client now negotiates supported protocol versions, runs modern `Client.listen` for explicitly subscribed resource URIs, and keeps the legacy `resources/subscribe` path for older negotiated versions. Listener events remain exact-URI filtered, bounded and marked untrusted; overflow or stream loss requests owner-driven resynchronization. OAuth now uses the v2 `httpx2` transport and result type. The focused MCP integration/unit slice passes (15 tests), the full deterministic Python suite passed earlier in this work (947 passed, 1 skipped), strict Pyright, Ruff checks, and repository-wide Ruff formatting passed, and the frozen Windows engine rebuild completed successfully. The first hosted Windows source run stopped at its formatter gate; three files were reformatted and the follow-up hosted run will recheck the branch. The rebuilt native desktop MCP acceptance also passed against real stdio and loopback Streamable HTTP OAuth fixtures, including resource subscription/notification, prompt/resource review and clean shutdown; evidence: `.acceptance/53f12c17-e61b-44ab-9dfb-0e141e03a413/`. No LLM inference was used. Updated the MCP architecture description and refreshed the locked dependency inventory and provisional notices for SDK v2 dependencies, including the platform-conditional `httpx2-jsfetch` license. Hosted Windows source run [42810648921](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42810648921) passed lint, format, Pyright, contract checks and the full Python suite (installer job skipped). Broader installed-release and third-party MCP compatibility, sampling, prompt use in model context, and full extension package lifecycle/parity remain open.


## Add OAuth authorization for HTTP MCP servers (2026-11-10)

Native Windows desktop acceptance now exercises HTTP MCP OAuth against a local PKCE-verifying server. The harness configures the server through the actual Tauri Integrations panel, launches the system browser to an automatic local authorization callback, verifies the protected tool discovery and Credential Manager token, disconnects/reconnects without a second authorization, clears sign-in, and confirms desktop/engine shutdown. A stale pre-OAuth native engine initially failed this path; rebuilding the bundle fixed it. The passing rebuilt native development app report is `.acceptance/d2a5b7eb-2f39-410f-8233-7c11e2923320/report.json`; its fixture trace confirms `code_ok=True` and `pkce_ok=True`. The smoke also retains stdio transport/resource/prompt/permission coverage and performs no model inference. Windows installer run [42800363137](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42800363137) passed the same acceptance against `build/native-release`, plus the remaining installer job checks. Third-party provider compatibility remains open.

## Restore MCP OAuth token expiry across restarts (2026-11-10)

The SDK reloads saved tokens without restoring their expiry timestamp, so a token saved before application shutdown could be presented as valid indefinitely. MCP OAuth storage now records an absolute expiry alongside the token response, and the pinned provider restores the remaining lifetime when it loads vault data. Expired tokens refresh before the next MCP request; legacy token-only profiles are treated as expired to force refresh or sign-in. The loopback OAuth fixture verifies authorization-code + PKCE, refresh after expiry, migration from the former token-only vault format, reconnect without another browser launch, and owner denial with no token saved. The combined MCP HTTP, stdio and API suite passes (48 tests), along with strict manager/API Pyright, Ruff/format, generated contract and desktop TypeScript checks. Hosted Windows source run [42795531243](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42795531243) passed the full suite, browser/API checks and recovery diagnostics; the installer job was skipped because packaging inputs were unchanged. No live provider, real user credential or model inference was used.
Streamable HTTP MCP connections now respond to OAuth challenges through the pinned MCP SDK's authorization-code + PKCE flow. The client binds an ephemeral loopback callback on `127.0.0.1` with a random path, opens the owner’s browser, and saves dynamic client registration metadata and access/refresh tokens in per-server Windows Credential Manager profiles. Connect allows up to five minutes for sign-in; subsequent reconnects reuse the token, and the desktop can show saved authorization and clear it. Configured static bearer credentials continue to take precedence. A real local ASGI authorization fixture verifies metadata discovery, client registration, callback/state handling, PKCE verifier exchange, vault persistence and reconnect without a second browser launch. A denied `access_denied` callback now displays a refusal message, saves no token and leaves the server disconnected; SDK-wrapped OAuth errors produce a sanitized API error. The combined MCP HTTP, stdio and API test batch passes (48 tests); Ruff, format, strict Pyright, API contract export/generated types and desktop TypeScript pass. Hosted Windows source runs [42791959669](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42791959669) and [42793462487](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42793462487) passed the full Python suite, browser/API checks and recovery diagnostics; the installer job was skipped for this source-only change. No external OAuth provider, real user credential or model inference was used. Installed-app browser acceptance and broad provider interoperability remain open.

Added a real stdio fixture that terminates its process during a tool call. The ambiguous call is not replayed. The full MCP stdio integration file passes (9 tests), with Ruff, formatting and strict manager Pyright checks passing. `MCPManager.connect_server` now checks that the connection-owner task is still alive after discovery and before publishing the server's tools. ## Fix native process-death installer acceptance database path (2026-11-10)

Dispatched hosted installer run [42736938234](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42736938234) exposed that `scripts/native_restart_smoke.mjs` seeded its crash-boundary output event into `%LOCALAPPDATA%\XAA\xaa.sqlite3`, while its app process uses `XAA_DATA_DIR=<evidence-root>` and stores the database at `<evidence-root>\XAA\xaa.sqlite3`.
