## Hermes conditional skill visibility declarations (2026-11-06)

Pinned Hermes source documentation defines `requires_toolsets`, `requires_tools`, `fallback_for_toolsets`, and `fallback_for_tools` as conditions for showing skills based on available capabilities. The pinned 186-skill inventory has ten `requires_toolsets` keys (nine non-empty, one empty) and none of the other three fields. Agent Plugin discovery and immutable candidate provenance now preserve all four as bounded untrusted names; readiness explicitly reports the conditions as unassessed. X.A.A. does not filter procedure discovery, automatically select fallbacks, or map these declarations to grants. Regression/API and browser coverage exercise each field. Focused importer tests pass (15 passed, one Windows symlink skip); the full suite passes (642 passed, one Windows symlink skip, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning). Ruff, formatting, strict Pyright, API/generated contracts, TypeScript, and production desktop build pass. Dependency-resolution, toolset compatibility and conditional selection remain open.

## Reviewable package dependency identifiers (2026-11-06)

Pinned Hermes source inspection found that all 30 skill dependency declarations use lists, often with version constraints, and do not declare a dependency manager. Discovery and immutable candidate provenance now include validated package identifiers and explicitly declared manager keys where applicable. Version constraints, direct URLs and unparsed dependency values are omitted; imports do not install dependencies. Coverage verifies package-name extraction, manager reporting, direct-reference omission, and provenance redaction. Focused importer tests pass (15 passed, one Windows symlink skip), the full suite passes (642 passed, one Windows symlink skip and the existing Starlette/httpx warning), and the targeted desktop discovery browser flow passes. Ruff, formatting, strict Pyright, API/generated contracts, TypeScript and production desktop build pass. Dependency resolution and setup remain open; overall release completion remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

## Current-machine review for imported skill declarations (2026-11-06)

Package discovery now compares declared platform names, required command executable names, and required environment-variable names against the running engine's platform, `PATH`, and environment. It reports only command names and environment-name presence; it does not expose command paths or environment values. A scan checks at most 256 distinct executable names and reports any remaining names as unchecked. Dependency installation, command semantics, and Hermes toolset support stay explicitly unresolved. The result is computed for discovery only, is not persisted into the immutable source manifest, and grants no readiness or permission. Unit/API tests cover compatible, incompatible and unrecognized platforms, present/missing/unchecked command and environment names, unresolved declaration reporting, and value redaction; the browser discovery flow verifies the advisory status is rendered. Focused tests pass (15 passed, one Windows symlink skip); the full suite passes (642 passed, one Windows symlink skip, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning). Ruff, formatting, strict Pyright, API/generated contract checks, TypeScript, production desktop build, and the targeted browser test pass. Overall release completion remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

## Hermes skill requirement declaration review (2026-11-06)

Agent Plugin discovery now surfaces bounded summaries of imported skill platform declarations, Hermes toolsets, prerequisite field and executable names, required environment-variable names, and dependency counts. The same summary is stored as immutable candidate source provenance, while dependency values and environment values are omitted. Desktop discovery and candidate review label the declarations as untrusted and explicitly state they grant no permissions. Regression coverage verifies command argument parsing, environment-name redaction, omission of dependency values, and preservation of original skill text. The focused importer suite passes (12 passed, one platform skip); the full Python suite passes (639 passed, one platform skip, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning). Ruff, formatting, strict Pyright on changed production modules, generated API contract checks, and desktop TypeScript checks pass. This improves review visibility but does not resolve platform readiness, dependency installation, or toolset-to-permission mapping; release completion remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

## Pinned Hermes skill package compatibility audit (2026-11-06)

Added a reproducible, object-level inventory of all 44 default and 142 optional skill packages at the pinned Hermes commit, including metadata keys, declared platforms/prerequisites, package file hashes/modes and local Markdown destinations. No package content was imported or executed during the source audit. The audit found 181 packages using nested `metadata.hermes` metadata and nine declaring Hermes toolsets, 23 SKILL.md files above X.A.A.'s 16 KiB candidate limit, and five under-limit packages with unresolved linked destinations; 158 meet the current bounded text/resource preflight. The importer now accepts the pinned Hermes metadata extension as untrusted source data and maps its category only when consistent with standard category metadata. Package frontmatter regression tests pass (12 passed, one Windows symlink skip). Ruff and strict Pyright pass for the importer and new audit script. Direct discovery of Hermes folder layouts, prerequisite/platform/toolset-to-permission mapping, the size/resource exceptions, external catalog packages and script execution remain open. This closes one metadata compatibility mismatch and adds an audited dependency/size matrix; overall completion remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

## Bounded local provider and specialist stream recovery (2026-11-06)

LM Studio chat requests now retry one time after transport failures and transient HTTP 408/429/500/502/503/504 responses, with a short bounded delay; permanent client errors are returned without retry. Specialists now reject incomplete/invalid streamed tool calls before execution and may request one safe repair completion when their persisted iteration budget has room. The retry reason is stored as a safe error code and shown in the desktop activity timeline. Focused provider, specialist and stream assembly tests pass (35); Ruff, formatting, strict Pyright, TypeScript and native development build pass. The full engine suite passes 637 tests, one Windows symlink-creation skip, and one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning.

A rebuilt native app with LM Studio `qwen/qwen3.5-9b` completed diagnostics, generated an inspect→review plan, and ran both dependent specialists. Each specialist independently read the random fixture, returned the exact code and matching SHA-256, and the fixture's byte-level review accepted both candidates. Retained evidence: `.acceptance/d93dfc1a-748a-49d1-8c4c-25960004ea1c/`. Earlier failed and timeout acceptance artifacts remain retained. This verifies a bounded recovery path and one successful native specialist scenario; model reliability, broad transient-failure acceptance, and the wider orchestration/release gates remain open. Overall completion remains not quantified (historical figure retired).
## Pinned external execution backend source audit (2026-11-06)

Completed pinned source reviews for the remaining external execution declarations: Cua Spaces, DeepInfra Sandboxes, smol machines, Upstash Box and Hermes WSL terminal. Together with the prior Bubblewrap, WSL Containers and Singularity reviews, all eight external backend declarations in `execution-inventory.json` now record source paths, exact commits, observed constraints and backend-specific acceptance still required. Findings include implicit Hermes skill/cache/credential file transfers for some cloud backends, Upstash Boxes' account-connected guest credentials and same-name sharing risks, explicit guest networking for smol machines, Cua's varying OS/stream/cancellation paths, DeepInfra's ignored generic CPU/memory settings, and WSL terminal's unrestricted distro-user access. Sources were fetched at their catalog commits and inspected only; none was installed, imported, or executed. The inventory and parity matrix retain every external backend as planned, with no runtime or X.A.A. parity claim. Phase 0 still has broader catalog/channel audits open; all runtime and release gates remain open. Overall goal completion remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

## Installed command acceptance and engine launch environment (2026-11-06)

The installed Tauri package and frozen engine now pass an end-to-end project-fix acceptance with the real local `qwen/qwen3.5-9b` model and Ubuntu-24.04 WSL Bubblewrap sandbox. The model completed the tool-use diagnostic, changed only the fixture calculator through workspace tools, ran one restricted command, and passed five percentage cases. The command snapshot hash matches the retained corrected file; current-project validation passed and the fixture review completed. The isolated installer run also verified app-owned authenticated API access, resisted hostile inherited `XAA_DATA_DIR`, `XAA_API_TOKEN` and `XAA_API_PORT` values, shut down the engine, and uninstalled both binaries. Report: `.acceptance/installed-command-16dc8efdd5b840699cdb7514220049a2/report.json`; command details: `.acceptance/installed-command-16dc8efdd5b840699cdb7514220049a2/command-acceptance/report.json`.

This acceptance exposed that Rust `Command` inherited the desktop caller's environment into the engine. The launcher now clears the child environment, passes only required Windows runtime paths and app-generated API credentials, and leaves the engine's data path under the isolated Windows app profile. The smoke harness exercises hostile parent values and the installed-package data path. Locally, the installer build and TypeScript typecheck pass. Hosted [run 42169762843](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42169762843) passed Windows source checks and the installer build, frozen worker diagnostics, and install/boot/uninstall. ## Supervisor recovery loop resilience (2026-11-06)

The periodic supervisor previously let one uncaught store/dispatch/command-cleanup exception terminate its recovery task. It now isolates run scans, per-run dispatch, and stopped-command cleanup so one transient failure does not stop the other recovery work or later polling cycles; cancellation still exits normally for shutdown. A regression injects transient SQLite and command-recovery failures, then verifies the next cycle recovers and dispatches a durable queued run exactly once while the recovery task remains alive. Ten focused recovery/lease/cleanup tests pass. The full engine suite passes 610 tests with one Windows symlink-creation skip and one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning; Ruff, formatting, strict Pyright, and diff checks pass. Hosted [run 42162651770](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42162651770) passed engine checks and the Windows installer build, frozen worker checks, and install/boot/uninstall. This hardens recovery availability but does not close Phase 2's broad external-write/reboot acceptance gate.

## First authenticated email channel slice (2026-11-06)

Added one configurable text-only IMAP mailbox with SMTP replies through the shared supervisor. Mailbox credentials and server settings are stored in Windows Credential Manager; the database stores only non-secret server/allowlist metadata plus durable UIDVALIDITY/UID records. An exact sender allowlist is combined with the first `Authentication-Results` header from a configured exact authserv-id and aligned passing DMARC; conflicting duplicate headers from that authserv-id also fail closed before a message can create an Observe run. Automated senders, unlisted senders, HTML-only/empty and oversized messages are ignored. Ingress is deduplicated by mailbox UIDVALIDITY and UID, with a durable per-mailbox high-water UID that resumes after restart and resets safely when UIDVALIDITY changes. Schema 18 adds the mailbox and message journal tables plus cursor persistence. Replies retain Message-ID threading; durable send intent makes SMTP ambiguity `unknown` and never retries it. Shutdown waits for its bounded in-flight SMTP operation before returning, leaving interrupted delivery evidence for restart reconciliation. The desktop includes setup and status controls. Seven email channel/config/service/API tests and the schema-18 migration test pass; focused channel/migration run passes 25 tests. The full engine suite passes 605 tests with one Windows symlink-creation skip and one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning. The email browser settings flow passes; Ruff, formatting, strict Pyright, generated OpenAPI/types, TypeScript, client tests and production desktop build pass. Hosted [run 42157798400](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42157798400) passed engine checks and the Windows installer build, frozen worker checks, and install/boot/uninstall flow. Live IMAP/SMTP acceptance is not claimed because no mailbox was configured. Live mail-server acceptance, transport health diagnostics, broader mailbox/thread semantics, restart-on-real-mail, attachments, multi-account operation, and remaining Hermes channel inventory are open. This is an implemented partial slice; no channel parity gate or overall release phase is claimed complete.

## Bounded local inference queue (2026-11-06)

Replaced implicit unbounded lock waiting with one FIFO admission queue shared by inference, exact prompt counting, model lifecycle, endpoint and credential changes. It bounds queued operations at 16 and the estimated active-plus-waiting prompt/tool/output working set at 2,000,000 tokens, and rejects queue waits beyond 180 seconds. Weight uses compact UTF-8 request bytes plus the requested output allowance; uncapped calls use the known instance context length or an 8,192-token default. Saturation and timeout fail before inference HTTP; cancellation releases request count and weight. The provider still serializes to one local server, and the byte-based estimate is not tokenizer or GPU telemetry. FIFO order, queue-count and weighted limits, deadline cleanup, cancellation and exact-count path pass focused tests (29 cases); the full suite passes 609 tests with one Windows symlink-creation skip and one existing deprecation warning. Ruff, formatting, strict Pyright and diff checks pass. Hosted [run 42161018495](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42161018495) passed engine and Windows installer checks, including packaged workers and install/boot/uninstall. Live cancellation on Qwen2.5 0.5B and a 100 ms response-body timeout after server headers on Qwen3.5 9B both recovered with a successor `READY`; evidence is linked in [local inference queue](architecture/inference-queue.md). Hardware-aware capacity discovery, prioritization, cross-process scheduling and broad model-by-model acceptance remain open.

## Editable specialist identities (2026-11-06)

Specialist display names are now editable through an authenticated API and the desktop profile panel, with nonblank, bounded, case-insensitively unique names. Saved task plans snapshot the identity name along with the specialist model route and role instructions; the runtime prompt uses that frozen identity and retains the stable role for memory scoping. Schema 17 backfills identity names for existing tasks from their configured role profile. Unit coverage includes profile validation, immutable plan snapshots after renaming, runtime routing, and schema migration; the specialist-profile browser flow passes. Focused engine coverage passes 28 tests; the full suite passes 597 tests with one Windows symlink-creation skip. The specialist-profile browser flow, client checks, production build, Ruff, strict Pyright and generated contracts pass. Hosted run [42154038953](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42154038953) passed engine checks; the Windows installer job also completed successfully, including frozen worker, private command scope, package install, boot and uninstall checks. Live LM Studio acceptance passed the harmless `qwen3-0.6b` tool round trip and two independent `qwen/qwen3.5-9b` specialists reading separate nonce files. Successful specialist evidence is in `.acceptance/63a5b6b7-d7df-43c8-8005-2df82d5bd284/`; one earlier flaky attempt is retained separately as failed evidence. Overall estimate remains not quantified (historical figure retired); durable specialist identity/routing parity is still partial.

## Model-facing procedure discovery and selected version view (2026-11-05)

Added the journaled `procedure_catalog` coordinator tool. It returns at most 50 paginated entries from the non-deprecated library, with versioned description/category, lifecycle state and declared tool/permission requirements; it never returns procedure bodies. Agent Skills `metadata.category` and descriptions survive import/export, and user-authored procedures can set their own description/category. Exact category filtering is supported. Results are labeled untrusted, and both tool description and system prompt state that metadata grants no authority and cannot select a procedure; run selection remains an explicit user action. The tool is available only when a workspace is selected. Runs with an explicitly selected immutable procedure snapshot receive `procedure_view`, which verifies the snapshot digest and returns only that selected version's bounded text as untrusted data. Runs without selection cannot invoke it. Tests cover filtered discovery, explicit selection, immutable view after revision and refusal without selection. The full suite passes (596 passed, one Windows symlink-creation skip); focused procedure tests pass (12 passed). Ruff, strict Pyright, generated contracts and TypeScript pass. The overall release estimate remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

## Export versioned procedures as Agent Plugins packages (2026-11-05)

Added authenticated ZIP export for an exact procedure version and connected the desktop Export action to download it as a binary package. The package contains a valid Agent Plugins manifest, one `skills/<slug>/SKILL.md`, and the selected immutable version's linked resources. Valid imported frontmatter is preserved; X.A.A.-authored procedures receive generated name/description/allowed-tools frontmatter. The export excludes evaluation history and local permissions. Unit coverage exports and rediscovers an imported skill with linked resource bytes, and exports a locally authored procedure into a rediscoverable package. At delivery the full suite passed (590 passed, one Windows symlink-creation skip); Pyright, Ruff, contract checks, TypeScript, client tests, browser export and the desktop production build passed. Both hosted engine and Windows installer workflows passed for the export commit. The 16 KiB skill document cap means export rejects text plus generated frontmatter that exceeds that import bound; full upstream schema semantics, package install, non-text assets and scripts remain open. This adds a package round trip but does not close Phase 5 or the overall release gate.

## Versioned linked resources for imported Agent Skills (2026-11-05)

Completed bounded support-file import for local Markdown links in an Agent Skill. Import resolves links relative to the skill folder, rejects normalized-path violations, traversal and symlink escape, accepts UTF-8 text formats only, and caps the package at 32 files, 64 KiB per file and 1 MiB total. Resource bytes, media types and SHA-256 digests are stored per immutable procedure version; selected run snapshots include them and verify integrity. A run exposes a `procedure_read_resource` tool with an enum restricted to that candidate's linked paths; returned contents are labeled untrusted, and revisions preserve resources when the editor submits no assets. Full Python suite passes (589 passed, one Windows symlink-creation skip); strict Pyright, Ruff, generated contracts, TypeScript, client tests, dependency lock check and MCP browser flow pass. Package export, non-text assets, scripts, full Agent Skills semantics and broad parity remain open. Total release estimate remains not quantified (historical figure retired); this slice does not close a phase gate.

## Explicit Agent Skill import to inactive candidate (2026-11-05)

Extended Agent Skills handling with bounded YAML frontmatter validation. Discovery reads no more than 16 KiB per `SKILL.md` prefix and checks name/directory agreement, description, compatibility, metadata and allowed-tools; aliases are rejected. The desktop displays the declared metadata. On explicit import, exact X.A.A. tool names map to candidate requirements and corresponding workspace permission requirements; unknown source tools are preserved as unmapped provenance and never granted. This declares what a candidate needs but does not authorize a run; selected run permissions still gate each trial. UTF-8 content is capped at 16 KiB, and source package/version, metadata and file digests survive revisions and are visible in procedure review. Tests cover malformed/aliased metadata, auth, requirement mapping, provenance retention and oversized input refusal; browser coverage verifies discovery, import and refresh. The full suite passes 583 tests with one Windows symlink-creation skip. Full schema semantics, configuration/platform dependency resolution, support-file snapshots, install and MCP launch remain open.

## Static Agent Plugins package discovery (2026-11-05)

Added authenticated `POST /extensions/packages/discover` and a desktop discovery panel. The scanner accepts one package directory or its immediate child packages, with limits of 128 packages, 128 skills per package, 64 MCP servers and 256 KiB per manifest/skill file. It rejects duplicate JSON keys and escaped paths, checks bounded frontmatter and summarizes MCP transport metadata without returning raw arguments, environment values, HTTP headers or endpoint paths. Stdio summaries expose only command basename, argument count and environment variable names; HTTP summaries require HTTPS or loopback HTTP. A separate explicit import action reads at most 16 KiB of UTF-8 skill text into the procedure library as an inactive candidate with source digests, metadata and mapped requirements; it refreshes the desktop procedure list. Focused tests cover static discovery/redaction, auth, frontmatter validation, import provenance/revision and size refusal. A browser flow verifies discovery, import and list refresh. Pyright, Ruff, OpenAPI and generated desktop contracts, TypeScript, production build, and the full suite pass (583 passed, one local symlink-creation skip). Full skill schema/dependency mapping, package install, MCP launch and activation remain open. The repository still estimates total release completion at not quantified (historical figure retired); this narrow feature does not close a phase gate.

## Current release status

not quantified (historical figure retired) of the complete objective. This is a rough release-scope estimate, not a claim that any broad phase or Hermes parity gate is complete. Entries below record historical milestones and retain their original test counts/limitations; later evidence supersedes earlier pending statements only for the specific checks it proves.

## Run trajectory export (2026-11-05)

Added authenticated `GET /runs/{run_id}/export` and `xaa export RUN_ID --output PATH`, returning a versioned JSON trajectory with a whitelisted run summary, ordered public event history, and artifact provenance manifest. Exports are bounded by 10,000 events and 10 MiB, omit lease/grant internals and artifact bytes, and redact configured LM Studio, Brave Search, Telegram and MCP vault values plus common bearer/API-key forms. API tests cover authentication, redaction and both export caps; a CLI test verifies URL encoding and saved bytes. Trajectory replay, batch evaluation and broader secret-pattern audits remain open.

## Shared CLI conversation search (2026-11-05)

Added `xaa history` with literal objective or conversation-text search, bounded page size, and opaque-cursor continuation through the same authenticated `/runs` API used by desktop. The focused CLI tests verify bearer auth, query scope and pagination parameters. Interactive chat was added in a later milestone below.

## Interactive CLI conversation (2026-11-05)

Added `xaa chat --model MODEL_ID` over the authenticated run API. Each user turn creates a separate durable coordinator run; the server now persists up to six recent user/candidate pairs in the existing SQLite session/message tables, bounded to 8 KiB of context, and enforces model/workspace/mode consistency. Named sessions resume across CLI restarts; `/reset` or `--reset` clears one transcript without deleting its durable runs, while Ctrl+C requests cancellation of the active run. Oversized saved answers are clipped only in chat context and link back to the full candidate in run history. API, SQLite restart, migration, settings isolation, context carry-over, reset and cancellation tests pass. No live interactive model session was run; CLI/desktop session unification remains open.

## Persistent specialist model routing (2026-11-05)

Added six durable specialist role profiles with authenticated model-route and role-instruction management. Routes must match a currently discovered LM Studio model; saving a plan snapshots each role's route and system instructions into task records, so later profile edits affect only future plans. The desktop exposes both settings per role. Explicit role-scoped memory now has authenticated save/list/delete endpoints and desktop controls; specialist prompts recall up to 4 KiB from that role's separate memory scope, labeled as untrusted and unable to grant permissions. API, role-isolation, delete, plan-snapshot, schema-12 migration, runtime prompt, and browser flows are covered. The full engine suite passes 558 tests; desktop browser checks pass 17 with two live-only skips, all eight client checks pass, and formatting, Pyright, API contracts, TypeScript and production build pass. Broad provider routing parity and richer automatic knowledge extraction remain open.

Working verified slices include real LM Studio discovery/tool use/streaming and the complete first-run onboarding path, durable permission-scoped workspace file operations and recovery, bounded specialist graphs with private edits and explicit conflict integration, restricted WSL commands, exact-snapshot integrated-project validation, explicit parent and specialist preference recall/deletion, conversation FTS, controlled versioned procedure evaluation/activation/rollback, initial approved web fetch/search/browser integrations, persistent interactive CLI chat sessions, and initial image/document input. Source verification passes 574 engine tests. Desktop verification passes 17 browser tests (two live-only skips) and 11 client tests; the objective browser path explicitly checks document upload content. Pyright, Ruff, generated contracts, TypeScript and the production build pass. The focused backup/API/migration set passes 26 tests. Real-model and native evidence is indexed in the architecture/evaluation/release documents.

## End-user operation and restore documentation (2026-11-05)

Added `docs/user-guide.md` covering Windows development-preview setup, first-run model/workspace checks, modes and per-run permissions, result review, integrations, CLI sessions, recovery, local data and current product limits. Corrected the backup workflow to restore `.xaa-backup` bundles into `C:\new-data\XAA` and set `XAA_DATA_DIR=C:\new-data`, matching the engine's actual path resolution. The database-migration guide now records schema versions 10–14 and earlier native-onboarding evidence superseding the initial provider-settings gap. This improves operator guidance but does not establish clean-user installation, upgrade, signing or full product release readiness.

## Initial image input (2026-11-05)

New desktop objectives can include one optional PNG/JPEG image (3 MiB maximum). The API requires the selected LM Studio model to advertise vision, stores the image as a managed artifact with the run, and reconstructs its data URL only after path and SHA-256 checks. The coordinator sends image and user text together using the OpenAI-compatible image message shape; the ordinary context estimate assigns each image a fixed 4,096-token allowance, while exact SDK token counting refuses image prompts because it currently models text only. The full engine suite passes 567 tests; Pyright, Ruff, generated contracts, TypeScript, client tests, and the desktop production build pass. The local LM Studio endpoint currently lists 29 models but advertises vision for none, so real-model image interpretation could not be exercised. Document/audio input is not implemented.

## Initial text document input (2026-11-05)

The desktop can now attach one PDF, UTF-8 text file or Markdown document (up to 4 MiB) to a run. `pypdf` text extraction rejects encrypted/invalid PDFs, caps extraction at 50 pages and 48,000 characters, and gives scanned PDFs without selectable text an explicit OCR-not-supported error. The original attachment and extracted text are separate managed artifacts with size/digest metadata; resume reconstructs the text only after containment and SHA-256 checks. The coordinator marks document contents as untrusted reference material, and text is counted by the existing prompt boundary. It cannot be combined with an image in one request. Focused extraction/API/coordinator tests, type/format checks, generated contract checks, client tests and the production UI build pass. The local server has no advertised vision model and no live PDF-reading model run has been completed. OCR, office formats and voice remain open.

## Telegram delivery restart reconciliation (2026-11-05)

Added a service-level restart test using the real `TelegramService.cycle()` and `TelegramBotApi` against an HTTP `MockTransport`. When a schedule delivery was claimed before shutdown, the reopened service reconciles its durable `sending` state to `unknown` and issues no second send request. The Telegram suite passes 20 tests and the full engine suite passes 550 tests. This verifies at-most-once recovery for an ambiguous in-flight send; it does not claim live bot interoperability or successful delivery after restart.

## Scheduler restart dispatch recovery (2026-11-05)

Added a regression test for a process stopping after the scheduler transaction durably creates a queued run and execution but before its in-memory submit callback. Reopening the SQLite store and starting the real `RunSupervisor` recovery loop dispatches that same run exactly once; a subsequent tick does not duplicate it. The focused schedule suite passes 10 tests, and the full engine suite passes 549 tests. Ruff, formatting and Pyright pass. This verifies the scheduler/supervisor crash window without claiming live Telegram delivery or full schedule parity.

## Initial MCP stdio integration (2026-11-05)

Added authenticated server configuration, explicit stdio connect/disconnect, bounded tool discovery, schema validation/digests, default-deny per-tool permissions, read/write mapping, run-specific write grants, OS credential-store references for configured environment variables, and untrusted/journaled tool results. The desktop Integrations panel can add servers, connect, manage secrets and permissions, and select write tools for the next run. The official Python MCP SDK is pinned through `uv.lock`. Per-connection background owner tasks preserve the SDK's AnyIO stdio lifecycle across API request and shutdown tasks.

Verification: the stdio fixture subprocess passes protocol initialize/discovery, default-deny and permission checks, run-specific write gating, schema-change revocation, a tool round trip, untrusted output, API and coordinator disconnect handling without unresolved calls, engine-restart reconnect with persisted permissions, in-flight cancellation with unknown-outcome journaling, authenticated API lifecycle, credential handling, run-grant persistence, and ambiguous write error/output-limit classification. All 548 engine tests pass. 16 desktop browser tests pass with two live-only skips; the new UI flow covers server configuration, tool discovery and explicit run-scoped write grant selection. Eight client checks pass. Ruff, strict Pyright, TypeScript, generated API contracts and desktop production build pass. The rebuilt frozen engine archive contains the MCP SDK session/stdio modules and JSON Schema validators; no packaged live-server run was performed. Hosted run [42065429668](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42065429668) passed engine and installer checks for `97e7c8f`. HTTP transports, package discovery, third-party compatibility, and full Hermes parity remain open. Details: [MCP architecture](architecture/mcp.md).

Packaging produces an unsigned installer with a bundled engine; installed hashes, uninstall and data-marker preservation pass on disposable hosted Windows. Hosted run 41795825037 now passes installed GUI boot, authenticated engine readiness, graceful shutdown, uninstall, binary hashes and data-marker preservation. The test applies and removes an app-specific debugging argument on its elevated runner, as required by modern WebView2. Evidence: `docs/releases/evidence/windows-install-13075bf.json`. This is a test-transport diagnosis, not a completed clean-user release gate.

Hosted run 42000075898 attempt 2 passes source checks, installer build, all three frozen-worker checks, and installed boot/uninstall on commit `6db195a78df45c8b32086b67370054f9b5fa89d9`. The step-level metadata is recorded in `docs/releases/evidence/windows-installer-rerun-6db195a.json`. The subsequent backup/restore commit `3d4790e9cebd87d43fe3c49607a3da3c2ef2dc2c` also passes the hosted source suite, installer build, frozen worker checks and installed boot/uninstall in run 42003096507; see `docs/releases/evidence/windows-backup-restore-3d4790e.json`.

The managed-browser implementation commit `8b5f558378d154def92c663b395b15ce0ebc9725` passed hosted run [42054996676](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42054996676): Windows source checks and the Windows installer job, including packaged-resource checks, frozen worker checks, install/boot/uninstall, and data preservation.

Major remaining work includes autonomous artifact/test review, broader merge recovery, durable agent identities/routing, complete skill-package discovery/drafting, the remaining desktop screens/settings, MCP and broader web/browser workflows, scheduling delivery and messaging, media/backends, the remaining pinned capability audit, upgrades/recovery/install coverage, signing/notices and complete user documentation. Existing narrow tests do not prove those requirements.

### Phase 7 partial — managed browser (2026-11-05)

Added a distinct per-run browser grant, exact-host-constrained managed Playwright worker, disposable Edge context, bounded visible-text snapshots, accessible link navigation, and screenshot PNG artifacts with desktop image preview. Browser requests are routed through a local CONNECT proxy that checks exact hostnames, public DNS answers and GET/HEAD methods; redirects and page requests are rechecked. The worker strips engine/provider tokens, enforces request/output/page/image/network limits and is stopped as an owned process tree. Forms, buttons, downloads, saved profiles and general browser interaction are intentionally unsupported in this slice; MCP and full Phase 7 remain incomplete. Details: [managed browser](architecture/managed-browser.md), [pinned-source gap](parity/web-browser-source-audit.md).

Verification: all 542 engine tests pass; the focused API/browser set passes 20 tests; Ruff, formatting, Pyright, TypeScript, API/desktop generated-contract checks and eight client tests pass. The desktop browser suite passes 15 checks; its two skipped cases require live dependencies. The controlled objective/browser-permission flow passes. Live Microsoft Edge read acceptance against `example.com` passed in source mode and through the one-file frozen engine; both returned visible text and roughly 45 KB PNG output. The unsigned 59.91 MiB Windows installer rebuild passed locally, and hosted run 42054996676 passed its installer build, frozen-worker checks, and install/boot/uninstall steps. Evidence: [source browser](releases/evidence/browser-live.json), [frozen browser](releases/evidence/browser-frozen-live.json). The browser runs under the current Windows account and is not an OS-level isolation boundary; public GET requests may still have site-specific side effects.

Private editing milestone: write-enabled specialists use immutable snapshot artifacts plus atomic task-owned journal overlays. Two real local-model developers produced distinct valid changes to the same starting file while the project remained unchanged. User review now shows full diffs and current content, requires explicit conflict choices and authorization, and integrates exact approved bytes through owned workers. Recovery checks cover unknown effects, completed writes awaiting review commitment, known no-effect retries and duplicate idempotency keys. Schema 5 prevents older readers from accepting proposals without integration. Scope and evidence: [specialist authority](architecture/specialist-authority.md), [acceptance index](releases/evidence/task-workspaces.json). No autonomous reviewer or parent completion is claimed by the fixture.

## Phase 0

- Repository initialized with project scope and initial architecture/API/data contracts.
- Official Hermes repository pinned to commit `1e208b280deb18f01d015b0acdf3ef3552d08039`.
- Added an initial capability matrix covering the required product areas, expected observable behavior, proposed module, dependencies, acceptance evidence and honest planned status. See `docs/parity/hermes-matrix.md`.
- Added a pinned Python project setup (`uv.lock`) and an asynchronous LM Studio provider supporting health checks, OpenAI-compatible model discovery/chat/streaming, native capability metadata, and native model load/unload without downloading models.
- Live local integration evidence: LM Studio at `127.0.0.1:1234` returned healthy and exposed 29 models. `scripts/lm_studio_tool_diagnostic.py` loaded the already-installed `qwen3-0.6b`, received a structured `add_numbers(40, 2)` call, schema-validated the arguments, returned `42`, and received a correct final answer. Diagnostic unloaded the model afterward; native model inventory confirmed no instance remained loaded.
- Added authenticated FastAPI routes for provider health, model listing and SSE chat streaming. Service startup requires an explicit 32+ character bearer token and binds to loopback; CORS permits the Tauri localhost origins only. Three unit tests cover authentication, stream sequencing and short-token rejection.
- Added explicit run states and legal transition graph plus SQLite WAL persistence for durable run records and monotonic run events. The initial schema includes sessions, messages, tasks/dependencies, tool calls, artifacts, approvals, memories, skills/versions, schedules/executions, channel bindings and provider profiles.
- Added authenticated run create/read/event replay/pause/resume/cancel API routes. A recovery test reopens the database, reads the paused run and events, then resumes the run; invalid terminal-state transitions are rejected.
- Verification now passes: Ruff, Pyright, and 6 pytest cases. Pytest data uses a guarded unique directory inside the workspace because the configured Windows Temp directory is inaccessible to this process. FastAPI's TestClient emits a Starlette/httpx deprecation warning; tests still pass.
- Live streaming evidence: loaded the same installed model, streamed a response through the provider (185 SSE data chunks including reasoning/content/final chunks), then unloaded it; native model inventory confirmed no loaded instance remained.
- Ruff and Pyright pass for `engine` and `scripts` after correcting a strict type-checker path issue.
- Audit status: upstream README and top-level source inventory inspected; exact channel/backend inventory and source-level capability verification remain in progress.
- Phase 0 gate: incomplete until exhaustive pinned-revision inventory, typed API schemas, and acceptance criteria review are done.
- Phase 1: partial. Discovery, one real harmless tool round trip, model load/unload, real streaming, and a bounded structured-tool capability diagnostic work. Context admission uses a conservative prompt estimate or an owned exact-counting worker and reserves the selected output allowance. A bounded process-local FIFO inference queue now serializes provider inference, prompt counting and lifecycle work while accounting for estimated prompt/output working set. Real cancellation and response-read timeout acceptance each pass on one installed model, with successor requests succeeding; broader model compatibility and hardware-aware scheduling remain open. The harmless tool round trip does not establish broad model compatibility.
- Phase 2: partial. Durable run/event storage, explicit transitions and API controls are present. Persistent fenced run leases, a periodic supervisor recovery loop, tool-call intent/result journaling, per-run permission grants, and storage/coordinator recovery paths are implemented. Store recovery requeues expired runs with no unresolved side effects and blocks uncertain intents for reconciliation. Actual read-crash resume and interrupted existing-file-write reconciliation have evidence. A blocked-run panel now supports owner-reviewed no-effect reconciliation for interrupted workspace create/edit intents, with filesystem hash/absence checks before the saved failure is attached. These paths do not cover every external write, abrupt reboot, or all worker/process recovery paths. The phase gate remains incomplete.
- Lease and recovery implementation grew beyond the initial lease primitive: unique per-ownership fencing tokens are checked on every owned write/transition, expired ownership is recovered at startup, and write intents cannot be blindly replayed. `tests/unit/test_leases.py`, `test_recovery.py`, `test_tool_journal.py`, and the real read/write crash diagnostics cover distinct layers. The latest supervisor regression proves transient scan/cleanup errors do not permanently kill the periodic recovery task; see the dated entry above. The next acceptance gaps are broader end-to-end crash/reboot coverage and recovery for every external-effect type.

## Overall

### Portable database and artifact recovery (2026-11-05)

Authenticated API backups now produce an atomic `.xaa-backup` bundle containing the consistent SQLite snapshot and every managed artifact referenced by that snapshot. Creation verifies stored artifact bytes against the database size and SHA-256 metadata and refuses unsafe links, paths, or oversized bundles. New offline `xaa restore-bundle` validates archive membership, schema, SQLite integrity/foreign keys and every artifact before atomically publishing a new data directory; it refuses overwrite. The prior database-only restore remains available and explicitly documented as metadata-only. Four targeted backup tests pass, including real artifact recovery, overwrite refusal, traversal refusal, and snapshot behavior. Clean-machine disaster recovery, retention, and automated backup scheduling remain open. See [backup and restore](releases/backup-restore.md).

### Persisted LM Studio address, selection and desktop tool diagnostic (2026-11-05)

The authenticated API now persists the LM Studio loopback endpoint and selected discovered model in the existing provider profile. HTTP loopback is required; credentials, paths, query strings and remote hosts are refused. Address changes are rejected while a worker owns a run and clear the selected model. The desktop setup panel shows actionable Local Server instructions, checks connectivity, discovers model IDs, saves selection and runs the existing harmless schema-validated tool round trip. The existing coordinator still performs its own runtime diagnostic before enabling tools. Twelve provider configuration cases and fourteen API cases pass; a new browser setup scenario verifies saved address/model and the diagnostic interaction. This adds the provider setting and diagnostic step but does not complete onboarding's workspace selection or reviewed demo, nor a real native fresh-user onboarding acceptance. Details: [provider settings](architecture/provider-settings.md).

First unsigned NSIS Windows installer now builds with pinned CLI, release Tauri desktop and bundled frozen engine. A real release boot/authentication/shutdown smoke check passes under restricted PATH. A disposable hosted installer job now builds, installs, boots, uninstalls and checks default data preservation; hosted install execution remains pending. Scope and reproducible commands are in `docs/releases/windows-installer.md`. Signing, notices, upgrade/clean-end-user acceptance and full release gates remain unfinished; estimated completion remains approximately 10%.

First hosted Windows CI is now green for commit 365780b: locked uv/npm setup, Ruff checks, strict typing across engine and all diagnostics, API/type-generation consistency, TypeScript, client tests and pytest all pass. Run 41789006353 follows the initial failed run 41788808844; exact scope and links are in `docs/releases/ci-validation.md`. This provides independent hosted verification of configured source checks, not native packaging/live-model/installer acceptance. Overall estimate remains approximately 10%; full release work continues.

Evaluated procedures now support explicit activation/upgrade, active-version reuse, rollback and deprecation with monotonic stale-decision guards and durable schema-4 audit history. Real model cases prove distinct version-two behavior and reverted version-one behavior without changing files; native controls, passive histories and schema-3 migration pass. All 294 engine tests pass, along with seven browser and seven client checks. Evidence/limits are in `docs/architecture/procedure-candidates.md`. Automatic skill drafting/discovery, broad representative evaluations and full parity/release gates remain incomplete; estimated completion remains approximately 10%.

Procedure evaluation now records explicit criteria/rationale against at least two completed, accepted trials of the exact frozen version, with typed evidence and stale-review protection. The desktop provides a matching-trial picker and saved evidence view; revisions preserve history and reset to candidate. Real ASCII/Unicode model trials and native review submission pass without activating the procedure. The Unicode trial also exposed/fixed locale-dependent filesystem worker output; source and frozen regressions pass. All 291 engine tests pass; details in `docs/architecture/procedure-candidates.md`. Activation, deprecation, rollback and full release gates remain unfinished; estimate remains approximately 10%.

Explicit procedure-version trials now work through run creation and parent model context, with atomic immutable snapshots, digest checks and permission/tool dependency validation. A real local model trial reads the exact file code and follows a reporting label available only in the selected older version; the library stays candidate/inactive. The trial also exposed and fixed SDK merging of consecutive user messages; normal count fixtures remain matching. Source, native selection and automated evidence are in `docs/architecture/procedure-candidates.md`. Evaluation approval, activation, rollback, representative reuse and release gates remain unfinished; estimate remains approximately 10%.

Versioned user-authored procedure candidates now work through authenticated API and desktop: create, inspect, revise with optimistic conflict detection, export a historical version and delete all versions. Backup/restore and two-connection concurrent edits are tested; final native editor/reload/deletion evidence is in `docs/architecture/procedure-candidates.md`. All 273 engine tests and seven browser checks pass. Candidates remain inactive and are not used by runs; evaluation, activation, rollback, runtime reuse and the complete skill/release gates remain unfinished. Overall estimate remains approximately 10%.

Public conversation phrase search now works through the API and desktop using a schema-3 FTS5 index. Native passive recall of an existing real model run proves answer-only retrieval, migration from schema 2, reopen/reload and unchanged journal rows. All 266 engine tests pass; details and limits are in `docs/releases/database-migrations.md`. This closes the initial public-text recall slice, while skills, broad parity and release gates remain incomplete. Estimated overall completion remains approximately 10%.

Reservation visibility now reaches authenticated API metadata and the desktop, including paused/interrupted holds after native startup and renderer reload. Single-snapshot reporting separates measured usage from outstanding tokens; native synthetic fixture evidence and automated checks are recorded in `docs/architecture/tokenizer-service.md`. Broader provider equivalence, default exact admission and full release gates remain incomplete. Estimated overall completion remains approximately 10%.

Durable inference-linked token holds now participate in opt-in automatic admission for parent, diagnostic and specialist requests. Transactions reserve counted prompt plus output before HTTP against parent/own limits; matching durable measured replies replace holds with actual usage, while interrupted/unknown requests retain holds. Real source read acceptance records four holds and matching counts; deterministic checks prove pre-HTTP parent/specialist quota rejection and persistence/release boundaries. Details in `docs/architecture/tokenizer-service.md`. Broader model count parity, default enablement, UI reservation visibility and the full release gates remain incomplete. Overall estimate remains approximately 10%.

Opt-in automatic SDK context admission now holds one provider inference/lifecycle lock across counting and normal/streamed prediction, rejects count errors and context overflow before HTTP, and supplies a 512-token cap when unspecified. Enable with `XAA_EXACT_CONTEXT=1`; the default remains conservative while broader model compatibility is unproven. Source real-run and stream/overflow evidence are in `docs/architecture/tokenizer-service.md`. Durable aggregate reservations and the remaining release gates are still incomplete; estimated overall completion remains approximately 10%.

Canonical model history and full-catalog tokenizer compatibility now implemented. New parent/specialist journal messages retain only public assistant text and structured tool calls; historical replay and API/SSE event views apply the same projection without rewriting old database rows. Tool IDs and raw argument strings remain unchanged. A restored metadata-only candidate blocks rather than becoming a final answer. The actual plan schema exposed SDK 1.6.0b1 dropping root `$defs` while retaining nested `$ref`; bounded in-document expansion is now shared by HTTP inference and SDK counting, with unsupported reference semantics rejected and original Pydantic argument validation retained. Real source acceptance `.acceptance/78c560b5-c420-4ae5-932b-8e39b0b5a0b9/` matches all four prompt counts (314, 148, 1469, 1639), reaches verifying after a real file read, preserves fixture bytes/mtime and journals no nonpublic model fields. Failed pre-fix evidence `.acceptance/9c8bf7ac-13ca-45ef-9000-2b9d2593acf2/` is retained. All 250 engine tests pass (one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning), Ruff, strict Pyright, diagnostic typing, TypeScript and generated contracts pass; native development build succeeds. This does not establish all-model tokenizer parity, atomic count/infer ownership or hard aggregate budgets. Overall estimate remains approximately 10%; release gates remain incomplete.

Owned tokenizer service implemented: authenticated `POST /providers/lm-studio/token-count` and `xaa tokens` count text/tool prompts against one already-loaded local instance without prediction, loading, tools or run-journal writes. The provider serializes counting with inference/lifecycle work and deducts queue wait from its worker allowance. Strict bounded JSON goes to a fixed `--tokenizer-worker` entry point; OS-vault/configured credentials use only its input pipe for process handoff, ambient provider/API credentials and proxies are scrubbed, and NO_PROXY=* forces direct loopback RPC. Initial real-SDK stalled-authentication tests found that in-process async cancellation left its connection open; replaced that design with owned process termination/join, shared with filesystem workers. Runtime/uv.lock now pin SDK 1.6.0b1 (prerelease) because stable 1.5.0 lacks the documented API-token argument. The SDK and metadata are bundled; no SDK agent loop is used. All 238 engine tests pass with the existing Starlette/httpx warning; six browser and seven client checks pass, one live browser check skipped; Ruff/format, strict Pyright, diagnostic typing, TypeScript and generated contracts pass. Source owned-worker parity `.acceptance/79d8eeb0-cd3d-4455-a383-ddb333fd6670/` matches all four earlier prompt counts. Final native API/development CLI acceptance `.acceptance/25a716a3-9c04-4c59-b0e6-50637e352f47/` returns counts 13/324 from the bundled worker under restricted PATH, requests no prediction and verifies model unload/graceful shutdown. Final frozen cancellation `.acceptance/65ad5a8f-2fb8-405b-a8ee-5571c4894488/` observes three owned processes, then verifies all gone and stalled connection closed with synthetic credentials/bad ambient proxy settings. Shared helper frozen filesystem regression `.acceptance/524637d5-07b1-40b0-adf0-34453369aebb/` preserves fixture and leaves no unresolved intent. See `docs/architecture/tokenizer-service.md` for synthetic-versus-real authentication scope and prerelease considerations. Automatic inference counting/reservations, canonical reasoning-history replay, broader model compatibility and release gates remain open; field-name-only audit found reasoning_content in older ordinary model journals, without reading or rewriting its text. This service does not establish hard aggregate ceilings. Overall estimate remains approximately 10%.

Context/output preflight corrected and tokenizer path measured: normal/streamed provider entry points now validate and reserve the selected output cap alongside the existing conservative JSON/template estimate; oversized known-context requests stop before inference HTTP. The desktop explains context-budget blocks. All 223 engine tests pass with the existing Starlette/httpx warning; six controlled browser checks pass, one live browser check skipped; Ruff/format, strict Pyright, explicit diagnostic typing, TypeScript and contracts pass. Real source admission evidence `.acceptance/f9976ebf-3e45-442d-8ec2-0131abe33eaf/` rejects normal/streamed output allowance 16,384 against loaded context 16,384 with zero inference posts, then admits output 32 and records base usage 13+32. Optional reasoning-detail count 33 differs from completion 32 and is documented rather than treated as exact. Rebuilt native model-generated quota regression passes in `.acceptance/547ca2e8-6f46-421e-9bab-e3972673e0ca/`; its fixture explicitly loads context 32,768 for the 4,096 planning output allowance. Separately, optional temporary SDK 1.5.0 probe `.acceptance/20de837c-6997-4b71-911e-295aa7bd1bfd/` matches formatted tokenizer counts to actual HTTP prompt usage across four fixtures on installed Qwen3.5 9B: 13 plain, 280 tool definitions, 324 tool-result sequence and 40 system/Unicode history tokens. Two earlier partial probes retained SDK history-conversion failures; corrected public builder use is documented in `docs/evaluations/tokenizer-parity.md`. No SDK dependency or second agent loop was added to the engine. Single-model fixture parity does not establish general tokenizer compatibility or hard aggregate ceilings; SDK authentication/cancellation/packaging, atomic count/reserve/infer ownership and broader resource gates remain incomplete. Overall estimate remains approximately 10%.

Native specialist-quota failure workflow verified: public plan records now expose only whitelisted block reasons, while private outcome content and worker tokens remain excluded (API regression included). The plan view explains own-token and dependency blocks. Specialist batches now transactionally propagate dependency blocks after concurrent execution as well as serial claims; explicit dispatch blocks the parent if no ready work or reviewable candidate remains. All 221 engine tests pass with the existing Starlette/httpx warning; six controlled browser checks pass, one live browser check skipped; Ruff, strict Pyright, TypeScript and contract drift checks pass. Rebuilt native acceptance `.acceptance/4cfdd3e8-75ee-4c36-ad1f-bc433365eb6a/` uses actual local-model filename discovery and model-generated inspect/review plan, verifies allowance 1/output cap 256, then dispatches through the UI. Inspect reports 652 tokens (142 output), blocks before any post-dispatch intent, review never infers and becomes dependency-blocked, parent becomes blocked, both safe explanations are visible and fixture bytes are unchanged. Earlier planning list/plan-save intents are preserved. Model unload and graceful engine shutdown pass under restricted PATH, with binary hashes/screenshot/graph/quota report saved. This is deliberate failure-path acceptance, not useful task completion or hard aggregate containment. Atomic token reservations/tokenizer accounting, restricted command/validation infrastructure, worktree integration and broader release gates remain open. Overall estimate remains approximately 10%.

Specialist token allowances implemented: optional saved per-task reported-token thresholds and output caps, schema-derived plan tools/API types and plan-view labels. Plan creation rejects explicit allowances exceeding the parent's latest unused reported threshold and caps above the parent; runtime applies the tighter output cap and checks each specialist's own durable usage before inference, candidates and tool intent, while preserving shared-run checks. Missing/over-threshold usage produces a blocked task outcome; dependents cannot execute. Fixed readiness validation to use the saved graph's original token bounds instead of repeatedly subtracting spending from its original declarations. Existing records inherit run settings. All 219 engine tests pass with the existing Starlette/httpx warning; six browser checks pass, one live browser check skipped; Ruff/format, strict Pyright, diagnostic typing, TypeScript and generated contracts pass. Real local source acceptance `.acceptance/50ba04ce-ec2a-434d-b29b-fca670f357e4/` uses an explicitly seeded graph and real Qwen3.5 9B specialist: 538 tokens against allowance 1, blocked before tool intent, preserved fixture, dependent persistently blocked without inference. Failed harness attempts and corrections are documented in `docs/architecture/token-thresholds.md`. Rebuilt native resource regression passes in `.acceptance/991767be-cda9-441c-9c0a-6d1619e09e6d/`, checking diagnostic/task caps, API/UI totals and graceful shutdown under restricted PATH. This native regression does not exercise task-specific quota blocking; that acceptance is source-engine evidence. Model-generated quota allocation, native quota-failure workflow, atomic aggregate reservations and tokenizer accounting remain open. These are measured stop thresholds, not hard aggregate ceilings. Overall estimate remains approximately 10%.

Diagnostic generation bounded: both standalone and run-triggered capability checks now request at most 512 output tokens per inference, separately from task-response limits. Run start events preserve the requested cap. Diagnostics validate the completion envelope and reject length/filter termination without caching capability success; coordinator/explicit dispatch block with a dedicated reason and the desktop explains that tools remain disabled. Usage remains journaled before a valid truncated response blocks. Provider output verification rejects unknown/over-cap usage. Updated benchmark wrapper to forward caps; normalized its historical evaluation document to UTF-8 and marked the original 12 trials as predating this policy. All 212 engine tests, Ruff/format, strict Pyright, TypeScript, explicit benchmark typing and contract checks pass; existing Starlette/httpx warning remains. Rebuilt real native acceptance `.acceptance/14c21888-7894-4c6b-8cfb-1fb710fc71dd/` independently verifies both diagnostic start caps and response usage <=512, counts two diagnostic responses (807 total tokens including prompts), and matches 2,116 total reported run tokens in API/UI. Task cap 1 still blocks before effects; zero intents, fixture unchanged, model unloaded and graceful shutdown verified under restricted PATH. Per-request diagnostic output bounding is established for this installed server; aggregate prompt-plus-output reservations, tokenizer accounting and per-task quotas remain incomplete. Overall estimate remains approximately 10%.

Run-triggered capability diagnostics now participate in durable token accounting: provider accepts an optional request callback, and parent execution/explicit specialist dispatch use it to persist a unique inference-start event before each real diagnostic request, then validated usage metadata before continuing. No diagnostic conversation or private reasoning is saved. Missing/invalid/interrupted usage stays unknown and configured thresholds fail closed; cached checks make no new request or charge. Workspace iteration allowances remain separate. All 207 tests passed in the full suite with the existing Starlette/httpx warning; eight focused diagnostic tests pass after adding explicit-dispatch coverage. Six browser checks pass, one live browser check skipped; Ruff, strict Pyright, TypeScript and contract checks pass. Updated fake-provider diagnostic signatures for the explicit callback. Real Qwen3.5 9B threshold-1 acceptance in `.acceptance/a775c2cf-901c-498a-aff7-f2848c7a293f/` records 481 diagnostic tokens and blocks before the next diagnostic request or workspace proposal, zero tool intents and preserved fixture bytes/mtime. Rebuilt native acceptance `.acceptance/5d076b8a-977f-485f-a019-c1b099de3e67/` independently sums two diagnostic responses (757 tokens) plus task output to 2,066 total tokens, matching API/UI; task output cap 1 still blocks before effects, with graceful shutdown and exact build hashes recorded. Diagnostics still use independent output configuration; generation caps for them, aggregate reservations, tokenizer accounting and per-task quotas remain open. Overall estimate remains approximately 10%.

Desktop resource controls completed as a vertical slice: editable iteration/time limits and optional reported-token/output-token values with whole-number/range validation; invalid values disable submission. A new run-resource view shows saved configuration, model and measured input/output totals plus unmeasured-call counts, refreshes on execution events and offers retry after usage-read failure. Timeline explains known budget/output blocks. Fixed a public-contract omission discovered during integration: RunLimits previously stripped stored token settings from create/get/control responses; explicit optional fields and a create/read/pause regression now preserve them. Kept specialist TaskBudgets separate after the full suite caught accidental unsupported token fields in task responses. All 200 engine tests pass with the existing Starlette/httpx warning; six controlled browser tests pass, one live browser test skipped; seven client tests, Ruff, strict Pyright, TypeScript and contract drift checks pass. Rebuilt native development bundle and actual LM Studio acceptance passed in `.acceptance/084cde79-fbd4-4e78-9473-54d7552a2284/`: UI-configured 3 iterations/60 seconds/5,000 reported-token threshold/1 output token persisted, real inference length-blocked with zero tool intents, fixture unchanged, usage displayed and explanation visible, then model unloaded and engine shut down under restricted PATH. Earlier passing native iteration `.acceptance/25a31ae8-ab3b-4d21-b3fb-864348198313/` retained; its screenshot was visually inspected. This proves configured output-cap behavior, not useful completion or aggregate containment. Aggregate reservations, diagnostic accounting, per-task quotas, installer and broader release gates remain incomplete. Overall estimate remains approximately 10%.

Per-request output-token limits implemented: optional `max_output_tokens` API/CLI setting persists with run metadata and is forwarded to normal/streamed parent requests and specialist calls. Provider sends LM Studio's documented `max_tokens` parameter, requests streamed usage for capped calls and rejects assembled capped responses with missing/inconsistent usage or reported output overage. Truncation keeps existing fail-closed behavior. Full suite passed 199 tests; final specialist forwarding/output checks passed 16 tests after adding inheritance assertions. Ruff/format, strict Pyright, TypeScript and regenerated contract checks pass. Live Qwen3.5 9B normal and raw-stream requests both reported one generated token and length termination for cap 1; the coordinator blocked with zero tool intents and unchanged fixture. Evidence `.acceptance/0bfe0c5b-2f4a-41ae-9a56-2f2088e7f8db/`. Provider unloaded afterward. These controls request/verify per-call output limits; aggregate prompt-plus-output reservation, diagnostics accounting, per-task quotas and desktop/native acceptance remain incomplete. See `docs/architecture/token-thresholds.md`. Overall estimate remains approximately 10%.

Reported-token stop thresholds implemented as a partial resource-control slice: optional API/CLI `max_total_tokens` persists in the initial run event and is returned with limits; old records remain unconfigured without a schema migration. Parent and specialist work checks aggregate durable usage before inference, after response journaling and before proposals execute. Missing/interrupted usage fails closed; exact-threshold responses may execute current tools but cannot infer again. Budgeted specialists run sequentially with fresh just-in-time claims; unconfigured runs retain concurrent task slots. Extracted coordinator tool construction into a method after strict Pyright identified excessive control-flow complexity. Full suite passed 188 tests with the existing Starlette/httpx warning; 15 focused specialist/threshold checks pass after the final claim-order adjustment. Ruff/format, strict Pyright, TypeScript and generated contract checks pass. Real local Qwen3.5 9B acceptance in `.acceptance/3a700b7c-668d-4a39-8695-acdcade2a756/` reports 1,377 tokens against threshold 1, proposes a read, then blocks with zero tool intents and unchanged fixture bytes/mtime. Model unloaded. This is post-response gating, not a hard inference ceiling: request reservations/output caps, diagnostic inclusion, per-task allowances and desktop configuration remain open; native bundle not rebuilt for this change. See `docs/architecture/token-thresholds.md`. Overall estimate remains approximately 10%.

Packaged directory retry acceptance passed: rebuilt frontend, PyInstaller engine and Tauri development app. Extended the actual Windows contention diagnostic with directory mode; frozen worker in `.acceptance/7069351d-fe06-4064-ad29-9e99dd19d9e6/` encountered the held exclusive directory handle, journaled one retry, then returned the expected entry after release, retaining one intent and unchanged fixture bytes/mtime. Existing frozen file mode also passes in `.acceptance/e5434515-e9ce-4f4d-971e-c14acc50e19a/`. Native discovery/lifecycle smoke for these exact rebuilt digests passes under restricted PATH, with 29 actual model choices, no implicit selection and graceful engine shutdown: `.acceptance/cb42e4b6-6d88-4fdd-b98d-f6b767670133/`. Diagnostic Ruff/format/strict Pyright pass. Latest full runtime suite remains 177 passed from the preceding change; runtime source did not change in this packaging follow-through. Model-driven retry UI, metadata retries, hard token limits and broader release gates remain open. Overall estimate remains approximately 10%.

Directory read retries implemented: enumeration retries only recognized transient I/O errors, with at most three fresh verified-handle attempts and 50/100 ms backoff. Ownership is checked before every attempt; partial listings are discarded and one journaled intent is retained. Windows CreateFile/enumeration failures now preserve winerror so real sharing violations are classified correctly. Nine focused listing tests pass, including an actual Windows exclusive-directory-handle contention scenario that succeeds after one retry, permanent-error/no-retry, exhausted retry and lease-loss checks. Full engine suite: 177 passed with the existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning; Ruff, formatting and strict Pyright pass. This is source-engine acceptance; the native bundle has not been rebuilt for this change. Metadata/path-resolution retries, hard token limits and broader phase gates remain open. Overall release estimate remains approximately 10%.

Local-model baseline and explicit selection: completed 12 real trials across three installed models. Qwen2.5 0.5B passed 2/4, Qwen3 0.6B passed 1/4 and Qwen3.5 9B passed 4/4. All parseable proposals were schema-valid, demonstrating that schema validity does not establish task success. Saved failed trials and a successful-tool-result journal audit; timing, resource scope and evaluation limits are recorded in `docs/evaluations/local-model-baseline.md`. Evidence: `.acceptance/92dcb1db-e855-4741-95e8-dde01ac3f5df/`. No production default selected. Desktop discovery now leaves selection empty and Start disabled until a model is chosen. Six controlled browser tests, TypeScript checks, native build and native discovery/lifecycle smoke pass; native evidence `.acceptance/e6b963d0-4638-4fdb-9434-6c2af2ebcdb8/` confirms 29 actual models and explicit selection. Latest engine suite remains 171 tests from the preceding runtime milestone. Broader reliability/resource evaluation and release gates remain open. Overall estimate remains approximately 10%.

Pinned channel audit expanded: parsed 27 manifests and all tracked platform registration sources without importing adapters; found 28 registered names because WeCom separately registers encrypted HTTP callback mode. Added three core transports, yielding 31 channel-specific requirement rows with shared identity, deduplication, authorization, delivery, cancellation, credential and media gates. Source/class locations, hashes, factory expressions, direct method declarations and configuration-name metadata are recorded in `docs/parity/channel-inventory.json`; declaration/mixin limits and Phase 8 versus full Phase 9 scope are explicit. Corrected generic manifest acceptance references to channel-specific IDs. Home Assistant's README lead resolves to official external plugin commit `4d492b1dc5326af3e38bb2cf3e4b9ad6d65f0072`, not an in-tree adapter. Also scanned all 976 tracked catalog manifests and recorded 49 platform-category candidates with pins, marking known non-channel categories separately rather than claiming 49 more channels. The audit highlights topic-level ntfy identity, tenant/thread isolation and distinct WeCom credential/crypto modes. Both inventory drift checks, explicit strict Pyright checks for the audit scripts, Ruff and formatting pass. No runtime code changed; 171 engine tests remain the latest runtime suite. No message was sent and no channel is marked implemented/verified. External source classification, deeper mixin/method inspection and other Phase 0 capabilities remain open. Overall release estimate remains approximately 10%.

Pinned execution-backend audit expanded: verified the reference checkout's exact commit, parsed the terminal registry and factory without importing upstream code, and recorded six built-in families plus the managed Modal internal mode with source hashes/class locations. Inspected all 976 tracked catalog manifests through a documented candidate rule, reviewed 25 matches and separated eight external backend declarations from 17 related-feature matches. The wider search found WSL terminal and WSLC sandbox declarations missed by the earlier narrow phrase search; pins, subdirectories, platform/configuration declarations and source-inspection limits are explicit in `docs/parity/execution-inventory.json`. `execution-requirements.md` defines eight shared acceptance gates, per-backend scenarios, dependencies, planned modules and phases. Full-access WSL cannot satisfy generated-code isolation; WSLC's declared missing controls require actual measurement before any claim. Local checks found wsl.exe/OpenSSH but no Docker, Podman, wslc, bwrap or Apptainer executable; no restricted remote target/account is configured. The reproducible audit script checks pinned/clean tracked source, ignores untracked catalog additions, rejects unreviewed candidates and detects inventory drift. Its explicit strict Pyright check, Ruff and generated inventory check pass. No runtime code changed in this audit; the preceding 171-test runtime baseline remains the latest engine suite. External pinned source inspection, remaining catalog discovery, channel-specific requirements and other Phase 0 capabilities remain open. No execution backend is marked implemented/verified. Overall release estimate remains approximately 10%.

Interrupted read recovery and review retries implemented: on explicit resume or a new review lease, version-1 built-in file-read/list/search intents without execution markers can be closed transactionally as `interrupted_read` with `effect: read_only`. No old contents are assumed and no operation is replayed; public failure results attach only to matching saved parent proposals, while helper/unmatched results stay internal. New read/list intent events preserve the internal flag. Writes, unknown tools/versions and anomalous execution-marked intents remain unresolved, and lease fencing is checked in the same transaction. Final/specialist review can now recover an interrupted internal verification read and perform fresh checks instead of remaining stuck. All 171 engine tests, Ruff, strict Pyright, OpenAPI/generated contracts and native build pass. Six controlled browser scenarios pass (one live browser scenario skipped).

Real read-crash acceptance: a controlled seeded initial proposal led to an actual file read in a separate worker; the worker was killed after reading but before saving its result. Recovery initially blocked. After explicit resume, real Qwen3.5 9B received the unavailable-result response, proposed a fresh read and reported the random code with unchanged bytes/mtime and no unresolved intent. Evidence: `.acceptance/604a77bd-3395-4776-aa41-dd2b3d811c14/`, including `read-recovery-audit.json`; earlier passing probe `.acceptance/783a1949-223c-41c4-b917-27de7580e0dd/` retained. Diagnostic launcher was converted from blocking Popen to asyncio after lint caught it. Native read-crash/resume UI acceptance remains open.

Combined native regression acceptance passed in `.acceptance/3d355988-2181-4293-8caa-daaacc61d3b2/`: planning, fresh specialist evidence, both fixture reviews, dependent continuation, a second objective in the same window, targeted edit, exact checks, final review and graceful shutdown. First combined attempt `.acceptance/1ec08f07-dfc7-40de-a829-2607757b944b/` completed specialists but exposed unstable implicit textarea labels when refilling Objective. A controlled Edge regression reproduced the failure. Objective and Expected file contents now have explicit labels; regression and rebuilt native rerun pass. Proposal-before-intent crashes, unknown/anomalous intents, directory/metadata retries, restricted commands/validation and broader release gates remain incomplete. Overall estimate remains approximately 10%.

Owned specialist evidence review and write reconciliation verified: specialist acceptance now rechecks selected evidence hashes through internal read workers under a shared ten-second budget, retains fenced task decisions and releases its lease on cancellation/failure. Worker errors/timeouts return conflicts without completing a task or unlocking dependents. Coordinator continuation invokes internal worker reconciliation only for pending write/create intents; matching effects are confirmed without rewriting, while other outcomes remain unresolved. All 163 engine tests, Ruff, strict Pyright, contract checks and frontend/native builds pass. Real native planning, independent specialist reads, fresh evidence rechecks, fixture reviews and dependent continuation passed: `.acceptance/338577a6-63ed-4baa-9e25-1931c1be1ef5/`; `review-worker-audit.json` confirms fresh internal reads before both accepted task decisions. Real process-kill creation recovery passed through source worker (`.acceptance/f8154712-e722-4439-9735-5607b726dea1/`) and frozen reconciliation worker after fsync (`.acceptance/0fd00624-67f0-44ad-bcd6-2f40d5f55e01/`) and before content/execution-event persistence (`.acceptance/c5bb2b71-7626-4d17-843c-ae492b0560f9/`). Both frozen cases record build digests and unchanged mtime; complete effects were acknowledged, empty creation stayed unresolved/conflicting. The synchronous task-verification library helper, directory/metadata retry policy and pure-read intent recovery remain open. Overall estimate remains approximately 10%; restricted commands/validation and broader release gates remain incomplete.

Owned candidate/final-review verification implemented and native-verified: coordinator file checks and final run-acceptance rechecks now use internal read workers, with one ten-second check budget clamped to remaining run time for coordinator checks. Final review is asynchronous, retains its fenced 30-second lease and guarded state/revision commit, and returns a conflict on worker failure/timeout without completing the run. Cancellation releases the lease without accepting. Four new cases cover fresh multi-file reads/internal replay exclusion, error/timeout rejection and review cancellation; all 157 engine tests, Ruff, strict Pyright, OpenAPI/generated contract checks and frontend/native builds pass. Rebuilt native Qwen3.5 9B targeted edit, exact file check, explicit fixture review and graceful shutdown passed: `.acceptance/2dc00828-0b00-471f-8396-167a69d705df/`. Independent `verification-audit.json` confirms an internal candidate read before verification.passed and a new internal read after that event but before final run.reviewed acceptance. Specialist evidence review, synchronous task-verification helper and reconciliation still have in-process filesystem work.
