Saved run history: added authenticated bounded run summaries, stable created-time/ID keyset pagination and literal case-sensitive objective filtering. Summaries cap objective excerpts at 500 characters and exclude lease authority; opening a run retrieves its full record. The desktop now lists, filters, refreshes and paginates saved runs, then replays the selected run's persisted candidate, plan, activity and artifact events. Selection aborts the previous stream and guards late updates; pagination is retained when opening another saved item. All 93 engine tests, Ruff and strict Pyright pass. Tests cover restart, tied timestamps, new insertions between pages, literal wildcard/injection-like query text, invalid cursors, output bounds and authentication. Four controlled Edge scenarios pass (live browser scenario skipped), including opening two saved results and clearing the previous result; screenshot `test-results/history.png` inspected. Production frontend build, four client tests and generated-contract checks pass. Native restart/reopen acceptance, full conversation-content FTS and scoped channel history remain incomplete. Overall estimate approximately 10%.

Explicit final run review: added authenticated accept/reject decisions with expected-revision fencing, objective/criterion confirmations and a required rationale. Acceptance requires a final candidate, no unresolved actions, completed specialist tasks and re-passing every saved file check; review audit and terminal transition commit together under parent ownership. Rejection terminates the run and cancels outstanding child work. Added a desktop final-review panel and regenerated contracts. All 91 engine tests, Ruff, strict Pyright, frontend production build, four client tests and contract drift checks pass; three controlled Edge scenarios pass (live browser scenario skipped). A new panel exposed an ambiguous test label selector, corrected with an exact match. Final-review screenshot visually inspected. Rebuilt native package passed a real Qwen3.5 9B targeted edit, exact file check, preserved neighbor, automated fixture review and persisted `completed` state, followed by graceful shutdown. Read-only audit confirms one local-user acceptance, one re-passed file check and zero unresolved intents. Evidence: `.acceptance/1c3d3f6c-cb38-444f-962c-3f5480bc2da0/` including build digests and `review-audit.json`. This verifies explicit user-decision plumbing via controlled automation, not autonomous reviewer parity. Installer/clean-machine gates and the remaining specification remain incomplete; overall estimate approximately 10%.

Known pre-write failure lifecycle: added durable failed tool results for exclusive-open/preflight/hash failures known to occur before writing. Model replay preserves those results and IDs, letting the coordinator return structured failure feedback and accept a new read/hash/write proposal within the existing budget. Journal failure resolution refuses calls with a `tool.executing` event; post-write/fsync/result-save errors remain intended and require reconciliation. Tests cover a real locked file, stale-hash correction with complete replay and injected fsync failure after bytes changed. All 88 engine tests, Ruff and strict Pyright pass; frontend typecheck passes with readable failure/rejected-plan activity labels. Real Qwen3.5 9B intentionally attempted a stale hash, received one known pre-write failure, reread and completed a new write; exact newline-sensitive verification and neighboring-file preservation passed with no unresolved intents. Evidence: `.acceptance/8f4bd81b-d44f-485f-924d-e27842515960/report.json`. Native rebuild/acceptance and broader read/compound-tool error handling remain incomplete. Overall estimate approximately 10%.

Bounded workspace search: added a schema-declared coordinator tool for literal text and filename search through the verified directory/file readers. It skips links/common generated directories, bounds directory/file counts, per-file bytes, match count and UTF-8 match output, and reports exclusions and every reached limit. Internal discovery/read results stay out of parent conversation replay. Cooperative yields allow cancellation between entries/files; an interrupted search remains an unresolved journal intent. All 86 tests, Ruff and strict Pyright pass, including Unicode case folding, exact line/path evidence, match/file-byte limits, directory/file scan caps, UTF-8 output cap, cancellation and a real external Windows junction. Real Qwen3.5 9B found a fresh marker in an unknown filename, then independently read that file to report a separate random code absent from the search excerpt. File bytes were unchanged. Evidence: `.acceptance/c8a7ea66-4646-4f59-9498-d9e5b82ba231/report.json`. Native rebuild/search acceptance, specialist search exposure, transient read retries and worker-isolated filesystem timeouts remain outstanding. Overall estimate approximately 10%.

Run-scoped tool identity and schema v2: reproduced a real storage failure when two runs used provider call ID `call-0`. Tool journal rows now have independent UUID primary keys plus provider IDs unique within each run; replay and reconciliation preserve the provider's original IDs. Existing primary keys and approval references remain intact during the transactional v1ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Â Ã¢â‚¬â„¢v2 backfill/index migration. Offline restore accepts v1/v2; newer databases are refused without version reset. All 78 tests, Ruff and strict Pyright pass, including cross-run reuse, within-run conflict, restored-v1 migration, legacy replay and approval foreign keys. Rebuilt native package booted an isolated copy of the earlier real v1 acceptance database, discovered 29 models and shut down gracefully with restricted PATH. Read-only comparison confirms every original column/row was preserved, version advanced 1ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Â Ã¢â‚¬â„¢2 and foreign keys remain valid. Evidence: `.acceptance/e6696a25-38dd-4963-bb71-3d281e94f1a7/` including `migration-check.json` and build digests. Added migration/rollback guidance. Installer upgrade and clean-machine migration tests remain incomplete; overall estimate approximately 10%.

Correctable plan proposals and stricter native ledger acceptance: coordinator now validates proposals before side effects and journals explicit rejected tool results for schema, permission or graph/budget errors. The model can correct its proposal without losing tool-call/result continuity; rejected plans create no tasks and leave no uncertain intents. Repeated invalid proposals remain bounded by charged inference attempts. All 75 tests, Ruff formatting/lint and strict Pyright pass. Rebuilt native package passed the full real Qwen3.5 9B specialist/review/dependency scenario under the stricter shared counter, using requested reservations of 2 iterations for inspect and 3 for review. File evidence/bytes, automated fixture review, restricted runtime PATH and graceful shutdown passed. Evidence: `.acceptance/464af0e7-f498-4b89-a2f2-865fd2bc0818/` including executable digests and independent persisted-ledger inspection. Human/autonomous review parity, token ceilings, coding isolation and release installer gates remain incomplete; overall estimate approximately 10%.

Shared iteration accounting: parent requests now reserve a durable inference attempt before contacting the provider, so cancelled/disconnected/unparsed requests consume their allowance. New response events refer to the attempt ID to avoid double charging; legacy response-only records remain countable. Specialists use the same transactional counter. Plan saving rechecks reservations against already-spent attempts and retains one final parent response; the model sees the remaining child allowance on each planning request. Usage reports flag attempts without measured responses as unmeasured. All 71 engine tests, Ruff and strict Pyright pass, including reopen-before-response, legacy/new mixed histories, no double counting, exhausted budget rejection and plan headroom. Native fixture planning instructions now reserve 2 iterations for inspect and 3 for review to fit this stricter ledger; the changed native workflow still needs rebuild/reverification. Token ceilings, diagnostic-call accounting and full resource benchmarking remain unfinished. Overall estimate approximately 10%.

Native specialist/review/continuation acceptance PASSED: rebuilt the native application and frozen engine, then used Qwen3.5 9B to discover a fresh fixture, save a researcherÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Â Ã¢â‚¬â„¢reviewer graph, dispatch the researcher, exercise evidence review controls, dispatch the newly ready reviewer and independently read the same file. Both candidates contained the fresh random code with matching complete file bytes/SHA-256. Automated fixture decisions completed both tasks while the parent remained verifying. SQLite evidence confirms 3 parent model turns plus 4 specialist attempts, within the unchanged 8-iteration run limit. Restricted child PATH, unchanged fixture bytes, 29-model discovery, model unload and graceful native/engine shutdown passed. Evidence: `.acceptance/5181d3d6-4ad4-4771-9b9a-0000bea80fa8/` (report/digests, candidates, graph, screenshot, logs); screenshot inspected. Initial attempt `.acceptance/84be4a1b-5ed9-49f5-9c7c-f8363d9d527f/` failed when the reviewer repeated a read and exhausted its 2-iteration task budget. The next test reserved 3 per task without increasing the parent cap; failed-task diagnostics now preserve bounded underlying exception class names and a specific iteration-limit type. All 68 tests passed; focused specialist checks rerun after diagnostic assertions (4 passed), Ruff and strict Pyright pass. Automated controlled-data review is not autonomous reviewer parity or clean-machine installer validation. Overall estimate remains approximately 10%.

Dependent continuation: added authenticated asynchronous plan dispatch and a desktop Run ready specialists action. Explicitly accepted prerequisites unlock readiness; the shared supervisor owns continuation so pause/cancel/shutdown use the existing control path. Dispatch reserves parent ownership before returning running, preventing duplicate workers; completion returns the run to verifying. Specialists receive bounded prerequisite candidate excerpts and recorded file path/hash references as untrusted assignment context (8 KiB total), while their tools independently inspect actual files. A supervisor callback releases ownership and pauses a dispatch cancelled before coroutine startup. All 68 engine tests, Ruff and strict Pyright pass; frontend production build and generated-contract checks pass. Controlled browser acceptance now covers criterion/evidence review, acceptance, ready-task dispatch and a dependent candidate awaiting review; two controlled scenarios pass and the live browser scenario is skipped. Real-model/native dependent continuation, automatic reviewer behavior and coding isolation remain incomplete. Overall estimate remains approximately 10%.

Explicit user task review: added authenticated candidate/evidence and accept/reject routes plus a desktop review panel. Acceptance requires a nonblank rationale, confirmation of every criterion exactly once and actual file-read journal evidence belonging to the task. The engine acquires parent ownership, re-reads selected files and compares hashes before recording a local-user decision; changed evidence or an active worker prevents acceptance. Accepted tasks become completed, unlocking dependency readiness; rejection marks the task failed. Parent runs remain verifying. No model review tool is exposed. All 66 engine tests, Ruff, strict Pyright, frontend production build and four client tests pass. Two controlled Edge scenarios pass (live browser scenario skipped); review checks include disabled controls, safe candidate text, explicit request payload and retained parent review state. Screenshot `test-results/task-review.png` visually inspected; initial browser fixture missed the MemoryPanel background request and was corrected. Contracts regenerated and drift checks pass. This is explicit human review, not autonomous reviewer parity. Automatic dependent dispatch, real-model/native review acceptance and full artifact coverage remain unfinished; overall estimate approximately 10%.

Specialist interruption/recovery: parent pause/block/failure/approval-wait now transactionally blocks active specialists and deletes their worker leases. Parent cancellation cancels queued/running/verifying children. Abandoned parent recovery fences interrupted tasks even if their child leases have not expired, leaving the run blocked for reconciliation. Task lease schema initialization now belongs to RunStore. New tests exercise authenticated pause/cancel while two specialist requests are actively waiting, confirm both requests cancel and leases disappear, and prove resume does not replay the unresolved dispatch. Reopened-storage recovery tests preserve a live parent, expire only its parent lease, then reject a stale child result even under replacement parent ownership. All 64 tests, Ruff and strict Pyright pass. These checks use deterministic waiting providers and controlled lease expiry; real process-kill/native specialist recovery and an operator reconciliation workflow remain outstanding. Overall estimate remains approximately 10%.

Read-only specialist execution: coordinator now offers `plan_run_ready` after saving/loading a plan. It claims at most two independent tasks, gives each only its own assignment/criteria and read/list tools, maintains parent/task leases and persists candidate results as verifying. Specialist tool call IDs are preserved in separate task events and mapped to globally unique journal IDs; their internal read results do not contaminate parent conversation replay. Inference attempts are charged transactionally against specialist/shared iteration limits before requests, with task wall-clock deadlines and measured usage aggregated into run totals. This does not yet impose token ceilings or account for diagnostics. Tests cover independent reads with identical model call IDs, parent-context separation, unauthorized writes, failed prerequisite blocking, exhausted shared budgets and coordinator dispatch. All 61 tests, Ruff and strict Pyright pass. Real Qwen3.5 9B specialists independently read two fresh random codes correctly; both remain verifying. Evidence: `.acceptance/e42cacf8-d16b-4d46-bdf6-c12c27b80442/report.json`. Independent review/completion, dependency continuation, task restart/cancellation acceptance, isolated coding workers and native packaged dispatch remain unfinished. Overall release estimate remains approximately 10%.

Native planning acceptance PASSED: rebuilt the embedded React/Tauri application and frozen engine, then exercised real Qwen3.5 9B directory discovery and plan saving through the native WebView. The saved graph has two queued tasks with the requested dependency; the UI shows the dependent waiting, and fixture bytes remain unchanged. Child PATH was restricted to System32 and Python environment paths removed. All 29 models were discovered, the loaded model was unloaded, and native close produced confirmed graceful engine shutdown. Executable SHA-256 digests, graph, screenshot and logs are in `.acceptance/67871daf-1c7a-455a-806a-e453ecaaa1a5/`; screenshot visually inspected. Added repeatable procedure in `docs/releases/native-acceptance.md`. This verifies the development package on this machine, not an installer or clean-machine release. PyInstaller still warns that tzdata is absent; timezone scheduling is not implemented. Overall estimate remains approximately 10%.

Desktop plan view: added typed public plan/task records and regenerated OpenAPI/TypeScript contracts. The desktop fetches a run's initial plan and refreshes it on plan/task events, showing role, dependency readiness, model, workspace, permissions, reserved budgets and acceptance criteria. Queued tasks are explicitly shown as not started. API tests confirm parent/task lease tokens and internal result/checkpoint fields are not exposed. Controlled Edge acceptance verifies an empty initial plan updates after `plan.saved`, dependencies/readiness render correctly and model-written script markup remains inert text. Screenshot visually inspected at `test-results/objective.png`. All 57 engine tests, Ruff, strict Pyright, production frontend build, four client tests and controlled browser acceptance pass; opt-in live browser test skipped. Contract drift checks pass. Native packaged acceptance remains outstanding; approximately 10% overall.

Real model planning PASSED: added schema-validated `plan_save` to the workspace coordinator. Model proposals must explicitly provide dependencies and nonempty acceptance criteria; saved plans inherit the parent model/workspace and pass graph, permission and aggregate reservation checks. The tool is removed once a plan exists. Qwen3.5 9B discovered a fresh random filename using the real Windows directory tool, then saved researcher/reviewer tasks with the requested dependency; all tasks remained queued and file bytes were unchanged. Evidence: `.acceptance/113d3d12-a6ac-4341-9ef3-11a215e3b88b/report.json`. Initial run omitted the dependency/repeated a save, prompting stricter schema/tool availability; a second acceptance failure was the fixture's Windows newline translation, fixed by writing deterministic bytes. All 56 engine tests, Ruff formatting/lint and strict Pyright pass. Specialist dispatch, aggregate measured budget consumption, independent completion review and desktop plan rendering remain incomplete. Overall release estimate remains approximately 10%.

Workspace discovery: coordinator now offers a journaled directory-listing tool, bounded to 256 entries and 32 KiB of entry data. Windows enumerates the verified opened directory with `GetFileInformationByHandleEx`, avoiding a path-based second enumeration after containment checks. Links/junctions are labeled without traversal; requests resolving outside the workspace are rejected. Actual Windows tests cover Unicode names, 300-file truncation, a real external junction and a scripted model discovery round trip through the coordinator. All 54 engine tests, Ruff and strict Pyright pass. A real LM Studio discovery scenario, isolated tool timeout/cancellation, recursive search and model-produced plan integration remain outstanding. Overall release estimate remains approximately 10%.

Specialist file inspection: candidate task outputs can now be checked against explicit exact-content requirements through the existing permission-checked, journaled file reader. Results and requirement snapshots are stored in task checkpoints with durable verification events under the parent lease. The test submits a false success summary, observes actual-file failure, corrects the file and observes passing evidence after reopening storage. Checks do not mark the task completed or imply that natural-language criteria passed. All 51 engine tests, Ruff and strict Pyright pass. Scheduler/reviewer integration and release acceptance remain incomplete; overall estimate remains approximately 10%.

Specialist worker ownership: added transactional distinct-task claims, renewable fenced task leases, candidate-or-failure submissions and expired-worker recovery. Workers cannot mark their own results completed. Lost workers become blocked for reconciliation; their tasks are not automatically replayed. Tests cover two stores claiming different tasks, mismatched/expired tokens, failed dependency blocking, live renewal, stale-result rejection and idempotent recovery. All 50 engine tests, Ruff and strict Pyright pass. This is storage-level orchestration infrastructure; model planning, actual specialist dispatch, independent review and isolated integration remain incomplete. Overall release estimate remains approximately 10%.

Durable task plans: added transactional plan/dependency persistence under the live parent lease, revalidation against saved permissions/budget, parent model/workspace inheritance and rejection of silent plan replacement. Reopened storage reconstructs readiness; authenticated `/runs/{id}/plan` exposes saved tasks and dependency status. Forty-nine tests and static checks pass; generated contracts updated. Model plan creation and actual specialist scheduling/execution remain incomplete.

Task graph validation: added immutable specialist task definitions, cycle/unknown-dependency validation, permission subset enforcement and aggregate iteration/time reservations. Scheduler readiness identifies independent queued tasks and propagates failed prerequisites transitively. Forty-eight tests and static checks pass; task-graph checks were rerun after freezing definitions. Persistence, model planning, specialist dispatch, isolated editing/integration and resource accounting across actual workers remain incomplete; no delegation parity is claimed.

Live preference recall/deletion PASSED: Qwen3.5 9B returned a fresh explicitly saved random label in one new run; after deletion, another new run received no recalled IDs, returned unavailable and did not disclose the deleted label. FTS retrieval was empty. Evidence: `.acceptance/2ef31a86-c0f9-424f-93df-56a9637fdac0/`. The first attempt exposed incorrectly advertised workspace tools on a no-workspace run; coordinator now offers no such tools and skips tool-use diagnostics when no workspace tools are enabled. Forty-six tests and static checks pass, including a no-tools recall assertion. Native memory UI/recall still needs rebuild and acceptance; model final answers remain candidates awaiting review.

Memory interface: added a collapsible preference panel with search, save, edit, delete and JSON export through generated API types. Controlled Edge acceptance exercises the full create/edit/export/delete flow, plus objective submission/artifact preview. The browser check exposed and fixed a textarea label association issue in edit mode. Frontend production build, TypeScript and four Node client tests pass; controlled browser acceptance passes with the opt-in live test skipped. Native memory/recall acceptance remains unverified until rebuild.

Preference recall: coordinator now includes only local-user, user-provenance preference records before the current objective, explicitly subordinate to the objective and application permissions. Recall is bounded to 4 KiB and audits IDs/omitted counts without duplicating preference content into recall events. Tests verify deleted preferences disappear from new-run context, other scopes/tool knowledge are excluded and oversized preferences are reported omitted. Forty-six tests and static checks pass. Live recall, UI memory controls, channel-scoped identity and native rebuild remain outstanding.

Memory API: authenticated scoped search/create/edit/delete/export routes now expose typed records. Preference provenance is assigned by application code; request bodies cannot forge it. An API test covers authorization, provenance rejection, editing/removal from search and export. Forty-four tests and static checks pass; API and TypeScript contracts regenerated. The current desktop-only scope is local-user; channel/account identity mapping, UI controls and coordinator recall remain incomplete.

Explicit memory storage: added user-provenance preferences with scoped CRUD and SQLite FTS5 search. Transactional triggers update/remove indexed content with edits/deletions; a reopen/isolation test verifies persistence, scope separation, changed-term removal and deletion from retrieval. Forty-three tests, Ruff and Pyright pass. API/UI controls, export and coordinator recall remain incomplete; this module does not promote tool/web text into trusted instructions.

Desktop artifact preview: added typed artifact listings and authenticated text previews capped at 64 KiB, cancelling the upstream stream at the cap. React renders artifact text safely, including script-like content. Four Node client tests, controlled Edge acceptance, frontend build and static checks pass; the browser test confirms script text is displayed without execution. The opt-in live browser test was skipped in this controlled run. Native packaging and artifact backup coverage remain incomplete.

Git coordinator integration: models can request validated status/diff inspection; output is schema-checked, limited to a 4 KiB excerpt and linked to a registered full artifact. Added metadata for schemas, permission, version, timeout, cancellation, retries and auditing. A scripted-provider test exercises real Git invocation through the coordinator and returns its saved artifact to the model. Forty-two tests and static checks pass. Live Git-model/native acceptance, desktop artifact viewing and complete workspace tool metadata remain outstanding.

Artifact integration: Git output now registers durable metadata with provenance, bounded size and SHA-256 under a live worker lease. Authenticated run-artifact listing and attachment downloads enforce managed/opened-path containment plus size/hash integrity. A real API test verifies authentication, exact download bytes, listing and tamper rejection. Forty-one tests and static checks pass; OpenAPI/TypeScript regenerated. Artifact backups, typed artifact DTOs and desktop file viewing remain incomplete.

Git inspection primitive: added asynchronous fixed-argument status/diff commands, disabled external diff/textconv/fsmonitor/hooks, sanitized inherited Git environment, bounded execution/output and saved artifacts. A real fixture-repository test verifies status/diff. Forty tests and static checks pass. Docker is unavailable (`docker` command not found); arbitrary generated commands await a restricted backend. Model dispatch, artifact database registration/retrieval and full tool metadata remain incomplete.

Live targeted edit PASSED: Qwen3.5 9B explicitly used workspace_read_file then workspace_replace_text, producing the exact required final newline and leaving the neighbor unchanged (`.acceptance/0100944d-edce-4a56-8315-8fc9c1870d9d/`). Rebuilt the packaged engine/native executable and verified the same operation through the hidden native UI, including independent checks, automatic bootstrap and graceful close (`.acceptance/00c72195-af8c-4eb3-a7b2-a31a809abbea/`). Saved native model messages confirm replacement-tool use. Static checks and both contract drift checks pass. Full-rewrite model failures remain preserved; this does not establish terminal/coding validation, orchestration or installer parity.

Explicit internal-result metadata: new tool completion events identify verifier/helper reads directly, so replay no longer classifies current results by call-ID naming. Regression cases preserve legitimate model IDs with verification-like prefixes or source-like suffixes. Older events lacking the field retain legacy replay compatibility. Thirty-nine tests and static checks pass. Native executables need rebuilding to include this journal refinement.

Targeted edit tool: workspace-mode models may now replace one unique text occurrence using the current file hash. The executor preserves all surrounding UTF-8 bytes and newline style, rejects invalid UTF-8/ambiguous matches and delegates the resulting full content through the existing exclusive/journaled write path. Internal source reads are excluded from model result replay. Thirty-seven tests and static checks pass, including CRLF/comment preservation. This expands editing capability; original full-rewrite acceptance failures remain recorded. Live replacement and rebuilt native acceptance remain outstanding.

Rebuilt native editing bundle; acceptance FAILED. The hidden desktop submitted the workspace edit but never reached its passed-file-check event; its saved run blocked with iteration_budget_exhausted, leaving `value=2` without the required final newline. Evidence/database: `.acceptance/0646ae26-6b58-4f2d-9b1e-5d7dba534837/`. The smoke script now preserves failure reports in future runs and cleans its owned app/model. Both contract drift checks passed. Native editing is not claimed verified; model reliability and actionable correction handling still need improvement.

Correction-loop integration verified: a scripted provider intentionally omits the newline, receives persisted independent-check feedback, rereads the current hash and performs a second edit that passes. Six model turns remain within the original budget; tool/result replay stays valid. Added optional exact-file criteria to the React form and readable verification status messages. Thirty-six engine tests, frontend build/typecheck and controlled Edge browser acceptance pass, including preservation of a trailing newline in the submitted check. The default browser run explicitly skips the opt-in live test; packaged native editing still needs rebuild and acceptance.

Live model edit acceptance PASSED with the same exact-content/neighbor criteria: installed Qwen3.5 9B at 16,384 context read the fixture, supplied its hash, wrote `value=2` plus the required final newline, and reached independent verification with a resolved journal. Evidence: `.acceptance/d27e6a2a-0ee0-4bdf-9c62-e3345a6e3730/`. Earlier failures remain preserved. The larger context removed the prior conservative budget barrier; this passing run used one write and does not by itself prove a correction retry. Also hardened exact-content checks to compare UTF-8 byte hashes, preventing invalid UTF-8 replacement characters from producing false success. Thirty-five tests and static checks pass. Feedback-loop integration and rebuilding native editing support remain outstanding.

Restored-candidate verification fixed: saved final answers now pass through the same file checks as fresh candidates, and mismatch feedback remains subject to consumed iteration limits. A regression test confirms an incorrect restored answer blocks rather than bypassing verification. Thirty-four tests and static checks pass. Live 9B remediation still FAILED (`.acceptance/b442c93c-065d-4d9a-8c7a-d53253f6d4de/`); the missing newline remains and the run now blocks instead of leaving an unchecked candidate. No completion criterion was relaxed.

Independent artifact checks: API/run storage now accepts explicit expected file-content checks. Fresh coordinator candidate answers trigger journaled file reads; mismatches produce persisted feedback and return execution to running within the original iteration budget. Exact-content verification detects the missing-newline case and passes only after the actual file matches. Verification-only reads are excluded from model tool-call replay. Thirty-three tests, static/type checks and regenerated contracts pass. Restored-candidate verification, feedback/retry integration acceptance and the live model edit rerun remain outstanding; natural-language criteria still require independent review and passing structured checks does not automatically complete a run.

Live edit acceptance remains FAILED: Qwen3 0.6B supplied a wrong expected hash and was safely denied (`.acceptance/e05531fb-7920-4f95-ab68-aea74b673d11/`). Installed Qwen3.5 9B performed the hash-checked edit but omitted the required final newline on two attempts, including an explicit JSON-string prompt (`.acceptance/5ec53d81-186f-49d2-888d-fc381f56336f/`, `.acceptance/067aef08-e12d-490a-ab27-e1c439e58e54/`). Stored tool arguments confirm the model supplied `value=2` without newline; the executor faithfully wrote seven bytes. Neighboring files stayed unchanged. No acceptance criterion was relaxed. Next work is independent artifact verification with actionable mismatch feedback, rather than treating a candidate answer as completion. Diagnostic script lint/format and Pyright pass; no default model was selected and no model was downloaded.

Workspace mode dispatch: coordinator now offers read/hash-checked existing-file edits only for workspace-mode runs, with executor permission enforcement as a second boundary. Added explicit mode selection in React and CLI; Observe remains the default. A scripted-provider integration test reads a real file, uses its returned hash, edits it and stops for verification. Thirty-two tests and frontend build/type checks pass. Live model edit acceptance and rebuilding the staged native bundle with this feature remain outstanding; current packaged executables still contain the earlier read-only engine.

Real interrupted-write acceptance passed: `scripts/write_crash_diagnostic.py` killed a separate Windows worker after file fsync and before result commit. Expired-run recovery correctly blocked the uncertain action; explicit reconciliation then confirmed the desired hash, persisted its result and did not change the file timestamp or execute the write again. Evidence: `.acceptance/13baedb5-2f4b-4659-8f12-2c6c493a633a/`. Script formatting/lint and Pyright pass. This covers the existing-file write boundary only, not arbitrary commands/external services or all Phase 2 recovery invariants.

Interrupted-edit reconciliation: the executor inspects uncertain write destinations under exclusive access and records success only when the current bounded content exactly matches the desired hash. Unchanged or conflicting/partial contents remain unresolved and are never rewritten automatically. Coordinator recovery invokes this reconciliation before checking unresolved intents. Three interruption simulations cover applied, unchanged and partial states without modifying destination timestamps during reconciliation. Thirty-one tests and static checks pass. Real process-kill acceptance and model/UI edit exposure remain incomplete.

Edit contention protection: existing-file edits now hold an exclusive Windows CreateFile handle (no read/write/delete sharing) throughout hash validation, write and fsync. POSIX uses advisory flock and is not claimed equivalent to Windows mandatory sharing. A competing-handle test verifies refusal with unchanged contents. Twenty-eight tests, Ruff and Pyright pass. Model exposure still waits on interrupted-write reconciliation and complete tool lifecycle/error handling.

Workspace-edit primitive: reads now report a hash for complete bounded files. Added a 64 KiB existing-file edit operation requiring workspace mode and the expected previous SHA-256, checking the opened handle and journaling intent/result around a flushed write. A real-file test verifies Observe-mode denial, persisted edit result and stale-hash refusal. Twenty-seven tests and static checks pass. This edit is not yet offered to the model/UI: concurrent-writer exclusion, interruption/reconciliation acceptance and tool descriptors still need completion before enabling it.

Windows crash containment verified: the desktop joins a kill-on-close Windows Job Object before spawning any children, retaining the non-inherited job handle for its lifetime. Normal hidden-native shutdown still passes (`.acceptance/707a1fce-d805-4e8c-9c7f-3013ee716c55/`). A separate test terminated only the desktop PID (without /T); the OS stopped the owned engine and its endpoint disappeared (`.acceptance/b5a3c88a-211f-4647-b7c7-3433e7c4b1c8/`). Cargo build/format pass. This contains process lifetime; it does not provide the restricted execution sandbox required for untrusted generated code.

Native graceful shutdown verified: rebuilt both executables and reran hidden WebView acceptance. Automatic bootstrap discovered 29 models, window close stopped the engine, and the new log segment confirmed both Application shutdown complete and Finished server process. Evidence: `.acceptance/a3d05b82-512d-4f64-87b4-bae93557ac64/`. Both generated-contract drift checks pass. Current rough release estimate is about 10%, reflecting the verified read-only desktop slice; no phase or full-release completion is implied. Workspace editing/commands, independent verification, orchestration, memory/skills, schedules/channels, installer lifecycle and full parity audit remain substantial work.

Graceful shutdown implementation: added authenticated shutdown that cancels/joins workers before signalling Uvicorn exit. Tauri requests this shutdown, waits up to six seconds and retains forced-stop fallback. A blocking-worker API test verifies authentication, persisted pause and released lease before the server callback fires. Twenty-six tests, static checks and Cargo check pass; contracts regenerated. The packaged engine/native bundle must still be rebuilt and retested before graceful native shutdown is claimed verified.

Native smoke acceptance: hidden WebView2 test verified automatic bootstrap, 29 discovered models, native-window close and owned-engine shutdown. The first run exposed that state destructors were insufficient for Tauri exit; moved cleanup to the explicit RunEvent::Exit handler, cleaned the known orphaned test process tree and reran successfully. Evidence: `.acceptance/f60be50d-52d5-4c4c-938a-c38d162b2522/`. Forced termination is still the current shutdown policy; graceful cancellation/flush, crash containment and installer tests remain incomplete.

Native executable built: Cargo successfully linked the Windows desktop with the React production assets embedded through Tauri's custom protocol. Staged desktop/engine executables together under `build/native-dev` with SHA-256 manifest; added a repeatable `npm run build:native:dev` pipeline. This is a development bundle, not an installer or clean-machine release. Runtime launch/close verification remains outstanding.

Native engine ownership: Tauri now starts its packaged engine hidden, chooses a free loopback port, creates a fresh in-memory token, writes engine output to app logs and exposes connection data via a local native command. React detects Tauri and connects automatically with bounded startup retries. Shell teardown stops its owned engine process tree. Cargo check/format and React production build pass; actual native launch/exit and crash cleanup remain unverified. Port reservation currently has a bind handoff race and forced shutdown still needs a graceful stop path before release.

Bundled engine: pinned PyInstaller in the dev lock and added an executable build script. The Windows one-file engine executable built successfully and served authenticated model discovery from an isolated working directory with PATH/PYTHONPATH cleared, proving this check did not need source imports or a Python executable on PATH. Its owned process tree was stopped after testing. Native shell sidecar startup, bootstrap token exchange, graceful shutdown and installer acceptance remain incomplete. Build warned that tzdata is absent; scheduling/timezone packaging must address this before that feature ships.

Native shell: added a Tauri 2 Windows shell for the existing React UI, restricted its CSP to local engine access and generated an original minimal Windows icon. Cargo resolved/locked dependencies; `cargo check --manifest-path apps/desktop/src-tauri/Cargo.toml` passes on the installed MSVC Rust toolchain after adding the required icon. Native launch, managed engine startup/token bootstrap, bundled Python runtime and installer remain incomplete; no packaged-app claim is made.

Live browser slice verified: Edge connected to a separately launched authenticated engine, discovered and selected installed Qwen3 0.6B, submitted a file-read objective, and rendered the exact fresh random code returned through the real model/tool journal. Both controlled and live browser tests passed; Vite production build/typecheck pass. Candidate answers and activity now use readable text rather than raw event JSON. The diagnostic unloaded its model and stopped its owned engine. Native Tauri lifecycle, installer, independent completion verification and broader workspace tools remain incomplete.

Browser verification: a real Edge/Playwright test now exercises connection, generated model selection, objective submission and persisted-event rendering against controlled API responses. It exposed and fixed a native fetch receiver bug that build checks missed. Browser test, three Node client tests and TypeScript checks pass; screenshot saved at `test-results/objective.png`. This is browser evidence with mocked transport, not a live browser-to-LM-Studio workflow or native installer acceptance.

First React interface: added engine connection with in-memory token, model discovery, workspace/objective submission, active state/event activity and pause/resume/cancel controls backed by the real API. Vite production build and TypeScript checks pass. Optional XAA_UI_ORIGIN config permits an explicitly chosen development origin. Native Tauri shell, rendered/browser verification, reconnect behavior and full desktop onboarding remain incomplete; this is not yet a packaged desktop app.

Desktop event client: authenticated event consumption now supports caller-supplied reconnect cursors, cancellation, bounded buffering, fragmented UTF-8/CRLF frames, duplicate suppression and sequence-gap rejection. Three Node tests and TypeScript checks pass, including byte-by-byte Unicode event delivery from a saved cursor. Automatic reconnect scheduling and rendered UI state remain incomplete.

Desktop client layer: added a generated-type-backed API client for objective submission, run lookup and pause/resume/cancel. It holds credentials only in memory, rejects non-loopback URLs, forbids redirects and accepts cancellation signals for create/read requests. Two Node client tests and TypeScript checks pass; Windows CI includes them. UI, bootstrap authentication and reconnecting event consumption remain incomplete.

Dependency investigation: Redocly 2.73.0 removes the minimatch audit chain but breaks openapi-typescript's private-path import. Reverted the incompatible override; contract/type checks pass again. Three moderate dev-dependency findings remain, documented with exact versions and the failed fix in `docs/releases/dependency-issues.md`. No release-ready dependency claim is made.

TypeScript contracts: pinned openapi-typescript and TypeScript with npm lockfile, generated desktop API declarations directly from OpenAPI, and added generation drift/type checks locally and in Windows CI. `npm run contracts:check` and `npm run typecheck` pass. npm reports three moderate development-dependency audit findings; their dependency paths need resolution before release. No desktop UI is implemented by this change.

Public response contracts: run lifecycle routes now validate against RunRecord/RunLimits DTOs, and events/usage expose typed response schemas. Internal lease authority is excluded from public run records. OpenAPI was regenerated; a test confirms a live lease token is absent from the authenticated API response. Twenty-five tests, Ruff and Pyright pass. TypeScript generation remains outstanding.

API contract export: added generated `contracts/openapi.json`, explicit bearer-security metadata and a deterministic export/check script. Windows CI now rejects schema drift. Export/check, Ruff, Pyright and all 24 tests pass locally. Response DTOs and generated TypeScript client types still need refinement before the desktop contract gate is complete.

Context preflight: native loaded-instance context lengths are cached and checked before chat/stream inference using a conservative byte/template/output-reserve estimate. Oversized input is rejected before inference and coordinator runs block with an explicit reason, preserving whole tool/result sequences. Twenty-four tests and static checks pass. This estimate is not tokenizer-exact; unknown loaded contexts, compaction/retrieval, adaptive output reserves and server context changes still require handling.

Token accounting: coordinator model events now persist strictly validated provider usage (including incomplete responses), and authenticated `/runs/{id}/usage` aggregates prompt/completion/total tokens plus unmeasured-call counts across restarts. Twenty-three tests and static checks pass. Diagnostic overhead, delegated totals and enforced token ceilings remain incomplete; these are observed provider totals rather than a tokenizer-based hard budget.

Run budgets: API requests can now persist iteration and elapsed-wall-time limits. Coordinator clamps caller limits to the stored budget, counts prior model turns and measures wall time from the first recorded planning transition across restarts (including pauses). Diagnostic/inference waits use the remaining time. A one-turn-budget test proves execution stops before another model turn. Twenty-three tests and static checks pass. Token/resource accounting and tool execution time limits remain incomplete.

Offline restore: `xaa restore SNAPSHOT --destination NEW_DATABASE` validates schema, required tables, integrity and foreign keys, builds a separate copy and publishes it without overwriting an existing destination. A restore/reopen test verifies saved records and existing-destination refusal without data changes. Twenty-two tests, Ruff and Pyright pass. Artifact contents, retention and real crash recovery remain incomplete.

Backup slice: authenticated `POST /backups` and `xaa backup` create managed online SQLite snapshots, verify database integrity and foreign-key consistency, then publish the completed snapshot atomically. A snapshot-reopen test verifies exact run/event recovery and snapshot isolation from later writes. Fixed per-connection foreign-key enforcement and added refusal to open a newer schema. Twenty-one tests, Ruff and Pyright pass. A complete offline restore workflow, artifact-content backups, retention policy and process-crash acceptance remain incomplete.

Repository setup: initialized the X.A.A. Git repository on main, added generated-file/credential exclusions and LF normalization, and added Windows CI for locked dependency sync, Ruff lint/format, Pyright and pytest. Preserved JSON acceptance reports are eligible for tracking; source checkout, environments, SQLite databases and service logs are excluded. Applied the formatter to existing Python source. Local locked dependency check, lint/type checks and all 20 tests pass. The CI workflow has not been run on a hosted runner and no remote repository has been configured.

Cancellation investigation: added an identical pre-cancellation baseline and tightened the check to exact READY responses. Qwen3 0.6B passed the baseline but failed afterward (`.acceptance/be0b65d8-97e5-4160-a7eb-b4e24e124db0/`). Installed Qwen2.5 0.5B passed baseline, active-stream cancellation, queue release and the exact successor response (`.acceptance/09011911-7986-4b3f-bd7f-7b15100d0be8/`). This is per-model evidence; Qwen3 post-cancellation compatibility remains unresolved and no default model is selected on this basis. No models were downloaded.

Live cancellation diagnostic added: two real Qwen3 0.6B attempts cancelled an active stream after one chunk, propagated cancellation and completed a successor request through the same provider. Both failed the required READY-answer check even after an explicit system instruction; therefore the diagnostic remains FAILED and Phase 1 acceptance is not closed. Evidence: `.acceptance/e04c99f8-17eb-4ee3-bb28-f75ddb17d2ad/` and `.acceptance/a8b20f8e-c337-4f38-9dfa-1ff23badf75f/`. Ruff/Pyright pass. Next investigation should distinguish model instruction-following behavior from any post-cancellation server state issue using a baseline request and another installed model.

Credential storage: the engine now reads its optional LM Studio token from Windows Credential Manager via an explicit OS-vault backend. CLI set/delete commands use private input; credentials are not stored in SQLite or printed. A real temporary vault credential successfully completed write/read/delete verification and was removed afterward. Twenty tests, Ruff and Pyright pass; dependency lock updated. Desktop bootstrap-token management and non-Windows vault adapters remain incomplete.

CLI client: installed `xaa` entry point submits objectives and controls/observes runs through the authenticated API, with no second execution loop. A real hidden engine process and real CLI process successfully discovered 29 LM Studio models, using isolated data and a fresh token. The service was terminated after the check; logs are under `.acceptance/32173d4e-aabe-4eba-be76-f249e85881f2/`. CLI help, Ruff and Pyright pass. README now documents engine/CLI setup and reflects actual progress.

Pinned parity inventory: inspected actual platform manifests and terminal backend registry at the pinned commit. Saved 27 channel manifest records, required configuration names, phases and end-to-end acceptance requirements in `docs/parity/pinned-inventory.json`, plus three core gateway transports and seven reserved terminal names (including managed_modal mode). External plugin capabilities and detailed behavior audit remain incomplete; no adapter parity is claimed.

Model controls: authenticated provider status, per-model tool diagnostics and native load/unload API routes are present. Lifecycle operations now share the inference lock and invalidate cached diagnostics after model changes. Twenty tests pass, including the useful API error for failed tool diagnostics; Ruff and Pyright pass. Persistent provider profiles, OS credential storage and desktop onboarding remain incomplete.

Tool-use gating: the coordinator now runs a harmless structured echo diagnostic for the selected model before offering workspace tools. It validates the requested function, exact JSON arguments, call ID and correct final echo, caching success only within that provider instance. Nineteen tests and static checks pass; the live coordinator diagnostic was rerun with this gate. Diagnostic persistence, UI/API exposure and explicit negative-model acceptance remain outstanding.

Completion contracts: coordinator responses now validate assistant role, tool-call structure, function argument type and unique per-response call IDs before dispatch. Length-limited/filtered responses emit an incomplete event and block instead of becoming replayable final candidates; empty final answers are rejected. Nineteen tests, Ruff and Pyright pass. Broader malformed-provider and recovery acceptance remains in progress.

Opened-file containment: workspace reads now check the actual Windows handle destination using GetFinalPathNameByHandleW before consuming bytes (Linux uses /proc/self/fd; unsupported platforms fail closed). A redirected-open test simulates a destination change after path validation and verifies denial before a completed result is journaled. Seventeen tests and static checks pass. Full Windows junction stress tests and restricted worker isolation remain required.

Durable event delivery: added authenticated SSE replay/live stream with persisted event IDs, reconnect cursors, keepalives and terminal draining. A reconnect test verifies that the saved cancellation event is delivered after cursor 1 without replaying event 1. Sixteen tests, Ruff and Pyright pass. The desktop client and long-running reconnect/load acceptance scenarios remain outstanding.

Startup recovery: the supervisor now scans for dispatchable queued runs and expired planning/running ownership periods. Recovery preserves live workers, requeues abandoned runs with confirmed journals, blocks uncertain tool intents and records a single recovery event transactionally. Verification candidates remain waiting in `verifying`. Fifteen tests pass, including lease-preservation and repeated-scan checks; real process-kill acceptance around external writes remains outstanding.

Context replay: resumed coordinator runs now reconstruct saved model messages and matching confirmed tool results, retain consumed iteration count and avoid requesting inference again for a saved final candidate. A stored tool request without a confirmed result blocks with `incomplete_tool_sequence`. A database-reopen test covers the interrupted request boundary. Fourteen tests and static checks pass; automatic recovery of expired active runs remains outstanding.

API dispatch: runs with an explicit model now dispatch through a process-owned supervisor. Pause/cancel stop the active coordinator task after recording the requested state, resume resubmits queued work, and service shutdown cancels and joins workers before closing the provider. A blocking-provider API test confirms actual dispatch, pause cancellation and lease release. Thirteen tests pass; Ruff and Pyright pass. Durable startup dispatch/recovery and replaying saved context remain outstanding.

Live coordinator evidence: `uv run python scripts/live_coordinator_diagnostic.py` passed against the installed `qwen3-0.6b`. The real model requested one structured workspace-file read, received its journaled result and returned the fresh file's exact random code. Run `d69ebdf9-a8a9-4def-ab74-fad8fd75039a` remained in `verifying`. Evidence and SQLite events are preserved in `.acceptance/1dd96f07-9d5c-4d38-a9f7-02d009673f2e/`. The diagnostic unloaded its model instance afterward. Ruff and Pyright pass. This proves a live read-only objective/tool/result slice, not the full workspace or recovery acceptance gates.

Coordinator integration: a bounded async loop now loads a saved objective/model, acquires and renews its lease, persists model messages, validates structured workspace-read calls, journals their execution and returns matching tool-call IDs/results to the model. Candidate final answers stop in `verifying`; unresolved tool intents block execution. A scripted-provider integration test exercises a real fixture-file read and confirms persisted messages, resolved journal and released lease. Twelve tests pass; live coordinator acceptance, API dispatch, recovery context reconstruction, diagnostics gating and independent verification are still outstanding.

Inference queue: chat and streaming calls on a shared LM Studio provider now serialize through one cancellable lock, and stream cleanup closes its upstream generator. A deterministic contention test proves a cancelled waiter never enters inference and later requests still proceed. Eleven tests pass; live cancellation and hardware-aware admission remain outstanding.

Workspace execution slice: `WorkspaceExecutor.read_file` validates strict arguments, derives the workspace/mode from the saved run, resolves paths, rejects an existing outside-workspace target, bounds content to 64 KiB and marks it untrusted, then journals intent/result under the live lease. A real fixture-file test verifies read, journal resolution, duplicate-action refusal and parent-directory escape rejection. Ten tests plus static checks pass. Windows handle-based boundary enforcement against concurrent reparse-point changes, worker isolation, timeout/cancellation, tool descriptors and coordinator invocation remain required before claiming secure workspace tooling.

Tool journal: lease-guarded intent and result writes now commit with durable events. Reopening an unresolved intent returns it for reconciliation; repeating its idempotency key does not authorize re-execution, and changing arguments under the same key is rejected. Nine tests pass. This proves storage behavior only; real tool execution and destination reconciliation remain to be integrated.

Lease tokens: acquisition now returns a unique token for each ownership period. Renewal requires that live token; event writes, owned transitions and release reject tokens from earlier ownership periods even when the worker name is reused. Eight tests pass with an explicit same-name takeover case; Ruff and Pyright pass. These primitives still need integration into the execution coordinator.

Lease follow-up: worker event writes and owner-scoped state transitions now validate a live lease inside the same SQLite transaction. An expiry/takeover test proves the stale worker cannot append results or change state, while the new owner can proceed. Eight tests pass; coordinator integration and generation tokens for repeated owner identities remain outstanding.

Approximately 7% of the full release objective. This rough project-level estimate reflects a working provider slice, local API, and initial durable coordinator records; it is not a phase completion claim. Worker recovery, safe workspace tools, orchestration, desktop, memory workflows, schedules, channel integrations, and release packaging remain incomplete.

Rebuilt canonical-history native regression (2026-11-04): `.acceptance/d31e0150-767a-45fa-8d08-0934ca900b7c/` passes real model-generated plan/specialist quota enforcement, UI blocked-state explanation, restricted runtime PATH and graceful shutdown. `journal-audit.json` checks every raw persisted parent/specialist model message for public-only fields. Desktop SHA-256 `46afe4e8787c5ff3534f0d71cafec27cd0d789e9a4ffc6453ab41743a0d502a7`; engine `f48fdd7f4ee3322b6081ae4d6d48e484607cdae8710811e9036e2a446280d9e4`. This run does not claim native automatic token counting, streaming or installer acceptance.

Native automatic-admission regression (2026-11-04): `.acceptance/25976223-dda9-46ec-899e-75c9a4a96ea7/` passed with `XAA_EXACT_CONTEXT=1` and `XAA_NATIVE_TASK_QUOTA=1` (recorded in `invocation.json`). Real plan/specialist quota blocking, 29-model discovery, restricted runtime PATH and graceful engine shutdown passed. Desktop SHA-256 `a57877f5d1bec177e86f56540f868653f5eff718e3695df99f209e3caf0c16e0`; engine `f72fe16b552ca878139dd15e1e033a193e8865b011dd2f82521a01b422a3e4a0`. Full suite: 255 passed, one existing Starlette/httpx warning; a subsequent default streamed-cap verification regression also passed (six exact-context tests total). Ruff/format, strict engine and diagnostic Pyright and TypeScript pass. Native streaming and installer acceptance are not claimed by this run.

Failed native trial retained at `.acceptance/a31814bb-5aef-4335-b4a2-39c39f1d7fc8/`: the model called plan execution during the planning-only fixture, so the harness timed out waiting for verifying before manual dispatch. The database instead records the parent blocked, both tasks blocked, five parent/diagnostic holds and no specialist model reply; reservation denial contained the tiny specialist allowance. This is failed planning/manual-dispatch acceptance, not a pass. The subsequent fixture explicitly forbids `plan_run_ready` after saving.

Passing native reservation/quota regression: `.acceptance/163b5d57-a421-4e1e-b54c-c52337e5cdb4/` with exact context enabled uses a real model-generated saved plan followed by manual dispatch. The one-token specialist allowance rejects counted prompt/output before prediction; no specialist reply, tool intent or dependent inference occurs. Both blocked reasons appear in the desktop, fixture bytes remain unchanged, restricted PATH and graceful shutdown pass. Desktop SHA-256 `09f0c990e77488e9d55fb288f4907ecc16f5547373af7aad61b8bb033fea7574`; engine `f30b2b95dd5475a3e85558198170b9e3c94b44bf65fef3b7463aab9cbf8a7c32`. The failed earlier planning-only trial remains separately documented. All 26 targeted tokenizer/admission/reservation cases pass after the final two specialist tests; full suite previously passed 260, static Ruff/format and strict engine/diagnostic Pyright pass. This does not establish all-model counting equivalence, default enablement or full hard-budget parity.

Hosted CI follow-up (2026-11-04): the first GitHub Windows run for 03a956d reached uv setup/sync and Ruff successfully, then failed at `uv run pyright engine scripts`. Earlier local typing checks covered the engine and selected diagnostics; the broader CI command exposed 39 errors across four older diagnostic scripts. Fixed the benchmark provider's admission callback forwarding, typed the frozen-worker spawn wrapper, made read-crash run lookups explicitly non-null and aligned the write-crash store override's keyword parameter names. The exact full CI typing command now passes locally, together with contract checks, TypeScript and seven client checks. Actual frozen cancellation regression `.acceptance/6d48e55c-21e3-4596-be88-027aa4399526/` observes/stops both application children, preserves its fixture and leaves no unresolved intent. Hosted rerun is pending; this entry does not claim hosted success.

Installer hosted feedback: run 41790142305 builds the package, then fails installed WebView readiness. Its cleanup report proves binary removal and default-data marker preservation but does not prove startup. Added bounded startup diagnostics/longer cold-start allowance, hidden-report upload, compile-cache preservation and version-derived/current-build staging with installer/installed-binary hash checks. Local rebuilt release boot succeeds in 1645 ms; hosted retry remains pending. See `docs/releases/windows-installer.md`.

Installer manifest cross-check found optional Tauri bundle patching changed desktop bytes after the standalone artifact hash. Disabled that optional patching for the NSIS-only build; actual extracted installer desktop/engine now both match the manifest and boot correctly under restricted PATH. Source evidence is .acceptance/1117f3e5-15d6-49d2-80a6-feabeb518979/. Hosted installed startup remains unresolved; the next CI run retains all readiness/authentication/shutdown checks and startup diagnostics.

Pinned skill-source follow-up: static extraction at the unchanged Hermes pin identifies skills_list, skill_view and skill_manage, with five manager verbs/six operation shapes. Canonical source hashes and a requirement/phase map now distinguish verified local text-procedure lifecycle from unimplemented model discovery, package assets, namespace resolution, preprocessing and background mutation policy. The main matrix skill row is reconciled to implemented/partial rather than planned or full parity. See docs/parity/skill-requirements.md and skill-source-inventory.json.

Hosted run 41792144056 now confirms installed binary hashes, uninstall exit 0, binary removal and data-marker preservation. Overall acceptance still failed: engine startup and frontend /models requests were logged, but the test could not attach over CDP. The test now reads /json/version, connects to its validated loopback WebSocket URL with explicit IPv4, and retains the exact final attachment error within the same 60-second bound. DOM, authenticated API and graceful-shutdown requirements remain enforced. This revised harness passes locally against the extracted installer desktop in .acceptance/df890530-ec9f-4d6c-8ad9-b604780fb217/ (1363 ms); hosted verification is pending. An IPv6/address-resolution cause remains a hypothesis.

Selected skill inventory reproduction now lives in scripts/audit_skill_inventory.py: canonical Git blobs, static AST extraction, five source fingerprints and --check comparison. It reproduces the recorded three tools/five verbs/six operation shapes without importing upstream modules. Full Ruff/format and strict engine/scripts Pyright pass. Matrix preference-memory and conversation-recall rows now reflect existing source/browser/real-model and native FTS evidence while preserving broader identity/knowledge/native-memory gaps.

Hosted run 41793568220: all 294 engine tests and source checks passed; installer smoke still failed fetching the CDP metadata endpoint (TypeError: fetch failed), before WebSocket attachment. Hashes/uninstall/data preservation passed again. The harness now retains the underlying network cause code and gives each process a unique WEBVIEW2_USER_DATA_FOLDER per https://playwright.dev/docs/webview2. This avoids shared-profile interference; it is not yet a proven hosted fix. Local isolated-profile release boot passed in .acceptance/a1de7d97-5ea2-429b-a043-78d418e45043/ (1402 ms), including authentication and graceful shutdown.

Hosted run 41794323298: source checks passed; profile isolation did not resolve boot-test attachment. Final network cause is ECONNREFUSED at 127.0.0.1:19222, with the desktop still running. Installed hashes, uninstall and marker preservation pass; overall smoke remains failed. Added bounded failure-only diagnostics for owned descendant process IDs/names, WebView runtime version and debugging-port flag, listener state and RemoteDebuggingAllowed policy values. Raw process command lines and secret values are not emitted. The diagnostic script was exercised locally, including absent policies/listeners; hosted runtime diagnosis remains pending.

Hosted process diagnostic run 41795070401 confirms WebView2 156.0.4314.50 runs without any remote-debugging-port argument or listener; no RemoteDebuggingAllowed policy was found. Local process comparison .acceptance/cf806663-ad08-4893-817e-6c5b53f4bbe5/ identifies the expected argument/listener on runtime 156.0.4314.58 (process inspection only, not new full acceptance). Microsoft explains that runtime 150+ intentionally ignores WEBVIEW2_* overrides from elevated hosts: https://github.com/MicrosoftEdge/WebView2Feedback/issues/5645. The CI-only install smoke now detects elevation, refuses an existing app-specific HKLM argument value, temporarily sets only xaa-desktop.exe remote-debugging-port=19222, and removes/verifies removal in finally. It does not change RemoteDebuggingAllowed, wildcard app policy or production application code. PowerShell syntax validation passes; hosted verification of this documented configuration path remains pending.

First passing hosted installer acceptance: [run 41795825037](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/41795825037) at 13075bff3aec49d3e6851ef9175d95a5a05e1cd6 reports both source and installer jobs completed/success. Downloaded artifact 13304733886 reports installed hashes, GUI boot/authentication, restricted runtime PATH, zero runs/inference, graceful desktop/engine shutdown, uninstall exit 0, binary removal, preserved data marker and temporary app-debugging configuration removal. Startup: 7632 ms. Installer payload SHA-256 was independently checked against the downloaded manifest. Durable report: docs/releases/evidence/windows-install-13075bf.json. Installer remains unsigned; clean normal-user setup, upgrades, optional full user-data removal, signing and notices remain open.

Restricted execution environment follow-up: existing Ubuntu-24.04 WSL provides Bubblewrap/systemd/cgroup v2. Actual isolated probes pass read/write boundaries, host sentinel/network/credential denial, UID/capability controls, 128 MiB memory OOM enforcement, 32-task process denial, 32 MiB scratch ENOSPC, measured half-CPU quota and parent/descendant runtime cleanup. All owned probe units become inactive; host fixtures are unchanged and probe staging is removed. Evidence: .acceptance/0a0d097a-2a8a-4dc8-8c98-9fc86caf4fa2/ and docs/releases/evidence/wsl-bubblewrap-probe.json. This removes an environment-availability uncertainty, not the product Phase 3 gate: generated commands remain disabled pending complete snapshot/journal/cancellation/security/tool integration. Strict engine/scripts Pyright and probe lint/format pass.

Workspace snapshot transport implemented as execution groundwork: immutable bounded regular-file manifests, verified opened-handle containment, stable per-file reads, deterministic content/manifest digests, strict cross-platform codec and exclusive private staging. Fifteen unit cases pass, including a real Windows junction and redirected opened-file handle. Actual Windows-to-WSL transfer .acceptance/245ae1df-bee4-4d80-8d3c-3c18d76acc7b/ rejects malformed traversal before staging, reads Unicode/binary bytes inside the sandbox, preserves original Windows bytes and passes the isolation/resource/cleanup probes. Durable evidence: docs/releases/evidence/wsl-snapshot-transfer.json; design/limits: docs/architecture/workspace-snapshots.md. Product commands remain disabled until durable executor, cancellation, syscall policy and native/model validation are integrated.

Snapshot integration verification: complete engine suite 309 passed (one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning); full Ruff lint/format and strict engine/scripts Pyright pass. No API/UI contract changed in this slice.

Restricted command executor source slice: standard-library WSL manager plus fixed Bubblewrap/libseccomp profile now runs structured commands in private writable snapshot copies. Actual diagnostic .acceptance/564029d7-dd0b-4d0e-a37a-740406934669/ verifies defect-test failure then diagnostic-patched success, literal arguments, syscall/socket denial, private writes, output/runtime/memory/process/storage limits, exact exit 7 and cancellation after observing both workload processes. Outcome semantics distinguish supervisor success from cancelled/truncated execution. All 323 engine tests and static checks pass. Design/evidence: docs/architecture/wsl-executor.md and docs/releases/evidence/wsl-executor.json. Product tools remain disabled pending durable identity/lease/controller/cancellation-startup recovery, explicit per-run grants, packaging and real model/native project acceptance. No Phase 3 completion or autonomous repair claim.

Execution replay/recovery follow-up: private versioned Linux receipts now publish an exclusive claim before launch, retain closure fences after cleanup and gate systemd starts on persistent closed markers. Real diagnostic .acceptance/29dd457a-3cad-4376-9755-67a81db7836b/ verifies stop-before-launch, a simulated delayed systemd job, completed replay refusal, concurrent same-ID single-winner ownership and recovery after SIGKILL of a PID/start-time/boot-ID-verified manager with two observed workload processes. A fresh process seals/stops/cleans the operation without fabricating its missing outcome; replay stays refused. Private Linux negative fixtures verify admission exhaustion, symlink/root permissions and future-schema rejection. Full suite remains 323 passing, full Ruff/format and strict Windows plus selected Linux typing pass. Evidence: docs/releases/evidence/wsl-execution-receipts.json. Windows run/lease/grant integration, global command admission, protected state paths, packaging and model/native Phase 3 acceptance remain open; no user-facing command tool is enabled.


## Windows command journal milestone (2026-11-04)

Internal explicit command grants, atomic intent/identity reservation, original-lease snapshot/launch binding, registered artifact validation, two-command per-database admission, fresh-lease result reconciliation and stopped-revision cleanup are implemented without exposing a command tool to HTTP/UI/model callers. Actual SQLite-to-WSL acceptance passed in .acceptance/3873133b-83bd-4547-aea1-15279b27f717/; sanitized evidence is docs/releases/evidence/windows-command-journal.json. Known results recover without relaunch; missing results remain not_verified; killed-manager and cancellation cleanup preserve the original project and leave no unresolved commands. This is development evidence, not autonomous model repair or native product acceptance.

Actual executor resource/replay/crash acceptance also passes with per-unit cgroup checks and bootstrap identity/capability/seccomp/tmpfs checks in .acceptance/7dd79865-b68f-4a2f-bba1-cbbd59ab1b76/. Lease stop/resume now revokes old worker tokens. File edit handles reject hard-link aliases; Git discovery rejects project/relative PATH candidates and Windows batch wrappers; source filesystem workers ignore project imports and PYTHONPATH using isolated Python mode. Regression coverage includes a real isolated worker launched from a shadow-package directory and hard-link preservation.

Verification: 338 engine tests pass with the existing Starlette/httpx warning; Ruff checks/format and Windows typing for engine/scripts pass, as does targeted Linux typing for the standalone manager resources. Remaining product work includes owned bounded command transport/snapshots, parent remaining-time enforcement, protected runtime/state mutation policy, explicit UI grants, cross-engine admission, packaged resources and native/model recovery acceptance. Overall completion remains approximately 10%; the goal and Phase 3 gate remain active/incomplete.


## Live application mutation boundary (2026-11-04)

WorkspaceExecutor now guards actual opened edit handles and verified pinned creation parents against rewriting the live engine, desktop source/entry point, Python runtime, frozen app/extraction paths, SQLite journals, artifacts/backups or private WSL execution state. Creation checks run before any new file is created. Twelve focused policy cases pass, including real executor denial without file changes, a Windows artifact-directory junction and both WSL UNC names. Frozen paths are unit simulations, not new installed-desktop acceptance. See docs/architecture/workspace-mutation-policy.md. Ordinary project edits and existing containment/hard-link rules remain in force. Product command controller, permissions UI, packaged resources, remaining-time enforcement and model/native acceptance remain unfinished; overall scope remains approximately 10%.

Final verification for the mutation-boundary checkpoint: 350 tests pass (one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning), Ruff formatting/lint and strict Windows typing across engine/scripts pass.


## Owned WSL transport (2026-11-04)

Application transport now discovers the fixed WSL resource profile, checks granted distro/profile/resource hashes, uses structured bounded binary IPC and rejects command durations beyond the remaining monotonic parent allowance. It terminates and awaits its exact launcher on cancellation/failure and does not replay an interrupted request. Linux cleanup and journal reconciliation remain separate required controller steps; launcher exit does not prove unit cleanup or command success.

Actual acceptance .acceptance/659260e3-b75d-49ee-9fa3-64898c2305fd/ proves normal private execution, replay refusal, remaining-time rejection before a Linux claim, and cancelled-launcher plus separate sealed/dead-manager cleanup with a missing result left missing. Evidence: docs/releases/evidence/wsl-owned-transport.json. Thirteen targeted tests include actual child IPC, credential removal, stdout/stderr floods, timeout and cancellation. All 363 engine tests pass with the existing Starlette/httpx warning; Ruff and strict engine/scripts typing pass. The journal checkpoint hosted run 41812880770 also passed; subsequent hosted runs were still active when inspected, so this is not a completed new installer acceptance claim.

Owned snapshot capture, durable controller/supervisor integration, startup/reboot recovery, cross-engine admission, resource packaging, UI grants and real model/native command validation remain unfinished. No model or UI command tool is enabled. Overall completion remains approximately 10%.


## Owned capture and command-controller workflow (2026-11-04)

The internal command controller now joins explicit saved grants, lease-bound identity reservation, isolated owned snapshot capture, registered manifests, original-lease launch authorization, WSL execution, output hash/size checks, registered output artifacts and journal completion. Capture cannot accept a caller-selected path; it derives the project from the saved intent and checks original ownership before and after capture. The bounded binary worker handles up to 8 MiB of file contents and can be stopped independently of the engine event loop. Frozen entry dispatch is implemented but newly packaged/native snapshot-worker acceptance remains pending.

Actual .acceptance/cd208958-8b70-44ab-8065-04cec00fd15a/ verifies a complete successful workflow with three registered artifacts and private writes leaving the original project unchanged. It also observes a live manager, pauses the run (revoking its lease), cancels the controller, confirms remote cleanup and preserves the stopped revision. The actual saved cancelled result is failed/not_verified with no exit code; no command remains unresolved. Evidence: docs/releases/evidence/wsl-command-controller.json. The actor is an automated diagnostic, not a model or UI user.

All 373 engine tests pass with the existing Starlette/httpx warning. Ruff formatting/lint and strict engine/scripts typing pass. New tests cover full-limit binary/Unicode snapshot IPC, project import shadowing denial, owner replacement/revocation, controller artifact commitment, corrupt-output refusal, snapshot-error cleanup, expired-budget no-reservation and retained admission when cleanup cannot be proved.

Remaining: coordinator/supervisor ownership, lease renewal and durable deadline propagation; pending-result/startup/reboot recovery; repeated-cancellation hardening; cross-engine admission; packaged manager resources/frozen worker acceptance; explicit permission UI and real model/native defect validation. HTTP/model command tools remain disabled. Full release and parity gates remain incomplete; overall completion is approximately 10%.


## Cancellation retains cleanup ownership (2026-11-04)

Repeated caller cancellation is now deferred until owned cleanup finishes, then returned to the caller. Cleanup errors remain observable. This applies to command reconciliation, binary IPC termination, filesystem/tokenizer worker termination and supervisor worker waits. Supervisor close cancels all recovery/run owners before awaiting their combined cleanup.

Actual WSL acceptance .acceptance/710f9a80-908e-4f2c-a264-830131bc08fd/ deliberately holds remote reconciliation after pause/lease revocation, sends three additional cancellations, verifies the controller remains waiting, then confirms actual Linux cleanup and failed/not_verified journal resolution without changing the paused revision. Evidence: docs/releases/evidence/wsl-repeated-cancellation.json. Tests also exercise a real sleeping child and repeated cancellation during its awaited shutdown, observed cleanup failure and concurrent supervisor owner cancellation.

The full suite passes 377 tests with the existing Starlette/httpx warning. The final filesystem/tokenizer adoption additionally passes 21 targeted tests. Ruff lint/format and strict engine/scripts typing pass. This closes the covered repeated-cancellation ownership gap; abrupt engine death/startup/reboot recovery, coordinator integration, global admission, resource packaging and model/native permission workflows remain open. No UI/model command tool is enabled; overall completion remains approximately 10%.


## Fresh-controller pending-result recovery (2026-11-04)

An active replacement lease can now recover a saved command identity through the controller without capturing input or launching execution again. Recovery seals/cleans the identity, inspects the durable manager receipt and commits only matching registered local output whose actual bytes pass journal verification.
