Completed a source-level audit of the pinned Hermes memory manager, provider contract, dynamic tool registration, built-in store and context sanitizer. Findings are bound to the exact Hermes commit and SHA-256 hashes in [`parity/memory-source-audit.md`](parity/memory-source-audit.md), and the memory row in the Hermes matrix now distinguishes X.A.A.'s verified explicit-memory behavior from upstream query-prefetch, turn-sync and pluggable-provider gaps. This did not run external providers or claim parity. No runtime code changed; the next dependency-ordered Phase 0 slice is the adjacent pinned skill lifecycle source audit before expanding package behavior.

## Audit pinned Hermes skill lifecycle source (2026-11-11)

Reviewed the pinned core skill lookup and management paths and recorded SHA-256-bound findings in [`parity/skill-source-audit.md`](parity/skill-source-audit.md). The Hermes matrix now links the source audit while retaining the limits: bundled/optional skills, the catalog, representative package runtime behavior and broad platform readiness remain unaudited. This was source review only; no external package was installed or run. No runtime code changed.

## Rank explicit memory recall against each run objective (2026-11-11)

Parent and specialist run setup now queries scoped FTS5 indexes using up to 32 Unicode word terms from the current objective, ranks matching explicit preferences and user-sourced knowledge by FTS relevance, and keeps the existing byte caps and separate trust labels. Matching records rank first; other records in the same scope and record type remain eligible in recency order within the byte cap, preserving broad role preferences. The search remains local and does not create or promote memory. Added deterministic relevance, type separation, provenance and objective-context regressions. Focused Python tests pass (7); Ruff passes and Pyright passes for the three changed engine modules. This improves task-relevant recall but does not implement Hermes-style pluggable provider hooks or prove broad retrieval quality. Live inference remains deferred. Updated the memory matrix and source audit accordingly.

## Preserve XLSX formulas in bounded document extraction (2026-11-11)

XLSX ingestion now includes formula expressions beside any cached cell result, includes formula-only cells when no cached result exists, and records built-in/custom number-format codes for styled cells. Formula text is truncated at 2,048 characters and format codes at 128 characters per cell; all remain untrusted document content, and X.A.A. does not evaluate formulas or calculate formatted display values. Added unit and run-API regressions. Focused verification passes (14 tests); Ruff, formatting, Pyright on the changed engine module and `git diff --check` pass. No live model inference was used. The matrix now records formula preservation while leaving workbook formatting extraction, broad workbook compatibility and media accuracy open.

## Improve saved-run readability in the Windows app (2026-11-11)

Saved-run cards now show a two-line objective preview, status and timestamp; opening a run still preserves its full objective in the run view. The active run now shows a three-line objective preview with the complete text under an accessible disclosure. `npm run build:desktop` (including TypeScript) and an isolated native Tauri build pass. Opened the staged Windows executable and verified the bundled engine reached `Engine ready`; visually confirmed the compact run list and collapsed full-objective disclosure. LM Studio was unavailable, so no model inference or run control was attempted. The existing history browser acceptance initially timed out waiting for `Load older runs`: after the navigation redesign, Saved runs is hidden until its navigation link is selected. Updated the acceptance to follow the current navigation; `npm run test:browser -- tests/browser/history.spec.ts` now passes (1 test, including pagination, selected-run replay, objective search and conversation search).

## Render bounded XLSX clock and elapsed time formats (2026-11-11)

Spreadsheet text extraction now adds deterministic display text for common clock formats (`h:mm`, `h:mm:ss`, `h:mm AM/PM`, `mm:ss`) and bounded elapsed formats (`[h]:mm:ss`, `[m]:ss`, `[s]`) while preserving raw serial values and number-format codes. Minute/second rounding, 12-hour clock output, elapsed values beyond 24 hours, negative/non-finite values and unsupported/combined formats have focused regression coverage. No formula is evaluated. The full document extraction suite passes (72 tests), the XLSX run API test passes, and Ruff, formatting, Pyright and `git diff --check` pass locally. Microsoft documents the minute-token context, AM/PM behavior and bracketed elapsed formats in its [number-format guidance](https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/excel/review-guidelines-for-customizing-a-number-format). This does not implement Excel's locale or full cell-layout rendering.

## Render bounded XLSX combined date and time values (2026-11-11)

Spreadsheet extraction now adds display text for common combined date/time formats (`m/d/yy h:mm AM/PM` and `yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm:ss`) while retaining the original serial and format code. When the rounded time crosses midnight, the displayed date advances with it. Other locale-specific/custom sections and fractional seconds remain unsupported, and formulas are not calculated. The 73-test document suite, XLSX run API test, Ruff, formatting, Pyright, and `git diff --check` pass locally. Format behavior follows Microsoft's [number-format guidance](https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/excel/review-guidelines-for-customizing-a-number-format); this does not reproduce full Excel workbook layout.

## Render optional XLSX decimal places (2026-11-12)

Bounded XLSX numeric display now handles optional fractional `#` placeholders alongside required zeroes, including fixed-point, grouped, and percentage patterns such as `0.##`, `0.0#`, `#,##0.##`, and `0.0#%`. Trailing optional zeroes are omitted while required zero places remain; interleaved placeholder patterns fail closed. Formulas are still not evaluated, and colors, conditional sections, locale-specific currency, scientific notation, and full Excel layout remain unsupported. The focused document suite passes (106 tests), including a package-level ZIP/XML extraction regression; Ruff, formatting, Pyright and `git diff --check` pass. Hosted Windows source run [43129042751](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43129042751) passed all source, desktop, browser and recovery checks, including 1,141 Python tests. No model inference was used.

## Continue saved chat in the native desktop (2026-11-11)

The desktop chat acceptance now opens a saved conversation in the Windows Tauri WebView, selects its bound local fixture model and workspace, continues the saved context through a durable coordinator run, and verifies the new user/assistant turn is stored while the run reaches `verifying`, with no tool effects. It uses a deterministic loopback fixture, not LM Studio or an external model provider. This exposed a UI bug: the answer was appended only after the event stream closed, although `verifying` deliberately keeps the run open for review. The UI now saves the turn when the coordinator reaches `verifying` or `completed`. The rebuilt Windows Tauri executable passes `scripts/native_restart_smoke.mjs`; report: [`.acceptance/native-restart-21efb0b6-0e99-438e-b2c2-073ebe2aba81/report.json`](../.acceptance/native-restart-21efb0b6-0e99-438e-b2c2-073ebe2aba81/report.json). TypeScript, desktop build, engine bundle, native executable build, and `git diff --check` pass locally. This is a deterministic local acceptance and one Windows development executable, not a newly signed or published release.

## Launch a current isolated Windows desktop preview (2026-11-11)

Rebuilt the production desktop frontend, frozen Windows engine, and Tauri executable from the current source into `build/native-preview/`, separate from the existing development instances. The previous development engine log showed a startup failure because its database schema was newer than that engine. ## Add bounded local trajectory compression (2026-11-11)

Added `xaa compress-trajectories` for Hermes-style JSONL conversation entries. It counts with the exact already-loaded LM Studio model, summarizes only through the validated loopback provider, preserves first system/human/assistant/tool context and a configurable recent tail, keeps assistant/tool-result groups together, emits compression metrics, and atomically publishes a new output without overwriting. Limits include 10,000 entries, 10 MiB per input line, 256 turns, 256 KiB per summary region and 100 MiB total output. Malformed rows, incomplete summaries, unfit results and size violations fail closed without publishing partial output. The six deterministic fixtures pass; Ruff, strict Pyright, CLI help and `git diff --check` pass. No live summarizer inference was run. This accepts conversational JSONL, not X.A.A.'s native `events` export; conversion, broader benchmark/replay coverage, provider-specific training integration and a live-model acceptance remain open. The local `build/native-preview/` window displays the workspace with its engine ready; LM Studio has no available model, so setup/inference remains pending. An older development log contains a schema-newer startup error; the current isolated preview did not reproduce it, and the exact earlier process remains unidentified. Existing user data was left intact.

## Support MCP sampling on modern protocol (2026-11-12)

The MCP manager called the low-level session tool method directly, which bypassed the SDK 2.3.0 client's `InputRequiredResult` driver. Changed it to the high-level `Client.call_tool`; modern servers can now return an MCP `Sample` resolver request, and the SDK routes it through X.A.A.'s existing run-bound sampling callback then retries with the validated response. Added a real subprocess fixture and expanded the deterministic integration case to cover both automatic modern protocol negotiation and the explicit legacy callback. The modern callback remains text-only, tool-free, bound to the active run, and measured against its shared inference budget. Both modern and legacy focused sampling tests pass (2); the broader MCP stdio/HTTP and prompt-preview suites pass (26). The full local Python suite passes (1,149 passed, 1 skipped in 420.34s); repository-wide Ruff/format and strict Pyright pass. The auto-negotiated test asserts protocol `2026-07-28`, so it specifically covers the modern request path. This uses the SDK's official [multi-round-trip request design](https://github.com/modelcontextprotocol/python-sdk/blob/main/docs/handlers/multi-round-trip.md); the legacy standalone sampling RPC remains supported for old servers but is deprecated in modern MCP. No real provider/model inference was used. Hosted source run [43162201012](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43162201012) passed in 8m24s, including 1,150 Python tests, browser security checks, pinned Hermes inventories, Ruff/format, strict Pyright, generated API contracts, desktop browser acceptances, and specialist/scheduler recovery diagnostics. The full hosted installer acceptance passed in run [43162210451](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43162210451) in 7m59s. It passed notice coverage, native and frozen recovery, workspace write/edit/delete review, frozen execution scope, 0.1.0 upgrade, installed boot/uninstall, standard-user install and explicit data removal. The unsigned 0.1.1 installer and reports are preserved under [`.acceptance/hosted-installer-43162210451/`](../.acceptance/hosted-installer-43162210451/); its 77,697,125-byte executable matches manifest SHA-256 `8e86dc8729c6437c222e67af96882dd7c07f2f2104488b27b82ec2c74f4c22d4`; desktop SHA-256 is `adf713d4a62a750eef0ce211049f6e2169439f01c58acb7e773393126265f316` and engine SHA-256 is `9fc94e7eed3e36997368098d1ec3eed05fc986c5c551496675e6f685f61e5d14`. No real provider/model inference was used. The installer is an unsigned development package, not a signed or general release.


## Bound MCP stdio frames before parsing (2026-11-12)

The MCP SDK's stdio reader buffered a complete newline-delimited message without a size limit, allowing a malformed local server to grow memory use before X.A.A.'s result limits ran. Added a managed adapter that caps each inbound frame at 1 MiB before JSON parsing and reuses the pinned SDK 2.3.0 process creation and shutdown helpers so Windows Job Object and POSIX process-group cleanup remain in force. A deterministic subprocess emits an oversized frame without a newline; the focused transport test confirms a specific 1 MiB limit error and orderly process cleanup. All 21 MCP stdio integration tests pass via `uv run pytest tests/integration/test_mcp_stdio.py -q`; Ruff, formatting and strict Pyright pass for the engine changes. A direct `python.exe` invocation bypasses the project's PATH setup, so acceptance uses `uv run`. No live model inference was used. Hosted Windows run [43168373537](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43168373537) passed on `0900acd`, including 1,151 Python tests, Ruff, strict Pyright, desktop checks and browser security acceptances; the installer job was skipped because no packaging inputs changed. Network egress isolation remains open; a later progress entry records the Windows process resource-cap slice.

## Bound new MCP stdio process resources on Windows (2026-11-12)

New desktop-configured stdio MCP servers default to a 2,048 MiB job-wide committed-memory ceiling and 80% CPU hard cap; owners can set memory to 128–65,536 MiB and CPU to 1–100%, or leave either unlimited. Windows enforces the committed-memory limit across the job, and its CPU rate uses the documented percentage-times-100 scale with `JobObjectCpuRateControlInformation` class 15 ([memory limit structure](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winnt/ns-winnt-jobobject_extended_limit_information), [CPU rate structure](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/desktop/api/winnt/ns-winnt-jobobject_cpu_rate_control_information), [SetInformationJobObject](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/jobapi2/nf-jobapi2-setinformationjobobject)). Both limits are persisted and applied to the SDK-owned Windows Job Object, preserving kill-on-close process-tree cleanup; MCP package imports use the same defaults. Schema 31 adds memory and schema 32 adds CPU while existing saved servers remain uncapped for compatibility. Owners can change limits on existing stdio entries while disconnected; connection startup and limit edits serialize under the manager lock. A real Windows subprocess acceptance queries the Job Object and verifies both ceilings. The focused MCP stdio/HTTP and migration suites pass (52 tests), the desktop MCP browser acceptance passes (1), and the production desktop build, generated API contract, Ruff, format, Pyright, and TypeScript checks pass. Hosted Windows source run [43176300454](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43176300454) passed the full suite (**1,155 Python tests**) and source/browser/recovery gates. Hosted installer run [43176310435](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43176310435) passed package creation, native and frozen recovery, upgrade from 0.1.0, install/boot/uninstall, standard-user install, and explicit data removal. Its unsigned 0.1.1 installer is preserved at [`.acceptance/hosted-installer-43176310435/build/installer/Xyst AI Agent_0.1.1_x64-setup.exe`](../.acceptance/hosted-installer-43176310435/build/installer/Xyst%20AI%20Agent_0.1.1_x64-setup.exe), 77,709,485 bytes, SHA-256 `ebbaacfde420784ff2350f2c58a1c0873be174de7d5c267108b7fc1b17a2d4f7`, matching its manifest. Installer acceptance covers packaging and lifecycle; the process-limit behavior itself is verified by the Windows subprocess/source tests, not a dedicated installed-app cap test. Caps are currently Windows-only and applied after SDK process creation, so startup allocations precede enforcement. Process network egress isolation remains open. No model inference was used.

## Refresh connected MCP tool catalogs from modern server events (2026-11-12)

Connected servers advertising modern `tools.listChanged` now have a live MCP listen stream. On a change event, X.A.A. refreshes the pinned-SDK tool listing, persists the new catalog, applies the existing default-deny behavior to new or schema-changed tools, and queues an untrusted server-update notice. The desktop MCP panel surfaces that notice and refreshes its visible server/tool list when the owner checks for updates. The official TypeScript SDK 2.4.0 fixture exercises late tool registration and change notifications over stdio and Streamable HTTP; the two interoperability cases pass and the complete MCP HTTP/stdio integration files pass (35 tests). A fresh native Windows bundle also compiled and staged at `build/native-mcp-tools-refresh-20261112/`; its engine CLI help smoke passes. ## Refresh MCP resource and prompt catalogs after list-change events (2026-11-12)

The modern MCP listen stream now includes advertised `resources.listChanged` and `prompts.listChanged` capabilities alongside tool changes. Resource events invalidate cached resource, template and expanded-URI catalogs; prompt events invalidate cached prompt definitions, preventing use of stale entries. The owner-facing “Check for server updates” flow re-lists the affected resources/templates or prompts and clears stale displayed reads/previews. The official TypeScript SDK fixture emits late resources and prompts over stdio and Streamable HTTP; both transport cases and the complete MCP integration files pass (35 tests). ## Add owner-controlled MCP argument completions (2026-11-12)

Added authenticated MCP completion requests for prompt arguments and resource-template variables. The manager accepts only a reference and arguments from the current connection catalog, serializes requests with other operations on that server, and applies the protocol's 100-value maximum along with prefix, context and total-response bounds. The desktop labels returned suggestions as untrusted and copies one into editable arguments only after the owner selects it. Deterministic stdio and Streamable HTTP fixtures pass for both reference types; the stdio/API checks also cover catalog/argument rejection, authentication and request/output bounds. The focused browser acceptance passes for prompt and template suggestions and subsequent preview/read using the selected values. `npm run build:desktop`, generated API contract check, Ruff, strict engine/scripts Pyright and diff checks pass. The latest hosted source run [43265555299](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43265555299) passed before this change; hosted verification of completions is pending. This is Phase 7 progress, not full MCP parity. No live LM Studio or cloud model test was run, consistent with deferring model acceptance until the end. Overall project estimate remains roughly 56%, the latest recorded program-level estimate; this feature does not close a full phase gate. Remaining work includes real local-model final acceptance, broader MCP compatibility and channel coverage, Phase 9 parity gaps, and signed/release-grade packaging.

The additional listener-bind failure regression passes in the 14-test execution-readiness suite. The Hermes matrix records this as partial X.A.A.-owned backend coverage, not Hermes local-execution parity. No live model inference was run.

## Verify Windows AppContainer in the frozen engine (2026-11-13)

The packaged readiness probe initially failed because Windows redirects an AppContainer's temp directory to its profile-local `Packages\\<profile>\\AC\\Temp`, while the transport had only prepared its command staging directories. The host now obtains the profile-local directory with `GetAppContainerFolderPath`, creates and grants its private temp folder, and includes it in the command storage bound. The restricted child environment uses the base local-data directory corresponding to that profile so Windows resolves the same temp path, including when the parent engine runs with an isolated `LOCALAPPDATA`. The frozen engine shim's one-file extraction now uses its own host-only temp directory with a separate 192 MiB cap; the AppContainer cannot write there. The command storage monitor counts the private AppContainer temp and workspace against their 256 MiB and 32 MiB limits. It tolerates entries disappearing during frozen PyInstaller extraction/cleanup while continuing to reject reparse points and enforce byte and entry limits.

The focused Windows integration and unit/readiness checks pass (20 tests); the adjacent MCP stdio, Windows command, unit and readiness regression suites pass together (54 tests); Ruff and strict Pyright pass. The full unsigned 0.1.1 current-user installer build completed (74.02 MiB). Its freshly packaged engine passed `scripts/frozen_windows_appcontainer_readiness.py`, verifying AppContainer identity, no network capability, private snapshot and workspace isolation, private runtime temp access, credential removal, bounded process-tree cleanup, and a clean engine shutdown. Evidence: [`.acceptance/frozen-windows-appcontainer-c94f4ceb40d748599e304ed459bade9a/report.json`](../.acceptance/frozen-windows-appcontainer-c94f4ceb40d748599e304ed459bade9a/report.json), engine SHA-256 `8917ca59923100b5b292c9fcd38dacce4c1173aaec7a14bf6022f5f6736badbe`; the installer manifest records the same engine hash. Added the frozen readiness diagnostic to the Windows installer workflow, where its report will be retained with acceptance artifacts. Hosted verification of this workflow change, installed AppContainer command execution, clean-machine behavior, and signed release packaging remain open. This verifies one X.A.A.-owned backend path and does not establish Hermes backend parity. No live model inference was used.

## Package X.A.A. as a native Windows desktop program (2026-11-13)

The unsigned 0.1.1 current-user installer builds and opens as a Tauri desktop window with its bundled engine. The AppContainer trace identified `cryptography`'s `_rust` DLL under an Administrators-owned Python virtual environment; a standard user cannot add the required file ACLs there. X.A.A. now prepares a managed, code-only copy of the selected Python environment under its per-user data directory, grants read/execute access to the Windows AppContainer package group before copying its files, and launches the cached interpreter with its private profile temp path. The cache retains the selected `pyvenv.cfg` base runtime and hashes the source file inventory for reuse. The recovery journal now accepts the existing 50,000-object ACL bound, and cleanup skips entries that never received an ACL change. The stale journal from the failed ACL experiment was recovered. A later native acceptance exposed that the isolated stdio path's 15-second connection deadline was too short for cold startup: the operation timed out, while the direct shim test passed. Isolated stdio startup and initialization now have a 60-second bound, regular MCP connections retain their existing 15-second bound, and the native acceptance has a small dedicated working directory and matching deadline. Fresh packaged native acceptance now passes the AppContainer MCP startup, loopback denial, stdio/HTTP SDK interoperability, OAuth, package lifecycle, shared conversations and graceful shutdown: [`.acceptance/94c83872-1243-4c4c-95ed-aad43b7c72e9/report.json`](../.acceptance/94c83872-1243-4c4c-95ed-aad43b7c72e9/report.json). Ruff, strict Pyright, Node syntax and diff checks pass; the focused Windows AppContainer integration passes. Hosted source run [43444822859](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43444822859) passes. On hosted installer run [43445508366](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43445508366), attempt 1's frozen command assertion failed once but passed on retry; attempt 2 then passed package build, AppContainer readiness, coordinator command, installed AppContainer execution, and native MCP/OAuth. The separate native restart smoke timed out because its script did not expand the collapsed saved-conversations panel. The script now opens that panel before loading conversations; the full local native restart acceptance passes, including clean credential removal ([`.acceptance/native-restart-e1e87fc7-ddb9-4d1c-8c2e-ccfe6ec19e34/report.json`](../.acceptance/native-restart-e1e87fc7-ddb9-4d1c-8c2e-ccfe6ec19e34/report.json)). This verifies local native packaging, not clean-machine installation, signed publishing, or full Hermes MCP parity. No model inference was used.

## Resolve imported Python requirements into a candidate lock (2026-11-13)

Added `PyPIWheelResolver` and an authenticated exact-procedure endpoint plus desktop action. A fresh server-side WSL readiness probe supplies interpreter marker data and ordered wheel tags; the resolver fetches bounded PEP 691 project records and PEP 658 metadata, follows transitive `Requires-Dist`, evaluates target markers and `Requires-Python`, filters yanked or incompatible wheels, intersects multiple direct constraints, prefers the target's ordered tags, and backtracks across at most 16 candidate versions per package. A pending lock is saved against the immutable imported source digest. It does not bypass the exact-wheel acquisition gate: actual wheel bytes still have to match the index hashes, lock metadata, and ABI tags before approval. Resolution and exact-wheel acquisition still run in the API engine process without a separate OS process/resource boundary; no installation or procedure execution consumes the lock, so imported dependency execution and Hermes skill parity remain partial.

The focused resolver/acquisition/API group passes 22 tests, including transitive selection, backtracking, marker handling, tag preference, an exact pin older than the candidate bound, auth checks and source-bound persistence. Both Skills browser checks pass. Desktop typecheck, OpenAPI/client contract check, Ruff, strict Pyright and `git diff --check` pass. A real Microsoft Edge run passes deterministic page and nested-frame checks that block third-party image, script and frame resources; local report: [`.acceptance/browser-subresource-edge-b330a43890784910911bc508bbc48831/report.json`](../.acceptance/browser-subresource-edge-b330a43890784910911bc508bbc48831/report.json). Hosted source run [43524032973](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43524032973) failed because its live Wikipedia navigation timed out before assertions. The hosted browser diagnostic now defaults to deterministic Edge page fixtures; setting `XAA_BROWSER_LIVE_NETWORK=1` retains the optional real-site check. Hosted verification of this change is pending. No public PyPI request, package installation, or model inference was used.

## Isolate imported Python dependency resolution (2026-11-13)

The default resolver now runs PEP 691/658 parsing and dependency backtracking in X.A.A.'s network-denied Windows AppContainer command manager. The engine retains a stdio HTTPS broker restricted to `pypi.org` project pages and `files.pythonhosted.org` wheel metadata: TLS verification, no ambient proxy or redirects, at most 192 requests, 2 MiB per simple page, 1 MiB per metadata response, 96 MiB aggregate response bytes, and 4 MiB worker output. The AppContainer command boundary applies its existing process-tree, 128 MiB memory, 50% CPU, 32-process, bounded private-storage, and 120-second limits. Hosted verification is pending. No public PyPI request or model inference was used.

## Rebuild X.A.A. as a Windows desktop program (2026-11-13)

Rebuilt the React/Tauri desktop app and bundled Python engine from commit `b2a5c50`, staged the native executable at `build/native-dev/xaa-desktop.exe`, and launched the refreshed desktop window. `npm run build:native:dev` completed the frontend, PyInstaller engine, and Rust desktop build; initial staging was blocked by the older app holding its executable open, so that instance was closed and staging then succeeded. Built a fresh unsigned current-user installer at `build/installer/Xyst AI Agent_0.1.1_x64-setup.exe` (77,691,541 bytes; SHA-256 `a335c14eaea7e7b0d5b1f1a2b914d4d7da3fd8718e60835d66b4058ac0493d33`). Hosted Windows source run [43532611169](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43532611169) passed; its installer job was skipped. This verifies the desktop build launches on this development machine, not installation on a clean machine, signed release, or public publication. LM Studio remains separately required for model inference; no inference was run.

## Isolate exact-wheel acquisition and storage (2026-11-13)

Moved exact locked-wheel download and archive verification into a dedicated network-denied Windows AppContainer worker. The engine brokers only fixed-host HTTPS requests and transfers wheel bodies on demand in 512 KiB protocol chunks; one locked wheel is streamed at a time, with a 512-request cap, 352 MiB aggregate response cap and existing 120-second worker/process resource bounds. The worker writes only into its private snapshot. After a successful result, the engine checks the exact workspace file set and independently hashes and inspects each wheel by path, then atomically copies files into the content-addressed wheelhouse. Approval now rechecks hashes and metadata from disk without aggregating the wheelhouse into memory. The frozen engine has an explicit worker entry and PyInstaller hidden import.

Windows source and frozen-worker AppContainer acceptances pass with a 1 MiB in-memory mock wheel, including multi-frame streaming and parent-side verification; the complete Windows command transport plus resolver/acquisition integration set passes (12 tests). Focused wheel/API/storage/dependency units pass (25 tests), plus the broker wheel-file URL boundary case; Ruff and strict Pyright pass. Hosted verification is pending. No public PyPI access, package installation, or model inference occurred. Offline managed installation and procedure-scoped execution remain open.

Rebuilt the unsigned current-user Windows installer from this source: `build/installer/Xyst AI Agent_0.1.1_x64-setup.exe` (77,712,937 bytes; SHA-256 `24d6765d300783c3f45792b9f22cb7ece972e042457eec966773b7e64db50ceb`). The packaged engine SHA-256 is `4e7c939b851805230bccdb777924c8028c606348bc9209384851fa7b13a2d66e`. This is a local installer build, not clean-machine installation or public release verification.
## Install approved npm dependencies offline in WSL (2026-11-14)

Added a root-managed npm environment installer alongside the existing Python environment manager. Installation accepts only a saved owner-approved lock, exact source-bound `package.json` and `package-lock.json`, and the persistent ordered tarball set. The manager rechecks lock and asset hashes plus every tarball SHA-512, copies the inputs into private root-owned staging, and invokes only local `npm cache add` and `npm ci` with `--offline --ignore-scripts`; the unprivileged Bubblewrap sandbox has no network namespace and systemd memory/CPU/task/runtime bounds. On success it removes install cache/source staging, validates an immutable tree (including only relative symlinks that resolve inside the tree), seals it read-only, and publishes a hash manifest. Authenticated install/remove routes persist verified receipts; the desktop shows the installed tree and exact removal action. Frozen-manager resources participate in the WSL profile fingerprint.

Live WSL acceptance against a local tarball passed: exact offline lock install, module load, npm `.bin` symlink, install-script marker absent, idempotent reinstall, status integrity check, and digest-scoped removal ([report](../.acceptance/npm-node-environment-9bf90ac14dc1437ba70c32b45ea926b8/report.json)). The focused npm/API/storage tests pass (41); WSL manager, Python environment regression, transport and migration tests pass (66); the Skills browser flow passes (1). Ruff, strict Pyright, desktop TypeScript, generated OpenAPI/TypeScript contract check, and diff checks pass. A fresh unsigned current-user installer was rebuilt at `build/installer/Xyst AI Agent_0.1.1_x64-setup.exe` (77,833,785 bytes; SHA-256 `a7e311efe909df30048653d8b032e2b7788eb464d978e822756036422c49a2ab`); its bundled engine SHA-256 is `b6e6b332a4f0e25530f38606b68511cc3b90f5d86c7de930edd1092fe33c60f1`. This local package build is not installed-machine acceptance. The prior parser-isolation hosted run [43571254830](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43571254830) passed, while this installer change still needs hosted verification. The key remaining Node lifecycle step is binding an installed digest/tree into a selected procedure snapshot, mounting it read-only for the exact run, holding its lease through cleanup, and refusing removal while a run uses it. Hostile interrupted-install/recovery and broader npm semantics remain open. No model inference was used.

## Bind installed npm dependencies to Node procedure runs (2026-11-14)

Run creation now checks only approved installed npm environments for the selected imported procedure version and the command grant's exact WSL distribution. A single matching environment is selected automatically; multiple environments require the desktop's explicit digest choice. The immutable `procedure.selected` event carries the environment digest, target distribution, tree hash and Node/npm versions. Command reservation copies that identity plus the selected procedure UUID/version into the saved `CommandIntent`, so model arguments cannot choose or replace it. WSL rehashes the immutable tree under a shared use lease, mounts only that procedure's `node_modules` read-only, adds its `.bin` directory to PATH, and holds the lease through service cleanup. Environment removal now returns the explicit in-use refusal while a command holds the lease.

The live Ubuntu-24.04 acceptance passed exact offline install, repeat install, module integrity and lifecycle suppression, module import from a selected procedure script inside Bubblewrap, removal refusal during that command, post-cleanup removal, and final absence verification: [report](../.acceptance/npm-node-environment-6b7f7990aeaf4a5fabbe17763ede3bac/report.json). The focused run/API/command/WSL group passes 97 tests; both Skills browser flows pass (2). Desktop TypeScript, generated API contracts, Ruff, strict Pyright, `git diff --check`, and a fresh frozen engine build pass. A fresh unsigned current-user installer was built from this slice: build/installer/Xyst AI Agent_0.1.1_x64-setup.exe (77,838,957 bytes; SHA-256 e7472ed87fd1a7e8ec791ceb342f61d077de5be7496c5d2bfbdf70d833db935a); its bundled engine SHA-256 is e614f58abb15cce73dbb52e92ff4c0ca2b03a3293ba244fbcd12e18e3e378a06. This is a package build, not installation acceptance. Hosted CI remains pending. The model-mediated procedure trial remains deferred until the end; broader npm compatibility, interrupted-install recovery, and hostile/recovery acceptance remain open. No public npm request or model inference was used.

## Recover interrupted npm environment trees safely (2026-11-14)

Exact-digest removal now works even when an interrupted install left an invalid manifest or a corrupt tree. While holding the manager lock, removal must obtain the digest's exclusive lease, verify the exact target is a root-owned real directory, stop its exact transient installer unit, and confirm that unit is inactive before making the tree writable and deleting it. The same guarded cleanup repairs partial state before reinstall; a held execution lease still blocks deletion. Two focused manager recovery tests pass. Repeated live WSL acceptance with the stricter unit check again passed install, Node procedure import, active-use removal refusal, cleanup, and removal: [report](../.acceptance/npm-node-environment-96a173d9178d4e068953f379ad4fb47d/report.json). The full focused set passes 135 tests, and Ruff, format check, Pyright, desktop typecheck, generated contracts, and diff checks pass. GitHub run [43576335917](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43576335917) stopped at Ruff formatting; those formatting changes are corrected locally. The installer must be rebuilt and hosted verification rerun after this recovery fix. Broader package compatibility and model-mediated procedure trials remain open; no inference or public npm request was used.

## Exercise interrupted npm recovery in live WSL (2026-11-14)

The WSL acceptance seeds a partial root-owned environment tree and starts the matching transient systemd unit before recovery. It now also starts a real offline npm install, kills its exact systemd service's entire cgroup with SIGKILL, verifies the failed attempt did not publish an environment, then retries the same approved digest. The complete procedure fixture passed twice for seeded partial-tree recovery and once for process-kill/retry recovery; the process-kill evidence confirms a 4,109-file tree, successful sandbox import, active-use removal refusal, lifecycle suppression, cleanup, and exact removal: [report](../.acceptance/npm-process-kill-recovery-20261114/report.json). Earlier seeded-state reports remain available: [first](../.acceptance/npm-node-interrupted-recovery-20261114/report.json), [repeat](../.acceptance/npm-node-interrupted-recovery-20261114b/report.json). The acceptance attempts exact-digest cleanup in `finally`, including setup failures. Ruff, format, and diff checks pass for the script. Hosted run [43578398675](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43578398675) passed the Windows source workflow, including the full Python suite; installer packaging was skipped by workflow conditions. The unsigned 0.1.1 installer was rebuilt locally after the manager fix (77,839,457 bytes; SHA-256 `7b48c07de6032ef5722d029c20dea12583e3c967b5d9a8bbc9af86eb4f0ec96b`). Rollback to an older digest, system/runtime preservation, project isolation, hostile archive cases, and the deferred model-mediated run remain open.

## Verify npm project scoping and runtime preservation (2026-11-14)

The live WSL fixture now checks that its `node_modules` link resolves to the read-only mount only under the selected procedure version and that no project-root `node_modules` is created. It hashes the host Node executable, the installed npm tree and the global-module root before and after the complete install/kill/retry/procedure-run/removal flow. Ubuntu-24.04 preserved Node `v18.19.1`, npm `9.2.0`, both runtime hashes, and the absent global-module directory; the selected module imported successfully and the managed environment was removed: [runtime/isolation report](../.acceptance/npm-runtime-isolation-20261114/report.json). A second verified path installs procedure v2 to a separate digest while retaining v1, successfully reruns v1, removes v2 without affecting v1, then removes v1: [rollback report](../.acceptance/npm-version-rollback-20261114/report.json). Ruff, formatting, and strict Pyright pass locally. This covers one WSL fixture, not multiple procedures executing concurrently. Hosted Windows source run [43579781434](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43579781434) passed the full source workflow for the preceding runtime/isolation slice; hosted validation of rollback remains pending. Installer packaging was skipped by the packaging-change filter. Cross-procedure isolation, hostile archive cases, and model-mediated execution remain open.

Windows integration acceptance now builds correctly SRI-matched archives with path traversal, an escaping symlink, and duplicate members. Hosted run [43580609010](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43580609010) passed the full source workflow, including the source/frozen hostile archive cases; installer packaging was skipped by the workflow filter. Archive-expansion checks, cross-procedure concurrency, and the deferred model-mediated trial remain open.


## Verify concurrent Node procedure-version isolation (2026-11-14)

The live WSL fixture now installs distinct v1 and v2 package contents, launches both selected procedure versions concurrently, and verifies v1 returns `offline-ready` while v2 returns `new-version`. Removal attempts for both active digests are refused; after both complete, v2 removal leaves v1 installed and runnable until its own exact removal. Evidence: [concurrent rollback/runtime/isolation report](../.acceptance/npm-concurrent-procedure-isolation-20261114/report.json). Strict Pyright, Ruff, formatting, and diff checks pass locally. Hosted validation of this expanded live fixture is pending; the previous source run [43580609010](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43580609010) passed, including Windows archive-member checks. Archive-expansion acceptance and model-mediated imported-procedure execution remain open.

## Resolve duplicate npm package versions by nearest path (2026-11-14)

Extended the offline Ubuntu-24.04 WSL dependency graph so the procedure root requires `xaa-transitive-fixture@2.0.0` while the scoped plugin requires `^1.0.0`. A procedure-bound Node command confirms the root resolves `2.0.0` and the plugin resolves its nested `1.0.0`. npm 9.2.0 passes this alongside the existing peer, optional, platform/CPU filtering, recovery, lifecycle suppression, isolation, rollback, cleanup, and unchanged-system-runtime checks with network disabled: [report](../.acceptance/npm-duplicate-versions-retry-20261114/report.json). Ruff, formatting, strict Pyright, and diff checks pass. The first run encountered a Windows cache staging file lock; its evidence is preserved separately. Hosted Windows source run [43594360256](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43594360256) passed the full Python suite, static analysis, desktop checks, and recovery diagnostics; it does not provision WSL. The fixture remains evidence for one distro/npm version, not broad npm compatibility. Additional listed WSL distributions could not be started because their registered VHDX paths are missing. Model-mediated procedure testing remains deferred.

## Reject required npm dependencies for another operating system (2026-11-14)

Extended the isolated Ubuntu-24.04 WSL npm acceptance with a lock and integrity-checked tarball for a required package restricted to `win32`. npm correctly fails installation on Linux, and status confirms no environment manifest/tree was published. The existing optional `win32`/`arm64` cases continue to skip those packages and complete successfully. The same live run rechecks rollback, process recovery, cross-procedure isolation, exact cleanup, procedure binding and unchanged Node/npm hashes: [report](../.acceptance/npm-required-platform-20261114/report.json). Ruff, strict Pyright, formatting and diff checks pass. This is failure-path evidence for npm 9.2 on one Ubuntu WSL distribution. Hosted validation is pending; model-mediated procedure execution remains deferred.

## Verify npm archive expansion limits in isolated workers (2026-11-14)

Added a correctly integrity-hashed gzip tarball whose compressed size is below 1 MiB but whose regular-file members exceed the 256 MiB aggregate expanded-size limit. Both the source worker and frozen Windows AppContainer engine reject it before verified files are published. Together with the traversal, escaping symlink, and duplicate-member cases, the Windows worker acceptance passes all 12 source/frozen cases: [JUnit report](../.acceptance/npm-archive-expansion-validation-20261114/report.xml). Ruff and formatting checks pass locally. Hosted run [43582338641](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43582338641) passed the full source suite, including all 12 archive-expansion worker cases. Broader hostile archive combinations and the deferred model-mediated imported-procedure run remain open.

## Expand npm archive rejection coverage in both isolated workers (2026-11-14)

The Windows source/frozen worker matrix now checks traversal, absolute and backslash paths, symbolic and hard links, FIFO entries, duplicate members, missing package metadata, truncated gzip data, bad gzip checksums, and aggregate expansion above 256 MiB. All 26 source/frozen cases pass locally: [JUnit report](../.acceptance/npm-hostile-archive-matrix-20261114/report.xml). Hosted run [43583296231](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/43583296231) passed the complete source job, including the Python suite and these worker cases. Model-mediated procedure execution remains deferred until the end.
